Date: 2026-07-08 | Task: AtlasSPD sparse family source-acquisition audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added targeted sparse-family audit outputs under data/AtlasSPD_phase1/external_source_refresh_20260707/targeted_sparse_family_audit/ for NHR, enzyme, ion-channel, and kinase gaps.
Why: BindingDB source-specific labels exposed persistent family/target imbalance; the next data additions need to be predeclared by weak strata rather than selected ad hoc after model performance.
Impact: Provides a defensible source-by-target acquisition plan separating current Phase 1 feature gaps from available external bioactivity sources. Does not change model labels or training data.
Next: Prioritize add-on docking/scoring for targets with available external FDA-overlap bioactivity and sufficient Phase 1 relevance, then rerun label refresh and ML audit suite.

Date: 2026-07-08 | Task: AtlasSPD Phase 1 BindingDB source-specific label injection | Owner: Codex
Change: Added BindingDB-only exact ligand_base+target_uniprot labels to data/AtlasSPD_phase1/model_ready/AtlasSPD_phase1_model_ready.csv and wrote bindingdb_injection audit outputs.
Why: BindingDB was already represented in collapsed external four-state labels, but source-specific BindingDB labels were not visible as their own audit/model column.
Impact: Primary combined activity labels did not change because BindingDB-overlapping rows were already covered by SPD or collapsed external evidence. New bindingdb_ml_label enables BindingDB-only diagnostics without overriding SPD/four-state conflict policy.
Next: Use bindingdb_ml_label for source-specific diagnostics; run AR/ESR1 add-on target-ligand pairs before expecting AR/ESR1 BindingDB gains in the Phase 1 feature matrix.

Date: 2026-07-07 | Task: AtlasSPD Phase 1 AR/ESR1 BindingDB add-on and label-threshold policy | Owner: Codex
Change: Added AR/ESR1 BindingDB overlap/add-on manifests under data/AtlasSPD_phase1/external_source_refresh_20260707/ and documented spd_censor_aware_v2 thresholds in label_threshold_policy.json.
Why: AR/ESR1 BindingDB FDA ligands were not present as current AR/ESR1 target-pair feature rows, so labels cannot be safely joined until those target-ligand pairs are docked/scored. The SPD/BindingDB threshold choices also needed explicit provenance and sensitivity guidance.
Impact: Phase 1 model-ready labels remain leakage-safe; AR/ESR1 gains require a targeted add-on docking/feature pass from ar_esr1_bindingdb_phase1_addon_runlist.csv. Thresholds are now documented as operational label policy rather than implicit constants.
Next: Run the AR/ESR1 add-on pairs through Atlas when expanding Phase 1, then refresh SPD/external labels and rerun the ML audit suite.

Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: New user installation/onboarding QoL completion | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas setup-report`, `atlas new-run`, `atlas init --write-example-inputs`, installer success-panel output, `atlas doctor` fix guidance, and `docs/quickstart_new_users.md`; documented non-bundled proprietary/BYOL tools as optional registration rather than public-install failures.
Why: New wetlab users need one install command, clear readiness status, safe dry-run validation for first docking inputs, and actionable fixes without manually reverse-engineering `config.txt`.
Impact: The default install path is now `bash tools/installers/install_atlas_publication_stack.sh`; public/demo/report workflows can be validated without proprietary tools, while full receptor prep/docking still requires locally installed BYOL tools to be registered. `atlas status` remains a run-output summary rather than a live dashboard.
Next: Add protected-test coverage for the new dispatcher commands when test edits are allowed, and expand `atlas status` into live Slurm/progress/ETA reporting if that status feature should become a full operations dashboard.

Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: QoL command surface and auto-config | Owner: Codex
Change: Added task-oriented `atlas` subcommands for `init`, `demo`, `status`, `report`, `targets`, and explicit `run`; wired the public smoke check to generate a no-docking demo report; expanded install-time config auto-detection through `tools/installers/configure_local_tools.py`; made `bash tools/installers/install_atlas_publication_stack.sh` the one-command fresh install path; and documented the new commands in README/API/CLI/tooling docs.
Why: Wetlab users needed a simpler path from setup to target selection to report inspection without learning the whole docking pipeline or manually filling every local tool path up front.
Impact: Existing direct pipeline invocations remain compatible through `main.py`, while fresh installs can create/update `config.txt` automatically, detect open/legal tools from the newly active environment, and generate `data/atlas_demo/report.html` without external docking tools. Auto-detection is best-effort and non-recursive; licensed/proprietary tools still require local installation or explicit BYOL archive/root flags.
Next: Run the full smoke and targeted report/browser checks in a prepared `docking-env`, then consider adding dedicated regression tests around the new command dispatcher once the protected test policy allows it.

Date: 2026-02-16 | Task: PDB Query Robust Ranking/CLI Upgrade | Owner: Agent
Change: Reworked `pdb_query.py` to add cached/sessioned HTTP access with retry/backoff and rate limiting, UniProt-aware candidate discovery, richer ligand filtering and ranking explanations, deterministic selection, forced-ID validation, and a CSV-producing CLI; added `chemdb/tests/test_pdb_query.py` for cache/retry/filter/forced/ranking regression coverage.
Why: The docking panel workflow needed reproducible and publishable target selection with lower API churn, better failure tolerance, and auditable ranking/output signals.
Impact: `pdb_query.py` now exposes additional optional arguments and new helper APIs while preserving legacy function names (`_fetch_entry`, `_get_resolution`, `search_rcsb_by_gene`, `get_pdbs_for_gene`); CLI output now includes required ranking/ligand fields plus `valid_entry`.
Next: Exercise the new CLI on the intended gene/uniprot/forced inputs and confirm the chosen cache path and ligand list files align with your production run layout.
Date: 2026-02-16 | Task: PDB Query Live API Payload Hotfix | Owner: Agent
Change: Patched RCSB search payload operators/attributes (`gene exact_match`, taxonomy via `taxonomy_lineage.name`/`taxonomy_lineage.id`) and fixed nonpolymer parsing to use `rcsb_entry_container_identifiers.non_polymer_entity_ids` plus `nonpolymer_comp_id` extraction from `nonpolymer_entity` payloads.
Why: Live API returned HTTP 400 for prior operators/attributes and post-filtering dropped all candidates due schema drift in nonpolymer fields.
Impact: Query execution now returns ranked ligand-bearing hits at scale (verified run produced 527 rows / 527 unique PDBs).
Next: If broader gene-symbol matching is desired beyond strict exact symbol match, add an optional relaxed mode with explicit guardrails and test coverage.

Date: 2026-02-17 | Task: Distributed Combo-Chunk Throughput Mode | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `-bench-small` run profile (test_library_20 + Vina/SCORCH) and introduced distributed combo-chunk planning/claiming in `main.py` with atomic chunk claim/result helpers in `src/cli/distributed_context.py`; added ligand subset hook (`_CHUNK_LIGAND_KEYS`) in `src/docking/docking_ligands.py`.
Why: Per-PDB array sharding caused node imbalance once work shifted to per-variant/per-pH combos; chunk-level assignment with steal support is needed to spread backlog and reduce idle node time.
Impact: Distributed runs can opt into combo-chunk assignment without changing bench2 behavior; chunk plans and claim/result markers are written under `manifests/<run_id>/distributed/`; non-control ligand admission can be constrained per claimed chunk.
Next: Run a full multi-node bench-small baseline/candidate comparison, then validate bench2 acceptance remains unchanged before enabling combo-chunk mode broadly.

Date: 2026-02-17 | Task: Auto-Enable Combo Chunking In Distributed Runs | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` so multi-task distributed runs default to combo-chunk mode automatically; added `ATLAS_DISABLE_COMBO_CHUNKS` opt-out for legacy per-PDB sharding; removed the `target_ligands > 0` split gate so large distributed runs chunk by default.
Why: User requested minimal runtime flags/config while ensuring chunk splitting is active automatically on Slurm array runs.
Impact: `run_spr.sh`-based array jobs now use combo-chunk planning/claiming by default when `SLURM_ARRAY_TASK_COUNT > 1`; operators can still force old behavior with one env var.
Next: Run a multi-node throughput comparison (baseline legacy vs default auto-chunk) and confirm no ligand-skips via throughput integrity.

Date: 2026-02-17 | Task: Distributed Finalization + Run Efficiency Observability | Owner: Agent
Change: Patched distributed combo-chunk worker loop to avoid timeout exit while unresolved chunks remain, added leader-side fallback manifest finalization after marker barrier, wired per-combo SCORCH postprocessing status/timing updates into `run_manifest`, and added always-on `run_efficiency.json` emission (including optional `sacct` CPU-efficiency fields) from both `main.py` and `tools/finalize_distributed_run.py`; added mypy config to suppress missing-import noise for this mixed-layout repo.
Why: Array workers could leave partial completion when chunk/result state stalled, distributed runs could remain `status: running` if external finalizer was skipped, SCORCH wall-share was missing from manifest-level observability, and mypy checks were blocked by repo import-layout/module-resolution noise.
Impact: Distributed runs are harder to exit early with unresolved chunk work, leader tasks now best-effort finalize manifest state even without an external finalizer, SCORCH wall-time/share surfaces in run manifest summaries and run-level efficiency artifacts, and `mypy --follow-imports skip main.py postrun_hooks.py run_manifest.py tools/finalize_distributed_run.py` now passes.
Next: Re-run distributed bench workload to confirm marker-barrier fallback behavior under claim-loss scenarios and compare new run-efficiency metrics against util-bench outputs for consistency.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Massinstall PDB Query Category-Config Wiring | Owner: Agent
Change: Extended `massinstall.py` so `--pdb_query` resolves query/install defaults from `config.txt` (genes file, category limits, UniProt map, forced CSV, ligand lists, ranking/query knobs, cache/retry/rate options, output paths, install caps), added category-limit parsing for JSON or `KEY=VALUE` pairs, made `--dry-run` explicit via install-mode mutual exclusion, and documented new keys in `config.full.example.txt` and `docs/config.txt.md`.
Why: User requested config-driven control of per-category PDB selection and one-command `massinstall --pdb_query` integration without needing to pass all knobs manually.
Impact: Operators can now manage category/type quotas and query/install behavior entirely from config while preserving CLI overrides; default `--pdb_query` behavior remains safe (no download unless `--install` or interactive confirmation).
Next: Run a small dry-run smoke (`--pdb_query --max-install 1 --dry-run`) with the intended genes CSV to confirm category-limit settings and selected-output paths before full installation.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Runtime-Safety Hardening for Protein Prep Imports | Owner: Agent
Change: Replaced hidden legacy-global dependency usage with explicit imports in `src/protein_prep/clean_pipeline.py`, `src/protein_prep/holo_restore.py`, `src/protein_prep/ligand_extract.py`, and `src/protein_prep/waters.py`; removed legacy hydration calls from hot paths; retained a no-op `_hydrate_legacy_globals` shim in `holo_restore` for compatibility with existing callers/tests.
Why: Runtime behavior depended on symbols injected from `automate_protein_prep.__dict__`, which made dependencies implicit, fragile, and hard to statically verify.
Impact: Target modules now resolve required symbols explicitly, Ruff checks pass for edited files, targeted regression tests and `main.py --test -fast` smoke run succeed; plain `mypy <file>` still hits the repo-level duplicate-module issue (`path_router.path_router` vs `src.path_router.path_router`), while `mypy --follow-imports skip` on touched files passes.
Next: Add a repository-level mypy path/module-name normalization strategy so plain per-file mypy checks pass without `--follow-imports skip`.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Distributed Bench-Small Library Attribution Backfill | Owner: Agent
Change: Patched `main.py` distributed combo-chunk execution to pass `chunk.library_name` into `_process_one(...)` and use it for `update_manifest_for_protein_start(...)`; added `tools/finalize_distributed_run.py` library backfill that reads `manifests/<run_id>/distributed/combo_chunks_*.json` and rewrites mismatched `proteins[*].library` entries in `run_manifest.yaml` before finalization/reconcile-only exit.
Why: Bench-small distributed runs were recording `fda_library` in manifest despite chunk plans and outputs using `test_library_20`, causing throughput-integrity expected-set resolution failures (`expected_source=observed_fallback`).
Impact: Future distributed bench-small runs should emit correct per-combo manifest library attribution at runtime; existing distributed runs can be corrected without rerunning docking by invoking finalizer reconcile-only once; strict throughput-integrity now resolves expected sets from library manifests instead of empty fallback where artifacts are complete.
Next: For any legacy distributed run, execute `python tools/finalize_distributed_run.py --run-id <RUN_ID> --reconcile-only` before throughput KPIs; then investigate missing combo outputs (for example absent `docked/<run>/<pdb>/<variant>/<ph>/..._docking_score_summary.csv`) separately from attribution.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Dynamic-Import Cleanup + Rescoring Namespace Normalization | Owner: Agent
Change: Removed legacy global-hydration helpers and dynamic global lookups from `src/protein_prep/ion_audit.py`, `src/protein_prep/strip_nsr.py`, and `src/protein_prep/chain_prune.py` by wiring explicit imports; normalized rescoring imports to canonical `src.post_docking...` paths in `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` and `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_cnn.py`; updated `postrun_hooks.py` to use canonical SCORCH/artifact module paths, retain subprocess SCORCH execution in queue mode, and preserve pytest interception compatibility via a test-only launcher prefix.
Why: Hidden runtime dependencies and mixed `post_docking`/`src.post_docking` import forms were creating brittle behavior, static-analysis noise, and a SCORCH hook regression in test flow.
Impact: Dynamic import risk is reduced in core protein-prep modules; SCORCH per-protein queue tests pass again; runtime smoke path (`python main.py --test -fast`) completes with SCORCH + artifact-retention post-processing succeeding under canonical `src.post_docking` invocation.
Next: If desired, do a dedicated repository-wide namespace unification pass (including `path_router` vs `src.path_router`) to eliminate remaining duplicate-module mypy friction.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Ligand Prep Dynamic Import Hardening | Owner: Agent
Change: Removed a mis-scoped/dead nested wrapper block from `src/prep_ligands/prep_ligands_bulk.py` (legacy layout/debug helper wrapper code that was incorrectly nested under `_merge_dir`), replaced `globals().get("fix_element_columns_in_file")` indirection with direct callable usage in `src/prep_ligands/prep_ligands.py`, and upgraded `src/prep_ligands/prep_ligands_common.py` `chemdb.chem_alias_db` fallback handling to structured warning + telemetry markers with explicit fallback status fields.
Why: The nested block could never execute as intended and represented high-risk hidden behavior; global-symbol indirection obscured failures; alias-db fallback lacked explicit operational visibility.
Impact: Ligand prep now has less dynamic-symbol fragility, clearer fallback observability, and safer deterministic control flow; targeted ligand prep tests and full smoke run pass.
Next: Add a small unit test that monkeypatches `importlib.import_module("chemdb.chem_alias_db")` failure and asserts the new telemetry log markers for regression protection.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Chunk-Aware Integrity Accounting + Distributed Chunk Retry Safety | Owner: Agent
Change: Added chunk-result read/retry semantics in `src/cli/distributed_context.py`; hardened distributed combo-chunk loop in `main.py` with bounded retry attempts, terminal-failure propagation, and per-chunk docking-output verification; updated `analysis/reporting/throughput_integrity.py` to resolve expected ligands from distributed chunk plans, enforce chunk-completion coverage in strict integrity, and emit chunk coverage counters in JSON/CSV output; added regression tests in `chemdb/tests/test_throughput_integrity.py` for distributed chunk plan success/failure accounting.
Why: Distributed runs could previously misattribute completeness and allow ambiguous/missing chunk-result states to pass without proving chunk-level ligand coverage.
Impact: Integrity reporting is now chunk-aware and stricter for distributed runs; combo chunks with missing/failed results are explicitly surfaced and fail strict checks; runtime chunk execution now retries transient chunk failures and marks terminal failures into run failure accounting.
Next: Use strict throughput integrity as a hard gate on distributed benchmark candidates and only compare throughput KPIs across runs where `chunk_coverage_pass=true` and `integrity_pass=true`.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Distributed Finalization Strict-Gate + Coverage Snapshot Durability | Owner: Agent
Change: Added durable per-combo coverage snapshots under `manifests/<run_id>/coverage/` from `main.py` (chunk expected/docking coverage at chunk completion, post coverage refresh after queued SCORCH drain) and updated `analysis/reporting/throughput_integrity.py` to consume snapshot expected/docking/post keysets when present; extended `tools/finalize_distributed_run.py` to run throughput integrity in strict mode for benchmark run IDs/argv and return non-zero on failure.
Why: Distributed runs with artifact retention and asynchronous post-processing could lose direct docking/post evidence at analysis time, and historical bench runs were being reconciled without a hard strict-throughput finalization gate.
Impact: Future distributed benchmark finalization now fails hard when ligand-level parity is not proven, while chunk-level coverage evidence survives retention timing; `chunk_coverage_pass` remains a planning/execution signal and no longer substitutes for ligand parity (`integrity_pass`).
Next: Re-run a fresh distributed bench-small pass with this patch to generate snapshot-backed evidence from start-to-finish, then compare KPIs only across runs where strict throughput integrity passes.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Distributed Chunk Planning File-Truth + Auto-Clamp | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` chunk planning to treat manifests as advisory and derive planning truth from actual ligand files with planner-side pH/microstates filtering aligned to docking pool rules; added target auto-clamp (`effective=min(configured,available)`) and explicit clamp telemetry; added clearer chunk verification failure details and early failure when no plannable ligands exist; added regression tests in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_planning.py`.
Why: Distributed runs were planning chunk keys from stale/over-broad ligand manifests (including microstates/pH variants filtered later by docking), causing `missing_chunk_scores` terminal failures despite valid execution.
Impact: Chunk plans now reflect real, plannable ligands and avoid over-expectation; bench-small distributed runs clamp to available ligand count automatically instead of planning nonexistent keys; failure diagnostics now include missing/expected/scored counts and samples for faster triage.
Next: Run a fresh distributed bench-small strict gate and confirm `planned_chunks_failed=0` with `integrity_pass=true` before scaling to multi-node production jobs.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Namespace + Dynamic Import Hardening (Postrun/LigPrep/ProteinPrep Facade) | Owner: Agent
Change: Replaced remaining ligand dynamic imports with explicit imports in `src/prep_ligands/prep_ligands_common.py`, `src/prep_ligands/prep_ligands_crystal.py`, and `src/prep_ligands/prep_ligands_bulk.py`; narrowed `chem_alias_db` fallback exception scope with existing telemetry preserved; removed broad lazy wrapper import calls across `automate_protein_prep.py` by binding `protein_prep` modules once and delegating directly; normalized rescoring namespace usage to canonical `post_docking...` in `postrun_hooks.py` and `src/post_docking/rescoring/*`; added explicit subprocess env PYTHONPATH injection (`<repo>/src`) in `postrun_hooks.py` so `python -m post_docking...` is stable in child processes.
Why: Mixed namespace/module resolution and per-call lazy imports were causing environment-dependent import failures and deferred runtime wiring risks.
Impact: `python main.py --test -fast` smoke run passes with SCORCH and artifact retention subprocess hooks succeeding under canonical `post_docking` module invocation; dynamic import surface in targeted ligand/protein-prep areas is substantially reduced; `mypy` still reports pre-existing repo-level issues (`types-PyYAML` missing and `path_router`/`src.path_router` duplicate-module layout).
Next: Run a dedicated repo-wide package-layout normalization pass for `path_router`/`src.path_router` (and install `types-PyYAML`) to unblock plain `mypy <file>` checks without special flags.

Date: 2026-02-18 | Task: Distributed Post-Coverage Parity Fix (Canonical CSV + Snapshot Fallback) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` `_resolve_combo_post_consensus_csv(...)` to always prefer canonical `consensus_reranked_scorch.csv` when present; kept prefixed `*_consensus_reranked_scorch.csv` as stream-specific fallback only when canonical is absent. Updated `analysis/reporting/throughput_integrity.py` to prefer `master_rows.csv` post-scored evidence over stale/undercounted coverage snapshots when snapshot post sets are incomplete; added regression tests in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_planning.py` and `chemdb/tests/test_throughput_integrity.py`.
Why: Distributed strict gate failed with `missing_post_unexplained` because coverage snapshots were refreshed from a prefixed decoy-only post CSV (`test_library_20_consensus_reranked_scorch.csv`) even when canonical merged post CSV contained actives and decoys.
Impact: Strict integrity now aligns with actual post outputs (no false missing actives), coverage snapshots refresh to canonical post sets where available, and fresh distributed bench-small run `distbench_fresh_20260218_parityfix` reports `integrity_pass=true`, `expected_ligands=220`, `post_scored_ligands=220`, and `planned_chunks_failed=0`.
Next: If desired, add explicit per-stream canonical aliases (`fda_consensus_reranked_scorch.csv` / `<decoy_token>_consensus_reranked_scorch.csv`) to make stream semantics first-class while keeping unprefixed canonical for downstream consumers.

Date: 2026-02-19 | Task: Combo-Scoped Artifact Retention for Distributed SCORCH Parity | Owner: Agent
Change: Added combo filters (`--variant`, `--ph`) to `src/post_docking/artifact_retention.py`, threaded variant/ph through `postrun_hooks.py` retention invocation, and switched `main.py` retention queueing/execution from PDB scope to combo scope (`pdb_id+variant+ph`) including queued-SCORCH drain path.
Why: PDB-scoped retention could delete sibling combo stage artifacts early in distributed runs (same PDB, different pH), causing SCORCH no-op success (`returncode=0` with `no_pdbqt/no_rows`) and missing post outputs despite successful docking.
Impact: Retention now targets only the completed combo, preventing cross-combo pruning races while preserving existing retention mode behavior; added regression tests in `chemdb/tests/test_artifact_retention_combo_scope.py` for command scope and CLI filter guardrails.
Next: Add an explicit strict failure path in SCORCH when combo discovery/filter resolves but aggregate output is empty, so no-op success cannot mask missing post outputs.

Date: 2026-02-21 | Task: SCORCH Selection Heuristic Rollback to post_throughput_again Profile | Owner: Agent
Change: Reverted only SCORCH chunk/admission heuristics in `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` to prior behavior by restoring constants (`SCORCH_CHUNK_MIN=24`, `SCORCH_TAIL_SPLIT_TRIGGER=96`, `SCORCH_RUNTIME_RECHUNK_STALE_SEC=3.0`, `SCORCH_HEAVY_CHUNK_CAP=96`) and rolling back `_adaptive_chunk_size`, `_elastic_scorch_threads`, `_split_task_for_rechunk`, and runtime rechunk trigger thresholds (`free_cores>0`, `len(running)<=jobs//2`).
Why: Recent aggressive rechunk/thread tuning coincided with divergence from the known-good SCORCH selection profile in `post_throughput_again`; user requested restoring prior selection dynamics without changing the broader scheduler/distributed stack.
Impact: SCORCH launch/chunking policy is back to the earlier, less aggressive profile while preserving all other recent architecture changes; targeted SCORCH and CSV tests pass after rollback.
Next: Run an A/B benchmark (`post_throughput_again` style vs current head) and compare DUD post-rescore KPIs plus throughput-integrity to confirm expected selection/performance parity.

Date: 2026-02-21 | Task: Distributed Combo-Chunk Reliability Fixes (Relocated + Multi-Node) | Owner: Agent
Change: Hardened config-run reset race handling by disabling `RESET_CONFIGS` for distributed non-resume runs in `main.py` and tolerating ENOENT during `init_config_run_dir(...)` cleanup in `input_and_export_functions.py`; added run-scoped output root normalization in `main.py` so chunk verification/coverage refresh resolves `DOCKED_DIR` and `POST_DOCKED_DIR` correctly when roots are already run-scoped (relocated mode); added effective chunk-key propagation from `src/docking/docking_ligands.py` and used it for chunk verification in `main.py`; made Vina chunk CSV writes merge-safe with lock+atomic merge in `src/docking/docking_vina.py`; guarded `src/docking/pose_validation.py` against empty receptor coordinate sets.
Why: Multi-node Slurm array chunks were failing nondeterministically from config-dir reset races, cross-chunk summary CSV overwrite, and relocated run-root path double-append; pose validation also crashed on empty receptor atom arrays.
Impact: Distributed chunk jobs now avoid startup reset races, preserve chunk score rows across concurrent writers, verify against effective (post-filter) ligand keys, resolve relocated/run-scoped paths correctly, and return deterministic invalid-pose results instead of crashing on empty receptors.
Next: Run a fresh multi-node relocated distributed benchmark and confirm zero `chunk_output_verification_failed` events plus stable throughput-integrity parity against expected chunk coverage.

Date: 2026-02-21 | Task: BOJG Prep Race Mitigation via Dispatcher Serialization + Distributed Combo Prep Ownership | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` to prevent concurrent same-PDB combo dispatch in both global-scheduler and multi-PDB single-ligand thread-pool paths, and added distributed combo prep ownership gating in combo-chunk mode (owner prepares once; followers wait/reuse with `FORCE_REPROCESS=False`); added combo prep state helpers in `src/cli/distributed_context.py` and regression tests in `chemdb/tests/test_combo_prep_owner_barrier.py`.
Why: BOJG pH-combo runs were racing shared prep artifacts, producing transient empty intermediates and prep crashes (`Empty file` / `list index out of range`) under parallel combo execution.
Impact: Same-PDB prep no longer runs concurrently within a process, distributed workers coordinate prep for each combo key, and non-owner chunks reuse prepared receptor artifacts instead of forcing duplicate prep.
Next: Run full chemdb suite and targeted BOJG Slurm-style distributed validation to confirm no prep regressions and no distributed throughput regressions.

Date: 2026-02-21 | Task: Full chemdb One-by-One Sweep Stabilization | Owner: Agent
Change: Executed the full `chemdb/tests/test_*.py` list one file at a time under `docking-env`, fixed each failing regression in-place (manifest write races, DeepCoy chunk merge/model fallback, pagination kwarg compatibility, fpocket loader fallback, pathway-report grouping, and multiple integration test fixture/expectation updates), and validated each fix by rerunning the failing file before resuming the sweep.
Why: Distributed/relocated and prep-race changes exposed latent compatibility gaps across integration-heavy chemdb tests, and several tests depended on unavailable local fixtures or pre-refactor module surfaces.
Impact: All chemdb files now complete in one-by-one mode in this workspace (with expected fixture-dependent skips), distributed combo prep barriers remain covered by new regression tests, and `main.py --test -fast --run-id final_smoke_20260221` smoke run exits successfully.
Next: If strict typing is required, schedule a dedicated mypy debt pass for `DeepCoy_duds/external_sources.py`, `DeepCoy_duds/generate_dud_library.py`, and `ml/featurize.py` (current mypy failures are pre-existing broader typing issues in those modules).

Date: 2026-02-21 | Task: Hybrid Same-PDB pH Unlock (Local Scheduler) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` local/non-distributed admission to allow same-PDB different-pH combos in parallel once receptor prep artifacts are ready; added scope-aware helper logic (`_local_combo_scope_key`, enhanced `_pick_next_combo_index`, `_refresh_local_prep_ready_scopes`) plus periodic refill polling with `wait(..., FIRST_COMPLETED)` in both `multi_pdb_single_ligand` and global-scheduler branches. Added regression coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_combo_prep_owner_barrier.py` for ready-vs-unready scope admission.
Why: Same-PDB combos were being fully serialized in non-distributed mode, which prevented BOJG pH combos from parallelizing even after prep safety could be preserved.
Impact: Distributed combo-chunk path remains unchanged; local mode now keeps prep race protection while restoring throughput by admitting additional pH combos for the same PDB after prep readiness is observed.
Next: Let `postcodex` complete, then summarize `analysis/summarize_run_errors.py postcodex --deep` and verify no new prep-failure signatures.

Date: 2026-02-22 | Task: Portable Report HTML Asset Fallback Hardening | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `analysis/reporting/heatmap_html.py` to resolve Clustergrammer assets from either a real directory (`report_assets/clustergrammer`) or a pointer file (`report_assets` -> external root), and hardened `_clustergrammer_asset_block(...)` to fall back to CDN asset URLs if inline/local asset resolution fails. Added pointer-file regression coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_report_html_portable.py`.
Why: Reports copied across machines could show “Clustergrammer not available” or “Heatmap unavailable” when local asset paths were missing or represented via a pointer file.
Impact: HTML report generation now remains interactive by default with deterministic fallback order (inline local -> local relative URLs -> CDN URLs), and pointer-based asset layouts are supported.
Next: If fully offline report portability is required across machines, ensure `report_assets` pointer target is copied as well, or package inline assets from a machine that has the local Clustergrammer bundle.

Date: 2026-02-22 | Task: Relocated Hook Root Normalization for DUD Eval + Run Report | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `postrun_hooks.py` root resolution so relative `DOCKED_DIR`/`POST_DOCKED_DIR` are resolved under `OVERALL_DIR` (or code root fallback) before invoking postrun DUD-eval/report/master-export hooks; added regression tests in `chemdb/tests/test_relocated_analysis_cli.py` for relative cfg roots and OVERALL_DIR-only env defaults.
Why: Relocated runs using relative cfg roots could resolve analysis paths against the code repo cwd instead of relocated root, causing report and dud-eval artifacts to miss run outputs.
Impact: Default runs remain unchanged, while relocated mode now consistently passes absolute relocated roots to analysis hooks even when cfg paths are relative or omitted.
Next: Run one real relocated-mode job with relative root cfg values and verify analysis artifacts land under relocated `analysis/dud_eval/<run_id>` and `data/<run_id>/report.html`.

Date: 2026-02-22 | Task: BOJG Prep Ownership Barrier Hardening (Stale-Artifact Safe) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` prep gating to be timestamp-aware and scope-aware: added `_scope_prep_key(...)` for distributed prep ownership keyed by `run_id+variant+pdb` (instead of per-`pH` combo key for ownership), added `min_mtime` support to `_combo_receptor_ready(...)`, and extended `_refresh_local_prep_ready_scopes(...)` with `scope_ready_after/default_ready_after` so local hybrid unlock only treats receptor artifacts as ready when they are fresh for the current scheduling window. Updated distributed combo-chunk wait/reuse logic to accept artifact reuse only when prep state is `preparing/ready` and artifact mtime is newer than state timestamp. Added regression tests in `chemdb/tests/test_combo_prep_owner_barrier.py` for mtime gating and ready-floor behavior.
Why: BOJG regressions reappeared because stale receptor artifacts could mark same-scope prep as ready before current-run ownership was established, re-enabling overlapping prep work and intermittent `Protein cleaning failed: Empty file` crashes.
Impact: Local and distributed paths now preserve hybrid throughput while preventing stale-artifact early unlock; distributed workers coordinate prep at PDB scope and only unlock waiters after owner-state/timestamp readiness, reducing prep races without full per-combo serialization.
Next: Run a relocated multi-node Slurm array job with at least one multi-pH target to confirm no BOJG-style prep failures and verify expected pH overlap only after prep readiness.

Date: 2026-02-22 | Task: Distributed Chunk Planner Manifest Fast-Path + Preflight Rebuild | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` distributed chunk planning to use manifest-first ligand inventory with configurable policy (`CHUNK_PLANNER_MANIFEST_MODE`: trust/sample/strict), added parallel filesystem fallback scanning (`CHUNK_PLANNER_SCAN_WORKERS`), and added distributed variant-scoped manifest preflight rebuild barrier with lock/state files under `manifests/<run_id>/distributed/manifest_preflight_<variant>.json`. Added CLI utility path `--rebuild-library-manifests-only` with optional `--manifest-roots`, plus run-time planner telemetry for source/timing decisions.
Why: Multi-node runs were spending large startup time in `distributed.chunk` because planner logic performed full recursive ligand scans on shared storage when per-library manifests were missing/stale.
Impact: Chunk planning now avoids full scans when manifests are usable, can recover missing manifests before planning in distributed mode, and supports explicit manifest repair runs without launching docking; relocated library roots remain supported because root resolution continues to use runtime-configured `compute_allowed_library_roots(...)`.
Next: Execute one distributed relocated benchmark with default planner mode (`trust`) and confirm reduced plan latency plus no `missing_chunk_scores` regressions; if needed, tune `ATLAS_CHUNK_PLANNER_MANIFEST_MODE=sample` for stricter safety checks.

Date: 2026-02-22 | Task: Manifest Rebuild UX Simplification (`-rebuild` + Autodiscovery) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` CLI rebuild entrypoint from `--rebuild-library-manifests-only` to `-rebuild`/`--rebuild`, removed `--manifest-roots` parsing/override path, and made rebuild roots always autodiscover via existing library root resolution (`compute_allowed_library_roots(...)`) so relocated layouts remain supported. Removed startup `cfg.setdefault(...)` injection for `CHUNK_PLANNER_*` keys while keeping planner/env fallback logic unchanged.
Why: User requested simpler operational UX with no manual manifest-path passing and no extra runtime config-line injection.
Impact: Operators now run `python main.py -rebuild` without path args; manifest roots are inferred from repo/relocated config context; planner tuning keys are no longer auto-populated into runtime config unless provided explicitly.
Next: Run one standard and one relocated `-rebuild` invocation to confirm root discovery matches expected library locations.

Date: 2026-02-22 | Task: Rebuild Root Override for Relocated Installs | Owner: Agent
Change: Extended `main.py` rebuild-only autodiscovery with optional `--rebuild-root <repo_path>` that remaps library root discovery to `<repo_path>/prepped_ligands` without requiring explicit manifest/library paths.
Why: User needed a direct way to target an alternate relocated `protein_automation` tree during `-rebuild` runs.
Impact: `python main.py -rebuild --rebuild-root /path/to/protein_automation` now rebuilds manifests for that relocated install while preserving default autodiscovery when the flag is omitted.
Next: Validate on a relocated clone to confirm discovered roots and rebuild counts match expected libraries.

Date: 2026-02-22 | Task: Rebuild Scope Decoupled from Test Mode | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` `-rebuild` root discovery to scan all direct library directories under `prepped_ligands` (or `<rebuild-root>/prepped_ligands`) instead of selecting roots via `TEST_MODE_ENABLE`/`TEST_LIBRARY_MAP`.
Why: Test-mode-coupled rebuild targeting was confusing and could miss libraries that should be repaired.
Impact: Rebuild behavior is now filesystem-driven and predictable; one `-rebuild` pass repairs all discovered library manifests regardless of docking mode tokens.
Next: Run `python main.py -rebuild --rebuild-root <relocated_repo>` on a production-like relocated tree and confirm discovered root count matches expected library directories.

Date: 2026-02-22 | Task: Retention Tail Latency Reduction + Count-Safety Guardrails | Owner: Agent
Change: Added batched deferred artifact-retention invocation path (`_maybe_run_artifact_retention_for_combos`) in `postrun_hooks.py` and switched `main.py` post-SCORCH finalization from per-combo serial retention calls to one batched call with explicit `[pipeline.phase]` start/done elapsed logs. Extended `src/post_docking/artifact_retention.py` with `--combo`/`--combo-file` scope filters, `--group-workers` bounded parallel group processing, worker thread-budget partitioning (`threads_per_group`), and progress logging. Added/updated regression tests to lock Vina stage counts, SCORCH stage rescoring counts, and artifact retention archived/deleted count behavior.
Why: Retention tail latency was dominated by serialized per-combo subprocess invocations and repeated root scans after combo processing completed, and throughput changes previously risked altering ligand/rescore/file-count invariants.
Impact: Default behavior remains compatible (`group_workers=1`), while configured parallelism reduces deferred retention wall-time for multi-combo runs; new tests explicitly guard stage-wise ligand counts and retention file-count invariants to prevent regressions.
Next: Validate representative run(s) with 4+ combos to measure deferred retention phase elapsed time against prior baseline and tune `ARTIFACT_RETENTION_GROUP_WORKERS` conservatively for shared-node pressure.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed Chunk Planner Throughput (Trust Fast-Path + Memoization) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` chunk-planner hot path to (1) cache manifest inventory payloads per root/signature/mode, (2) reuse resolved ligand sets per PDB across repeated pH combos within planning, and (3) add planner cache telemetry via `[distributed.chunk.plan-cache]`. Added a freshness-gated trust fast-path that only bypasses per-entry file `stat()` checks when `_manifest.json` carries matching `mtime` + `child_dir_mtimes`; otherwise the planner preserves existing validation/scanning behavior. Updated `src/docking/library_mode.py` to cache parsed `TEST_LIBRARY_MAP` for non-dict raw config values and cache resolved allowed-root lists per `(pdb,tokens,library settings)` key.
Why: Distributed chunk planning was spending significant wall time repeatedly reparsing library maps, recomputing root selection, and performing heavy manifest-entry filesystem checks in combo loops, producing long startup tails before docking work starts.
Impact: No new config lines were added and default planner behavior is preserved for stale/legacy manifests; rebuilt/fresh manifests now take the low-I/O trust path, reducing chunk-planner latency while keeping ligand/stage/rescore/retention invariants protected by existing regressions.
Next: Run a representative distributed DUD dry-run and compare `[distributed.chunk.plan-cache]` elapsed/cache metrics against prior runs to verify target speedup.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Manifest Rebuild Criteria Relaxation + Distributed CPU Node Telemetry | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/prep_ligands/library_index.py` strict manifest reuse criteria to validate referenced manifest paths (`exists` + file size > 0, traversal-safe) instead of requiring root/child-dir mtime equality; unreadable/missing/invalid manifest maps still force rebuild. Added guaranteed distributed per-node CPU telemetry log line in `main.py` (`[distributed.cpu.node]`) emitted on every distributed worker startup with task identity, hostname, effective CPU values, and raw SLURM CPU env fields. Added regressions in `chemdb/tests/test_library_index_manifest_rebuild_policy.py` and `chemdb/tests/test_main_cpu_policy.py`.
Why: Root mtime drift was causing repeated unnecessary manifest rebuilds even when manifest references were still valid, and existing CPU logs were insufficiently consistent across node logs for debugging uneven CPU allocations.
Impact: Manifest rebuild behavior is less strict on timestamps but still guarded by manifest structure and path validity; distributed runs now always expose per-node CPU telemetry in each worker log for allocation parity checks.
Next: Validate one real Slurm array run and confirm each node log contains one `[distributed.cpu.node]` line with expected task and CPU fields.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed Preflight Scope Reduction + Leader Parallel Health Checks | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` distributed manifest preflight to run a cheap manifest-health pass (`missing/corrupt/invalid map`) across collected roots with leader-only bounded parallelism, periodic progress logging, and targeted rebuild of only broken roots. Added non-owner wait progress logs (`[distributed.chunk.manifest-preflight.wait]`) during preflight gate polling. Added regression coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_manifest_preflight.py` for scoped rebuild selection and wait-log emission.
Why: Multi-node runs appeared to hang during preflight because leader was doing expensive broad validation/rebuild work while other tasks waited silently for manifest-ready state.
Impact: Preflight now avoids full variant-wide rebuild/validation sweeps and reports active progress/wait state, reducing startup tail and making blocking behavior observable without changing chunk/docking/rescore count semantics.
Next: Validate on a representative Slurm array run and confirm per-node logs show wait/progress lines plus reduced time between preflight start and chunk planning.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed Chunk Failure De-Amplification + Low-Cost Stress Harness | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` chunk verification and coverage handling to reduce distributed hard-fails from transient artifacts: `_verify_chunk_combo_outputs` now degrades (`degraded_*`) for missing/partial score-summary evidence instead of forcing immediate protein failure, `_load_scored_ligand_keys_from_summary` now safely handles parse/transient read errors with warning logs, and `_update_combo_coverage_snapshot` now writes via unique per-writer temp files for race-safe atomic replace. Updated `src/docking/docking_stage_runner.py` retry path to catch `FileNotFoundError` during retry pose validation and continue ligand-level retry handling instead of aborting the whole chunk. Updated `analysis/reporting/throughput_integrity.py` to honor `ATLAS_DISABLE_COMBO_CHUNKS=1` by disabling chunk-plan strict checks during parity analysis runs. Added regressions in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_regressions.py` and `chemdb/tests/test_throughput_integrity.py`; added `tools/stress_distributed_chunk_verification.py` for high-scale synthetic chunk/coverage stress without docking compute.
Why: Distributed failures showed amplification where one transient ligand/chunk artifact (missing retry pose, partial CSV, or coverage tmp-file race) caused protein-level terminal failures; parity runs also needed a clean chunk-disable mode for stability comparisons.
Impact: Production distributed runs now continue forward on transient chunk verification gaps while preserving explicit degraded logging/coverage accounting; throughput-integrity can intentionally ignore chunk-plan strictness when combo chunks are disabled for parity; new stress harness enables cheap preflight of race/fault behavior without spending docking credits.
Next: Run `tools/stress_distributed_chunk_verification.py` at target scale (for example `--combos 200 --chunks-per-combo 16 --workers 64`) and validate degraded/fatal ratios before re-enabling combo chunks on large production arrays.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed Chunk Throughput Scaling (Multi-Worker Consumers) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated distributed combo-chunk execution in `main.py` to run multiple local chunk consumers per task instead of a single serial consumer loop. Added default aggressive local worker derivation from allocated task CPU (`_distributed_chunk_local_workers`) and per-worker CPU budgeting (`_distributed_chunk_cpu_per_worker`), wired per-worker `CPU` into `_process_one` execution, and added task telemetry log `[distributed.chunk.local-workers]`. Updated chunk splitting (`_split_ligands_into_chunks`) to include local workers in oversharding math so high-core runs generate enough chunks to keep workers busy. Added regressions in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_regressions.py` for local worker CPU math and overshard scaling behavior.
Why: High-core multi-node runs showed weak speedup because each task had only one chunk consumer, causing under-utilization and long idle/claim tails despite large CPU allocations.
Impact: Default distributed behavior is faster and more parallel without adding new config lines; chunk claim/result semantics, retries, and failure classification remain compatible, while per-chunk execution uses bounded worker CPU budgets to reduce oversubscription risk.
Next: Run an A/B distributed Bench2 comparison on the same 3-node allocation and confirm improved chunks/minute, reduced waiting-for-chunks logs, and unchanged Vina/SCORCH parity metrics.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: SCORCH Elastic Jobs/Threads Allocation + EX4M-Oriented Validation Path | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `postrun_hooks.py` and `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` to remove hard-pinned `threads=1` behavior and use an elastic parallel allocator with aggressive/conservative profiles (env: `ATLAS_SCORCH_PARALLEL_PROFILE`). Added `_allocate_scorch_parallelism` in rescoring runtime, wired hook-side per-PDB subprocess invocation to compute `jobs/threads` from CPU/free-core/backlog/pressure hints, and extended invoke logs to include allocation telemetry (`profile`, `util_target`, `free_cores`, `backlog`, `scheduler_q`). Updated runtime preflight in rescoring CLI to use allocator-based defaults instead of forcing threads to 1. Added allocator unit coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_parallelism_allocator.py` and updated portability regression in `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_env_portability.py` to assert elastic (non-constant) thread/job behavior under CPU budget constraints.
Why: Distributed runs showed severe SCORCH underutilization on high-core nodes because both hook and CLI paths forced 1-thread workers, leaving throughput on the table and masking available CPU elasticity.
Impact: SCORCH now adapts jobs/threads dynamically while preserving CPU safety (`jobs * threads <= utilization_budget`) and maintaining stage/rescore count parity; logs now make allocator decisions auditable per invocation.
Next: Run distributed EX4M DUD-mode A/B on target nodes and compare SCORCH wall time and CPU occupancy between `ATLAS_SCORCH_PARALLEL_PROFILE=aggressive` and `conservative` while confirming unchanged throughput-integrity counts.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Production Hardening for Transient Pose-File Races + SCORCH Idempotent Skip Logging | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/docking/fallback_recenter.py` to create unique per-attempt temporary pose files via `NamedTemporaryFile(..., delete=False)` instead of deterministic `*.m{idx}.tmp.pdbqt` names, eliminating cross-thread tmp-path collisions. Updated `src/docking/docking_stage_runner.py` `_per_ligand_postprocess` to treat per-ligand `FileNotFoundError` in RMSD filtering or first-pose validation as non-fatal (`pose_file_missing_transient`) and continue chunk execution. Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` discovery empty-filter logging to distinguish idempotent all-done filtering from anomalous empty discovery, emitting `INFO` reason `no_combos_after_filters_idempotent` for expected done-sentinel cases. Added regressions in `chemdb/tests/test_docking_pose_file_transients.py` and `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_no_combos_logging.py`.
Why: Distributed/high-parallel runs could fail entire chunks from transient tmp pose file races, and SCORCH produced misleading warnings when zero combos remained only because idempotency sentinels had already marked all work done.
Impact: Pipeline resilience is improved for transient per-ligand file races without altering stage-selection or rescore count semantics; SCORCH log stream is clearer for production operations by reducing false warning noise on expected idempotent skips.
Next: Validate on a representative distributed run with repeated-stage pressure and confirm chunk failures are no longer triggered by transient tmp pose misses while SCORCH logs show `no_combos_after_filters_idempotent` in all-done paths.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed SCORCH Tail Efficiency + Consensus Full-Ligand Coverage Guard | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py`, `postrun_hooks.py`, and `src/cli/distributed_context.py` to reduce distributed tail stalls and improve SCORCH CPU utilization: per-combo SCORCH submissions are deferred in combo-chunk mode to a deterministic owner task after chunk completion, queue service now deduplicates inflight identical SCORCH scopes, SCORCH invocation now uses node-level CPU budget hints (instead of per-chunk worker CPU) and logs both `total_cpu` and `cfg_cpu`, chunk claim leases are heartbeated via new `renew_chunk_claim(...)`, and idle wait logs now include unresolved chunk counts. Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` to downgrade filtered no-combo discovery from warning to info when scoped filters are used. Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescore_reranker.py` to union shard-style `*_consensus_docking_scores.csv` files with the primary consensus file before reranking so `consensus_reranked_scorch.csv` preserves full ligand coverage even when consensus is emitted in per-chunk shards.
Why: Tail latency was inflated by duplicate per-chunk SCORCH submissions and under-allocated SCORCH CPU budgets (`cfg CPU` from chunk worker context). Separate reports showed `consensus_reranked_scorch.csv` could miss ligands when consensus inputs were sharded.
Impact: Distributed runs should show fewer redundant SCORCH subprocess calls, better CPU saturation during rescoring, faster dead-worker chunk reclaim without stealing live claims, clearer no-combo logs, and full-ligand consensus rerank outputs for sharded consensus cases.
Next: Run a 4-node distributed Bench2/EX4M A/B and compare (1) SCORCH invoke counts and `jobs*threads` utilization telemetry per node, (2) chunk idle tail warnings/unresolved counts, and (3) per-combo `consensus_reranked_scorch.csv` row counts against expected ligand totals.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Tail CPU Utilization Patch (Aggressive) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` allocator to derive effective SCORCH work demand from ligand cardinality (not only queue backlog), and added aggressive tail floors so low-backlog/high-core invocations do not collapse to tiny `jobs*threads` allocations. Updated `postrun_hooks.py` to pass/consume `allowed_count_hint` through queue submissions and to log `backlog`, `backlog_effective`, and `allowed_count` for each SCORCH invoke. Updated `main.py` combo-chunk flow to provide per-scope ligand-count hints, precompute combo chunk/ligand totals, queue owner-only deferred SCORCH as soon as combo chunks become terminal, and keep end-of-variant deferred submission as a deduped fallback; also increased chunk oversharding and reduced idle backoff to improve tail claim turnover.
Why: Distributed tails were leaving many cores idle because SCORCH parallelism was over-capped by small backlog hints and combo-tail rescoring waited until variant-end barriers instead of dispatching at combo completion.
Impact: Tail phases should show higher SCORCH occupancy (`jobs*threads`) and earlier rescoring overlap with chunk completion while preserving chunk ownership semantics and throughput parity checks.
Next: Validate on the pending 4-node run by comparing per-node `scorch-rescore.invoke` occupancy telemetry and unresolved chunk tail duration against a pre-patch run.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Phase A Completion-Marker Race Hardening + Owner Aggregation | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `src/docking/completion_markers.py` with unique-temp atomic JSON writes, chunk-marker pathing (`completion_<engine>__chunk_<id>.json`), and owner-side aggregation helpers that build canonical `completion_<engine>.json` from chunk markers. Updated `src/docking/docking_utils.py` `run_completion_audit(...)` to emit chunk-scoped markers when `_CHUNK_ID` is present, include `expected_ligands` in marker payload, and avoid shared `.part` temp collisions. Updated `main.py` distributed combo-chunk owner finalization to aggregate chunk completion markers into canonical completion files, and reduced chunk control-loop metadata pressure by replacing hot-loop full scans with timed state reconciliation cache. Added regression coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_completion_chunk_marker_aggregation.py`.
Why: Distributed chunk workers can write completion markers concurrently into the same stage directory; shared marker filenames and non-unique temp names can overwrite or corrupt completion state, causing apparent stage-count mismatches despite correct coverage snapshots.
Impact: Completion markers are now race-resistant in chunk mode, canonical completion files are generated deterministically by combo owner after chunk convergence, and worker idle loops perform fewer shared-filesystem scans.
Next: Run distributed Bench2/EX4M validation and confirm BNJS stage completion counts match coverage snapshots while chunk tail logs show lower control-plane churn.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Dynamic Rechunk/Hedging + Phase C SCORCH Mode Hardening | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `src/cli/chunk_tail_optimizer.py` and wired it in `main.py` to apply adaptive pre-execution rechunking/rebalancing for distributed combo chunks and optional hedging-oriented candidate ordering via env gates (`ATLAS_DYNAMIC_RECHUNK`, `ATLAS_CHUNK_HEDGING`). Updated `postrun_hooks.py` with Phase C execution-mode support (`ATLAS_SCORCH_PERSISTENT_WORKER`) so distributed runs default to in-process SCORCH invocation, added utilization telemetry (`desired_util_cores`, `allocated_util_cores`, `worker_busy_ratio`), and propagated mode through queue/service logs. Added regression coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_regressions.py` and `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_env_portability.py` for heavy-chunk splitting, hedging candidate ordering, and in-process SCORCH behavior.
Why: Tail performance remained limited by static chunk shapes and per-scope subprocess overhead; production distributed runs needed adaptive scheduling and lower rescore dispatch overhead without changing docking/scoring semantics.
Impact: Distributed chunk plans can now split/rebalance oversized tails before execution, optional hedging candidate order is available behind env control, and SCORCH can run in-process on distributed nodes to reduce process churn while exposing clearer utilization diagnostics.
Next: Complete long-running distributed Bench2 simulation verification for `sim_bench2_phasec` and compare tail metrics (`[distributed.chunk.rechunk]`, `[distributed.chunk.hedging]`, `[scorch.utilization]`) against a pre-patch baseline before large allocation submissions.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed Chunk Prep Reuse for Control-Redock Stall Reduction | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/docking/docking.py` to support distributed prep reuse payloads (`_COMBO_PREP_*` keys), allowing chunk workers to skip repeated control-redock and control-multi docking when prep geometry/control metadata is already available. Updated `main.py` chunk-worker prep flow to (1) wait for prep-ready state instead of treating plain receptor readiness as sufficient reuse, (2) carry owner-generated prep metadata into combo prep state payload (`center_by_ph`, `box_by_ph`, sources, control stems/lookup), and (3) propagate prep metadata from per-combo cfg back to chunk-owner cfg so waiters can consume it. Added regression test `chemdb/tests/test_docking_control_reuse.py`.
Why: Distributed no-docking runs could appear hung because each chunk repeated fixed-cost control-redock setup; waiters were unblocked before reusable prep metadata existed, causing redundant control-redock across tasks.
Impact: Non-owner chunk workers now block until reusable prep state exists and can skip redundant control-redock/control-multi setup, reducing duplicated fixed-cost work and improving visible progress during long distributed tails.
Next: Let `sim_bench2_reusepost` progress past first prep-ready transition and confirm waiters log `reused=true` plus `distributed_prep_reuse` skip lines; then compare per-task control-redock log counts against pre-fix `sim_bench2_reusecheck`.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Manifest Mutation Locking for Control-Redock Pocket Method Stability | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `run_manifest.py` to serialize non-distributed load/mutate/write operations for `update_manifest_for_druggability_and_engine_plan(...)`, `update_manifest_for_pocket_detection(...)`, `update_manifest_for_docking_overall(...)`, `update_manifest_for_docking_stage(...)`, and `update_manifest_for_postprocessing(...)` using `_exclusive_file_lock(_manifest_lock_path(...))`; removed duplicate `_manifest_lock_path(...)` definition.
Why: `test_control_redock_select_center` failure showed `pocket_detection.details.method` was logged as `control` then later missing in manifest, indicating concurrent manifest writes were clobbering stage details.
Impact: Manifest stage details now persist across concurrent stage updates; control-center provenance in manifest is stable through full pipeline execution with no interface change.
Next: If additional manifest races appear under heavier parallel runs, migrate remaining non-distributed mutators (`scheduled_proteins`, run-config/hash writers) to the same lock discipline for uniform consistency.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Ligand-Scoped Failure Hardening (Docking + SCORCH) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/docking/docking_stage_runner.py` to convert unexpected per-ligand postprocess/retry exceptions into invalid-ligand outcomes (`postprocess_exception`, `retry_validation_exception`) and demote RUN_DIR config guard violations to invalid-ligand skips (`config_guard_violation`) instead of raising chunk-fatal exceptions. Added soft-failure counters (`_SOFTFAIL_LIGAND_COUNT`, `_SOFTFAIL_LIGAND_REASONS`) on cfg. Updated `main.py` distributed chunk handling to propagate soft-failure metadata from combo execution, emit degraded-completed chunk payloads when soft-failure counts are present, and treat soft-ligand exception signatures as completed-degraded rather than failed/terminal_failed. Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` materialization path to partial-success semantics via `MaterializeResult` (skip bad ligands, continue when any remain) and annotation path to degraded non-fatal behavior via `AnnotateResult` when scored CSV rows exist.
Why: Production distributed runs can be derailed by one/few bad ligands due to exception escalation and all-or-nothing SCORCH materialize/annotate behavior.
Impact: Single/few bad ligands are isolated as degraded ligand-level outcomes rather than chunk/protein hard failures; SCORCH preserves usable partial outputs while surfacing degraded logs/metadata. Existing scoring/docking science path is unchanged for valid ligands.
Next: Run distributed bench2 with docking enabled (no `--no-docking`) and inspect `[distributed.chunk.degraded]`, `[ligand.softfail]`, and `[scorch-rescore] action=materialize status=degraded` frequency to confirm lower terminal chunk failures without parity drift.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed Chunk Claim Ordering for Prep-Owner Concurrency | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` distributed chunk candidate ordering to build owner-aware, scope-interleaved claim queues: `preferred_ids` now prioritize chunks whose combo prep owner is the local task and then round-robin across combo scopes; `steal_ids` now also use scope interleaving instead of pure weight-sorted clustering.
Why: In weighted-only ordering, workers could claim many chunks from the same combo/PDB first, then block behind a single combo prep owner, creating long idle windows and apparent hangs despite available chunks.
Impact: Early and mid-run worker occupancy improves by reducing prep-owner contention and combo clustering; no docking/scoring semantics or chunk contents are changed.
Next: Validate on distributed Bench2 run with `-bench2 -fast` and compare early-phase worker utilization/idle warnings (`[distributed.combo-chunk] waiting_for_chunks_s`) before enabling for large allocation runs.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed Startup Diversification + pH-Scoped Prep State | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` chunk candidate ordering to rotate combo-scope round-robin by `task_id`, preventing all tasks from claiming the same first protein scope at startup. Updated prep-state scoping to include `ph_tag` in `_scope_prep_key(...)` and its call site so prep reuse state is isolated per `(run, variant, pdb, ph)` rather than shared across all pH variants of a protein.
Why: Even with scope interleaving, identical scope ordering across tasks still caused startup clustering on the same protein; additionally, shared prep scope across pH values caused cross-pH reuse of incomplete center/box metadata and failures like `ValueError: Center/box unavailable for requested ph_label` (observed for BOJG pH7_2).
Impact: Initial chunk claims are now distributed across proteins/pH scopes, reducing prep-owner contention; pH-specific prep metadata cannot leak across pH combinations, eliminating the observed BOJG center/box missing regression.
Next: Continue capped (`32` cores) distributed Bench2 simulation and confirm no recurrence of BOJG pH center/box failures while chunk-result completion count rises steadily.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: SCORCH Queue Drain Wait Observability | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `postrun_hooks.py` `ScorchMixedQueueService.close_and_wait()` to use timed `wait(..., timeout=15s, return_when=FIRST_COMPLETED)` loop and emit periodic `[scorch-rescore.queue.wait]` progress logs (pending, submitted, completed, failed, elapsed) while draining queued futures.
Why: After `Processing Combos ... 100%`, the pipeline can appear hung with no output while SCORCH futures are still running in queue-drain phase; existing code blocked in `as_completed(...)` without heartbeat logs.
Impact: No behavior or scoring semantics changes; tail-phase progress is now visible, making SCORCH drain latency distinguishable from deadlock/stall in production logs.
Next: Run a full distributed bench run and verify the wait-heartbeat logs appear during long tail windows before `[scorch-rescore.queue.summary]`.

Date: 2026-02-23 | Task: Distributed SCORCH/Retention Race Guard + CSV Coverage Regression Test | Owner: Agent
Change: Added distributed phase-marker barrier primitives in `src/cli/distributed_context.py` (`write_phase_marker`, `wait_for_all_phase_markers`) and wired `main.py` to publish/wait on `phase=scorch_drain` after local SCORCH queue drain and before deferred retention; retention now skips when the distributed SCORCH barrier is incomplete to avoid deleting stage artifacts while peer tasks may still be rescoring. Added regression test `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_phase_markers.py` and added a throughput-integrity regression in `chemdb/tests/test_throughput_integrity.py` that fails on partial post-SCORCH coverage and passes once missing combo rows are present.
Why: In distributed runs, one task could enter deferred retention after its own queue drained while other tasks were still running SCORCH, causing missing/broken SCORCH inputs and incomplete combo CSV coverage.
Impact: Cross-node ordering between SCORCH and retention is now explicit, reducing missing SCORCH outputs caused by retention races; throughput-integrity regression now directly exercises the partial-vs-full SCORCH coverage case observed in bench-style distributed runs.
Next: Re-run the same distributed benchmark (`sim_bench2_fast_*`) and verify all expected combos emit `post_docked/<run_id>/<pdb>/<variant>/<ph>/scorch_scores_all.csv` and `scorch/_DONE`, with no `[scorch-rescore] ... FileNotFoundError` from `.scorch_inputs`.

Date: 2026-02-24 | Task: SCORCH Reconcile False-Failure Fix | Owner: Agent
Change: Fixed `main.py` reconcile success gate in `_reconcile_missing_post_scorch_outputs(...)` from `int(rc or 1) == 0` to `rc is not None and int(rc) == 0`, preserving true success when SCORCH returns `0`; validated with `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_reconcile_missing_outputs.py` plus throughput/distributed marker regressions.
Why: The previous expression treated `returncode=0` as falsey and converted it to `1`, incorrectly classifying successful SCORCH retry materialization as failure and preventing expected self-heal behavior for missing post-SCORCH outputs.
Impact: Reconcile retries now correctly mark successful reruns as succeeded, restoring robust post-SCORCH output recovery for future distributed runs while preserving existing semantics.
Next: Re-run a distributed bench (`-bench2 -fast`) and confirm missing-combo reconcile logs show `action=ok` when outputs are regenerated; use strict throughput integrity to verify full coverage.

Date: 2026-02-24 | Task: SCORCH Lock/Runtime Retry + Non-Fatal Materialize Missing Inputs | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `src/post_docking/rescoring/scorch_lock_fallback.py` and wired `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` to retry failed SCORCH subprocesses when lock signatures are detected (libmamba lock contention) by re-invoking with direct env Python; added runtime fallback for `MemoryError`/`BrokenProcessPool` signatures to retry with `--threads 1` when applicable. Updated SCORCH materialization path to treat `FileNotFoundError` for missing stage inputs as degraded per-ligand skip (non-fatal) instead of failing the entire combo stage. Added regression tests `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_lock_fallback.py` and `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_materialize_nonfatal.py`.
Why: Distributed runs showed transient environment-lock failures and occasional pool/memory runtime failures plus missing stage artifact races that escalated to combo-level failure; these are recoverable execution hazards rather than scientific failures.
Impact: SCORCH is now more robust under lock contention and runtime pool instability; missing per-ligand stage files in materialization no longer hard-fail the combo and instead continue with degraded coverage. Scientific scoring logic remains unchanged for valid inputs.
Next: Keep running strict throughput integrity on distributed canaries and monitor lock/runtime fallback counters; if repeated runtime retries persist, tune stage threading defaults and batch sizing for high-core nodes.

Date: 2026-02-24 | Task: SCORCH Top-Fraction Contract + Distributed Tail Occupancy Tuning | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated SCORCH defaults and tail scheduling in `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` to `SCORCH_TOP_FRACTION_DEFAULT=0.10`, `SCORCH_CHUNK_MIN=16`, `SCORCH_TAIL_SPLIT_TRIGGER=64`, and `SCORCH_HEDGE_MIN_ALLOWED=64`. Added partition-invariant instrumentation for selected ligand sets using new helper module `src/post_docking/rescoring/scorch_selection_invariants.py`, with structured logs reporting chunk partition preservation (`missing/extra/duplicate`). Increased distributed chunk worker occupancy in `main.py` by changing local worker policy constants (`_CHUNK_LOCAL_WORKER_CPU_DIVISOR=3`, `_CHUNK_LOCAL_WORKER_MAX=24`) and reduced control-plane scan pressure by setting `_CHUNK_STATE_RECONCILE_SEC=30.0`. Added regression tests in `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_top_fraction_global_selection.py` and updated distributed worker budget assertions in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_regressions.py`.
Why: Production intent requires global top-fraction SCORCH selection semantics (not per-chunk drift), while distributed tails were underutilizing available CPU and spending excess time in chunk wait/poll cycles.
Impact: SCORCH selection defaults now match intended 10% behavior when unset; selection-to-chunk partitioning is explicitly auditable in logs. Distributed nodes can run more local chunk workers in high-core allocations, improving occupancy during chunk execution/tail phases without changing ligand/scoring semantics.
Next: Validate full distributed canary completion for `sim_bench2_fast_top10_20260224_1` with strict throughput integrity and zero new summarized error signatures; if stable, keep these defaults for the next production allocation run.

Date: 2026-02-24 | Task: Distributed Log I/O Reduction in Per-Ligand Validation | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/docking/docking_stage_runner.py` to demote per-ligand successful validation payload logs from `INFO` to `DEBUG` while preserving invalid-pose validation logs at `INFO`.
Why: Large distributed runs were emitting very high-cardinality validation logs for every valid ligand, creating avoidable log I/O overhead and slower runtime progress under shared filesystem logging.
Impact: Runtime behavior and scoring semantics are unchanged; operational visibility for invalid ligands remains at `INFO`, with full valid-pose payloads still available under debug logging.
Next: Confirm end-to-end distributed canary completion and compare wall-time/log-volume deltas against prior canaries.

Date: 2026-02-24 | Task: SCORCH Tail Housekeeping Chunked PoseBusters Invocation | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` `_run_pose_bust(...)` to invoke `pose_bust.py` with `--posebusters-chunk 256` during SCORCH housekeeping.
Why: Tail investigation showed `bust` being launched with extremely large single argument lists (hundreds of SDF paths), increasing tail latency and creating unstable long-running housekeeping calls during `scorch_drain`.
Impact: PoseBusters semantics are unchanged (same inputs, same outputs), but processing is chunked into bounded calls to reduce argv overhead and improve tail stability.
Next: Validate on the next distributed canary that `scorch_drain` wall-time shrinks and no duplicate/missing SCORCH outputs regress.

Date: 2026-02-24 | Task: Benchmark Capture/Compare + Progress Snapshot Utility | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `tools/benchmark_bench2_compare.py` with `capture` and `compare` flows to snapshot bench2 run timing/chunk/protein/scorch counts, stage screening parity, consensus duplicate stats, and quality deltas versus baseline/reference tables; added `tools/progress_snapshot.sh` for compact distributed run progress reporting; added `analysis/benchmarks/` to `.gitignore`.
Why: Repeated distributed tuning needs a low-friction, reproducible way to capture baseline vs optimized throughput/quality and monitor live progress without parsing raw slurm logs.
Impact: No pipeline execution semantics changed; only analysis/progress tooling and ignore rules were added. Outputs are written under `analysis/benchmarks/<run_id>/...` and excluded from git.
Next: Use `python tools/benchmark_bench2_compare.py capture --run-id <id>` before optimization, then `compare --baseline-run-id <id1> --optimized-run-id <id2>` after changes, and monitor active runs via `tools/progress_snapshot.sh <run_id>`.

Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Bench2 Baseline/Optimized A-B + Chunk Tuning Verification | Owner: Agent
Change: Added benchmark artifact expansion in `tools/benchmark_bench2_compare.py` (strict throughput-integrity invocation, stage-order checks, stage-level screening aggregates, duplicate checks for consensus/scorch outputs, DUD-E target extraction, reference delta pass/fail, and optimization backlog generation). Added env-overridable distributed chunk tuning constants in `main.py` (`ATLAS_CHUNK_LOCAL_WORKER_CPU_DIVISOR`, `ATLAS_CHUNK_STATE_RECONCILE_SEC`) and ran A/B simulated distributed bench2 runs: baseline (`bench2_base_20260225_a`, forced divisor=3/reconcile=30) vs optimized (`bench2_opt_20260225_a`, divisor=2/reconcile=10). Updated distributed worker-budget regression expectation in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_regressions.py`.
Why: Needed explicit, reproducible benchmark artifacts and a low-risk throughput tuning pass for distributed execution with direct baseline-vs-optimized comparison and parity guardrails.
Impact: Benchmarking observability is significantly improved. The tested tuning combination regressed wall-time in this environment (optimized slower than baseline), so it should not be promoted as-is for production.
Next: Revert or gate the divisor=2 change for production, keep the benchmark tooling, and prioritize chunk-size/control-plane optimizations (adaptive chunk sizing and claim-loop result-cache) for the next throughput pass.

Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Distributed Worker Divisor Rollback to Conservative Default | Owner: Agent
Change: Kept `ATLAS_CHUNK_LOCAL_WORKER_CPU_DIVISOR` env override in `main.py` but restored default/fallback to `3` (from temporary `2`) after A/B regression; retained `ATLAS_CHUNK_STATE_RECONCILE_SEC` env override and tuned default at `10.0s`. Restored worker-budget regression expectation in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_regressions.py` to match default divisor `3`.
Why: Bench2 A/B showed divisor `2` did not improve end-to-end throughput and should not become production default without stronger evidence.
Impact: Production behavior remains conservative/stable by default while preserving explicit env-based experimentation for future throughput trials.
Next: Focus next optimization pass on adaptive chunk sizing and claim-loop control-plane caching instead of increasing local worker multiplicity.

Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Adaptive Chunk Sizing + Claim-Loop Cache Defaults (Knob-Light) | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `src/cli/distributed_chunk_sizing.py` and wired `_split_ligands_into_chunks(...)` in `main.py` to use adaptive sizing in distributed mode (larger chunks for large ligand sets, legacy sizing for non-distributed). Added `src/cli/distributed_chunk_claim_cache.py` and integrated cache-backed claim candidate iteration/reconciliation into the distributed chunk worker loop with local result cache updates on chunk write. Removed newly introduced env-toggle gates for these features and kept behavior default-on without new config-file lines.
Why: Bench-scale and production-scale runs were oversharded and paid avoidable control-plane FS cost from repeated per-claim result probing; user requested fewer knobs and unset-friendly defaults.
Impact: Distributed runs should issue fewer claim-loop metadata reads and use larger effective chunks on large libraries, reducing orchestration overhead while preserving docking/scoring semantics.
Next: Complete benchmark compare against latest passing baseline (`bench2_opt_20260225_a` or newer) and keep only the throughput-positive defaults; if regressions appear, adjust policy thresholds in `distributed_chunk_sizing.py` without adding config surface.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Distributed Throughput Pass (PoseBusters Core Cap + Root-Log Throttle) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` to run SCORCH housekeeping `pose_bust.py` with explicit `--posebusters-max-workers` capped by granted global cores (want up to min(4, jobs*threads), min still 1). Updated `src/docking/library_mode.py` to emit `[ligands.allowed-roots]` and `[ph_ligand.roots]` at INFO only on first unique root mapping per process and DEBUG on repeats.
Why: Bench2 distributed runs still showed control-plane/logging overhead and SCORCH housekeeping thread fanout could exceed effective node allocation, causing avoidable contention during tail.
Impact: Scientific outputs are unchanged; only scheduling/logging behavior is adjusted. Housekeeping now respects granted core budget and repetitive root logs are reduced to lower shared-filesystem log pressure.
Next: Re-run distributed bench2 and compare wall-time/throughput integrity against `bench2_optset2_20260225_a` and `bench2_optset3_20260225_a` baselines.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Distributed Claim-Cache FS Load Cut + Large-Scale Chunk Tier Retune | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/cli/distributed_chunk_claim_cache.py` so reconcile reads only non-terminal chunk IDs and claim selection iterates a refreshed non-terminal candidate list instead of scanning terminal chunks repeatedly. Retuned high-ligand adaptive chunk tiers in `src/cli/distributed_chunk_sizing.py` (`>=5000` and `>=20000`) toward larger chunk targets/minimums with lower parallel-floor multiplier.
Why: Distributed control-plane overhead remained high from repeated file probes of already terminal chunks, and large production ligand libraries were still oversharded into too many small chunks.
Impact: No docking/scoring semantics change; reduced metadata I/O and lower orchestration overhead at production scale. Bench2 semantics remain unchanged.
Next: Validate with simulated distributed bench2 and then production canary to confirm lower idle-tail wait and improved wall-time.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: SCORCH Tail Batch-Latency Trim (PoseBusters Chunk Size) | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` `_run_pose_bust(...)` batch size from `--posebusters-chunk 256` to `96`.
Why: Observed long tail behavior with very large single `bust` invocations; smaller chunks reduce single-call latency/argv size and improve forward progress visibility without changing scoring semantics.
Impact: Same input ligand set and validation path, but processed in more bounded PoseBusters calls to reduce tail straggler risk.
