Next: Validate next distributed bench2 run wall-time and ensure throughput-integrity/dud-e parity remain within tolerance.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Parity-First Rollback for Distributed Chunk-Local Stage Drift | Owner: Agent
Change: Reverted `main.py` distributed chunk splitting path from adaptive policy module back to legacy `_split_ligands_into_chunks(...)` logic (target/min/max + overshard factor), and removed the adaptive callsite argument wiring.
Why: Stage progression uses chunk-local selection (`select_ligands_for_next` computes `k=int(pool_n*pct)` per chunk). Changing chunk boundaries changed stage2/stage3 populations, which then changed SCORCH candidate volume and duplicate-row totals in aggregate outputs.
Impact: Restores baseline scientific parity behavior for stage counts/rescore coverage before any further throughput tuning. Claim-loop cache changes remain in place (control-plane optimization only).
Next: Re-run distributed `-bench2 -fast` and regenerate compare artifacts against `bench2_optset2_20260225_a`; only proceed with additional throughput tuning after parity checks pass.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Parity Drift Hardening for Silent Completed Chunk | Owner: Agent
Change: Hardened `main.py` chunk verification so distributed chunk mode fails when the chunk-scoped stage1 completion marker is missing and when all expected chunk ligands are absent from the combo docking summary (`missing_all_chunk_scores`). Kept backward compatibility by making `chunk_id` optional and only enforcing these hard checks in chunk mode. Added regression coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_distributed_chunk_regressions.py` for `missing_stage1_chunk_marker` and chunk-mode `missing_all_chunk_scores` fatal behavior.
Why: `bench2_parity_20260225` showed a silent drift pattern where one chunk (`BOJG HOLO pH7_2`) was marked `completed` with zero softfails but produced no stage outputs, dropping 33 ligands from downstream parity without explicit chunk failure.
Impact: Prevents silent successful completion of empty/missing-output chunks in distributed runs; such chunks now retry/fail instead of quietly shrinking screened/rescored ligand counts. Non-chunk verification behavior remains unchanged.
Next: Let active run `bench2_parity_fix2_20260225` finish tail housekeeping, then run strict capture/compare to confirm full parity restoration end-to-end.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: SCORCH Tail Unblock by Async PoseBusters Launch | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` to launch `pose_bust.py` asynchronously (detached `subprocess.Popen`) via a new `_launch_pose_bust_async(...)` helper under existing `run_housekeeping_once(...)` once-per-run semantics. Removed blocking core-acquire wait around pose_bust preflight so SCORCH scoring can proceed immediately; bounded async pose_bust workers to max 2 to reduce interference. Updated `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_pose_bust_invocation.py` for current pose_bust CLI contract and added async-launch unit coverage.
Why: Tail latency was dominated by synchronous pose_bust preflight, effectively gating SCORCH completion even though pose-buster outputs are not required to run SCORCH scoring.
Impact: SCORCH and pose_bust now overlap in time; run completion is no longer blocked on pose_bust preflight execution. Verified on `bench2_poseasync_20260225_a`: strict throughput integrity passed (`4920/4920`) and wall time improved vs strict baseline `bench2_optset2_20260225_a` (3154s -> 2312s, ~26.7% faster).
Next: If desired, add an explicit opt-in/opt-out switch for async pose_bust mode and tune worker bound (1 vs 2) based on production-node contention.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Deferred SCORCH Submit Race Guard + Aggregate Dedup | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` deferred SCORCH submission flow to use atomic reservation/finalization (`combo_scorch_submitting` + `combo_scorch_submitted`) so check-and-submit is no longer racy. Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` `_aggregate_combo(...)` to deduplicate repeated rows by `(source, Ligand_ID)` and keep the highest composite score per key.
Why: Distributed tail could queue the same combo from concurrent paths, creating repeated chunk-level SCORCH outputs; aggregate previously concatenated part files without dedup, inflating `scorch_scores_all.csv` and introducing non-deterministic drift.
Impact: Removes duplicate queue submissions for the same combo within a task and prevents repeated aggregate rows from affecting downstream artifacts. This addresses race/duplication-driven drift, but does not eliminate upstream Vina stochastic rank drift in `-fast` mode.
Next: Run an A/B pair on identical code/settings (different run IDs) and compare rescored ligand overlap to separate residual stochastic drift from scheduler-induced drift.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Verify-Tools Runtime Hardening for OpenBabel + Prepare-Ligand | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` `--verify-tools` path to include runtime probes: `OPENBABEL_PATH` now executes `obabel -V` and enforces major version >= 3; `MGLTOOLS_PYTHON` + `PREPARE_LIGAND_SCRIPT` now execute `<pythonsh> <prepare_ligand4.py> -h` to verify callability. Added structured runtime failure reporting and success detail lines to verify output.
Why: Path existence checks alone allowed incompatible environments to pass verification (for example Python-3 `pythonsh` with Python-2 `prepare_ligand4.py`, or Open Babel variants that exist but are unsuitable), which led to ligand prep failures at runtime.
Impact: `--verify-tools` now fails early for incompatible Open Babel versions and non-callable prepare-ligand toolchains even when executables are present on disk/PATH. This is a behavioral/output contract change for startup verification.
Next: If operators need flexibility, add a config/CLI override for Open Babel minimum major version while keeping the default guard at >= 3.
Date: 2026-02-25 | Task: Multi-PDB CLI Convention + Comma-Packed QoL Alias | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/cli/cli_utils.py` parsing to keep `--pdb`/`--pdbs` behavior and add strict comma-packed short-form support (e.g., `-test,temp,exam`) where every segment must resolve to an exact 4-char PDB-like token; updated `main.py` help text to mark repeatable `--pdb` as the recommended style and document aliases.
Why: Existing QoL short-flag usage was convenient but inconsistent for multi-PDB invocation; users needed a cleaner professional convention while preserving backward compatibility.
Impact: Existing flags remain intact, including `-fast`, `-dude`, `--pdb`, `--pdbs`, and bare-token inputs; new comma-packed short-form is additive and guarded to avoid accidental capture of longer control flags.
Next: If desired, add parser-focused unit coverage in an allowed test location to lock CLI compatibility behavior.
Date: 2026-02-26 | Task: Extracted Ligand Prep Crash Guard + Soft-Fail Continuation | Owner: Agent
Change: Fixed extracted ligand protonation candidate selection in `src/prep_ligands/prep_ligands_crystal.py` to use predeclared `protoA/protoB` paths, eliminating `UnboundLocalError` when RDKit AddHs path fails. Updated `src/docking/docking_ligands.py` `prepare_and_filter_ligands(...)` to catch `prep_ligands_from_pdb(...)` exceptions, record `_SOFTFAIL_LIGAND_COUNT/_SOFTFAIL_LIGAND_REASONS` with `extracted_ligprep_exception`, and continue execution.
Why: A single extracted-ligand prep exception (`protoA` referenced before assignment) bubbled up as a hard per-target failure, contradicting recent robustness goals.
Impact: Extracted-ligand prep failures now degrade runs instead of failing the whole target path by default, while preserving existing downstream skip behavior when no valid ligands remain.
Next: Re-run the previously failing target (e.g., `1E66`) and confirm no hard failure logs are emitted for this condition and degraded counters are populated when exceptions occur.
Date: 2026-03-02 | Task: Auto Execution Mode + Hybrid Completion-Lane Scheduler | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `src/cli/distributed_mode_policy.py`, `src/cli/hybrid_chunk_scheduler.py`, and `src/cli/distributed_combo_runner.py`; wired `main.py` to auto-resolve execution mode (`single/per-protein/combo/combo-hybrid`) with env override (`ATLAS_EXECUTION_MODE`), hybrid fallback guards, periodic candidate-order rebalance, completion-lane promotion, and structured scheduler telemetry (`[execution.mode]`, `[hybrid.lane]`, `[hybrid.rebalance]`, `[hybrid.progress]`, `[execution.summary]`). Added run summary artifact `manifests/<run_id>/scheduler_summary.json` and new tests covering policy thresholds, hybrid ordering determinism, combo/hybrid fallback, and A/B objective constraint checks.
Why: Large distributed runs needed a middle ground between per-protein completion latency and combo-chunk makespan while keeping configuration surface minimal and preserving existing compatibility switches.
Impact: Distributed runs can now auto-select a balanced mode at scale, adapt chunk-claim ordering toward near-complete scopes, and emit measurable first-result/makespan/rebalance metrics without requiring new config-file keys.
Next: Run a representative large distributed benchmark to calibrate lane-fraction behavior against observed wall-time and adjust fallback/threshold heuristics if production telemetry deviates from A/B targets.
Date: 2026-03-03 | Task: Main Orchestration Decomposition (Scheduler + Planner Extraction) | Owner: Agent
Change: Extracted large orchestration surfaces from `main.py` into new modules: `src/cli/distributed_chunk_planner.py` (chunk planning/manifest preflight/coverage+verification/cpu policy helpers), `src/cli/distributed_chunk_runtime.py` (distributed combo-chunk execution loop), `src/cli/local_scheduler_runtime.py` (single-ligand/global/serial local scheduling branches), and `src/cli/run_lifecycle.py` (scheduler summary + SCORCH drain/reconcile + deferred retention phase). Rewired `main.py` to call these modules and reduced direct in-file scheduling logic. Added compatibility shims/exports in `main.py` for legacy monkeypatch-based tests (`_build_combo_work_items` + pH discovery wrappers, `_resolve_combo_ligand_bases`, `_split_ligands_into_chunks`, `_distributed_chunk_local_workers`, `_distributed_chunk_cpu_per_worker`, `_collect_combo_library_roots`, wait constants, and `_ensure_distributed_manifest_preflight` forwarding wrapper).
Why: `main.py` had grown to ~7.3k lines with deeply nested scheduler and distributed control logic, making maintenance risky and review velocity low. The refactor was requested to move toward a more professional, modular orchestration layout while keeping runtime behavior stable.
Impact: `main.py` reduced to ~3.5k lines and responsibilities are now split across focused `src/cli` modules. Runtime smoke path (`python main.py --test -fast`) remains passing; scheduler/distributed regression suites pass after compatibility shims. `mypy` still reports pre-existing repo-wide environment/module-layout issues (`types-PyYAML` missing and duplicate-module discovery for `path_router` vs `src.path_router`) that are not introduced by this change.
Next: Continue phase-2 decomposition by moving distributed/non-distributed post-run finalize/report blocks into `src/cli/run_lifecycle.py` so `main()` becomes a thin composition entrypoint (<2.5k lines target), then add explicit contract tests for new runtime modules to reduce dependence on `main` monkeypatch shims.
Date: 2026-03-03 | Task: Professionalism Gate + Bench2 Audit Baseline | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `tools/quality_gate.sh` (scoped Ruff + MyPy gate with optional smoke), added GitHub workflow `.github/workflows/quality.yml` to run the gate on push/PR, documented the new gate in `docs/tools.quality_gate.sh.md`, added `docs/professionalism_audit_2026-03-02.md` with prioritized no-behavior-change professionalism findings, added `.editorconfig` for baseline editing consistency, added `tools/repo_hotspots.py` (+ docs) for objective simplification prioritization, simplified duplicate command/logging logic in `tools/simulate_slurm_array.py`, and hardened `tools/progress_snapshot.sh` status counting (no-plan safety + fast recursive file counting).
Why: The repo needed a repeatable quality baseline and a practical professionalism audit path that is enforceable in automation without blocking on legacy/vendor lint debt.
Impact: No pipeline runtime semantics changed; developer quality checks are now reproducible locally and in CI; bench2 benchmarking workflow remains unchanged and uses existing compare tooling.
Next: Finish the active bench2 baseline run, capture benchmark artifacts with `tools/benchmark_bench2_compare.py`, apply additional scoped simplification fixes, then run the same bench2 profile again and compare pre/post metrics.
Date: 2026-03-03 | Task: Main Post-Run Lifecycle Extraction + Distributed Runtime Helper Split | Owner: Agent
Change: Extended `src/cli/run_lifecycle.py` with `finalize_post_run(...)` and moved main post-run orchestration out of `main.py` (manifest finalize/defer, MMGBSA pass, distributed completion marker write/barrier fallback finalize, report/dud/master export, throughput integrity enforcement, rescore verification). Updated `main.py` to call `finalize_post_run(...)` and return/exit based on its result. Added `src/cli/distributed_chunk_runtime_helpers.py` and extracted distributed runtime helper logic from `src/cli/distributed_chunk_runtime.py`: scope-map construction (`build_scope_maps`), weight-total aggregation (`build_combo_weight_totals`), terminal chunk failure collection (`collect_terminal_chunk_failures`), and owned completion marker aggregation (`aggregate_completion_markers_for_owned_scopes`). Rewired `run_distributed_combo_variant(...)` to use these helpers.
Why: Continued decomposition of high-churn orchestration logic to improve readability and reduce cognitive load in `main.py` and distributed runtime paths without changing behavior.
Impact: `main.py` post-run control flow is now delegated to lifecycle module, reducing direct orchestration branching in entrypoint; distributed chunk runtime has reduced inline repeated blocks and clearer boundaries for planning/failure aggregation/finalization concerns. Existing regression and smoke behavior remains unchanged.
Next: Continue decomposition by extracting the remaining large `main()` startup/bootstrap section into `src/cli/run_bootstrap.py` and progressively replacing `main.py` compatibility shim exports with direct module-level contract tests when protected test-edit policy allows.
Date: 2026-03-03 | Task: Main Startup Bootstrap Delegation to run_bootstrap | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `main.py` to delegate the startup/bootstrap half of `main()` to `src/cli/run_bootstrap.py` via `bootstrap_pre_variant_state(...)`, then hydrate local runtime variables from the returned `BootstrapState`. Removed the large inline bootstrap block in `main()` and cleaned stale imports/locals with Ruff.
Why: `main()` still contained a very large pre-variant initialization path after prior scheduler/lifecycle extraction; moving it behind a dedicated module boundary improves readability and keeps entrypoint responsibilities focused on orchestration composition.
Impact: Startup behavior remains equivalent while `main.py` is substantially smaller and easier to scan; typing/lint checks on touched files pass and smoke test `python main.py --test -fast` succeeds (exit 0).
Next: Split `src/cli/run_bootstrap.py` into smaller focused helpers (resume hydration, runtime config defaults, distributed mode setup) to avoid shifting a monolith from `main.py` into a single new large module.
Date: 2026-03-04 | Task: run_bootstrap Helper Decomposition (Resume/Distributed/Defaults) | Owner: Agent
Change: Refactored `src/cli/run_bootstrap.py` to split `bootstrap_pre_variant_state(...)` into focused helpers: `_setup_distributed_runtime(...)`, `_hydrate_resume_state(...)`, `_apply_runtime_config_defaults(...)`, `_select_workload(...)`, `_record_scheduled_proteins_for_manifest(...)`, and `_prepare_variant_bootstrap(...)`. Date: 2026-03-06 | Task: SCORCH Chunk Input Isolation Fix | Owner: Agent
Change: Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` so `_materialize_inputs(...)` isolates staged `.scorch_inputs` by `chunk_tag` when SCORCH work is chunked, and updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/scorch_stage_runner.py` to forward the active `chunk_tag` into materialization. Added regression coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_materialize_inputs.py` and `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_materialize_nonfatal.py`.
Why: In chunked runs, later SCORCH tasks reused the first chunk's staged input directory when `overwrite` was false, so each part rescored the same ligand subset and aggregate output silently dropped intended coverage. `BIGregressioncheck` showed identical per-part row counts and collapsed aggregate counts for affected combos.
Impact: Chunked SCORCH rescoring now materializes and scores the intended ligand partition per chunk; unchunked behavior and output schema stay unchanged. Validation passed for targeted pytest coverage, focused mypy/py_compile, and smoke `python main.py --test -fast`; repo-wide `ruff check --fix .` still reports unrelated pre-existing violations outside this change scope.
Next: Rerun SCORCH for `BIGregressioncheck` (or a representative benchmark) to regenerate corrected `post_docked/.../scorch_scores*.csv` outputs and re-measure the expected lift against the prior broken aggregate.
Date: 2026-03-06 | Task: SCORCH Stage-Consistent Rescoring Provenance | Owner: Agent
Change: Added stage-provenance dataclasses in `src/post_docking/rescoring/scorch_types.py`, taught `src/post_docking/rescoring/scorch_selection.py` to retain the stage and docking score that selected each ligand, and changed `src/post_docking/rescoring/scorch_stage_runner.py` plus orchestration/parallel task plumbing to rescore the selected stage first with explicit fallback when that pose is missing. Updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py` annotation to stamp `selected_stage`, `rescored_stage`, `selected_docking_score`, `stage_match_flag`, and `stage_fallback_reason` into SCORCH CSVs, and updated `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescore_reranker.py` to preserve those columns in `consensus_reranked_scorch.csv` while preferring stage-matched SCORCH rows if duplicate ligand-base rows exist. Added regressions in `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_top_fraction_global_selection.py`, `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_post_engine_selection.py`, `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_stage_rescore_counts_regression.py`, `chemdb/tests/test_scorch_global_selection.py`, and `chemdb/tests/test_record_data_csv.py`.
Why: BOJG investigation showed a real selection/rescoring mismatch: docking-score selection could choose a ligand because of a strong lower-stage pose, but rescoring then defaulted to the highest available stage pose. That makes SCORCH evaluate a different conformation than the one that earned selection, which is a credible source of BOJG-specific performance loss once chunk coverage was fixed.
Impact: SCORCH now rescales on stage-consistent poses across engines, and the resulting CSV outputs expose enough provenance to audit stage mismatches directly from `scorch_scores*.csv` and `consensus_reranked_scorch.csv`. Focused validation passed: targeted SCORCH pytest suite (`26 passed`), focused mypy on changed rescoring modules (`Success: no issues found in 7 source files`), focused ruff on touched files (`All checks passed`), and smoke `python main.py --test -fast` (pass, run_id `20260306_132913`). Repo-wide `ruff check --fix .` still fails on unrelated pre-existing violations in other modules.
Next: Rerun SCORCH for `BIGregressioncheck`, compare BOJG `selected_stage` vs `rescored_stage` distributions post-fix, and then re-evaluate whether BOJG underperformance remains in raw SCORCH scores or shifts downstream into ranking/blending behavior.
Date: 2026-03-06 | Task: BOJG Sparse Rescoring Anchor Prototype | Owner: Agent
Change: Added an opt-in sparse-rescoring anchor path to `src/post_docking/rescoring/rescore_reranker.py` via `sparse_rescoring_anchor_threshold` and `sparse_rescoring_anchor_weight`, so rescored rows in low-coverage groups can keep a configurable share of their consensus z-score instead of being fully overwritten by blend-z. Added regression coverage in `chemdb/tests/test_record_data_csv.py` to verify the anchored path improves a strong-consensus rescored active without changing default behavior when the anchor is off. Built an isolated BOJG prototype output root at `analysis/_tmp/bojg_sparse_anchor_candidate_20260306/BOJG` and evaluated it with DUD-eval under `analysis/_tmp/bojg_sparse_anchor_eval_20260306`.
Why: BOJG stage-consistent reruns showed two separate problems: raw SCORCH remained weak, but `pH7_7` also buried rescored actives and elevated the control ligand because sparse rescoring replaced consensus z-scores outright for only ~10% of the library. The prototype addresses the downstream suppression piece without changing global defaults.
Impact: Raw BOJG SCORCH is still poor by selected stage, but stage1 is less bad than stage2 (`pH7_2` stage1 ROC_AUC 0.399 vs stage2 0.319; `pH7_7` stage1 ROC_AUC 0.461 vs stage2 0.359 on rescored subsets using `scorch_composite`). The sparse-anchor prototype improved BOJG vs the current stage-consistent baseline: `pH7_2` ROC_AUC `0.662 -> 0.704`, PR_AUC `0.105 -> 0.106`; `pH7_7` ROC_AUC `0.654 -> 0.693`, PR_AUC `0.064 -> 0.072`. It also demoted the BOJG `pH7_7` control ligand `19A_A360.sanitized.pdbqt` from final rank `1` to `15`. The prototype still does not beat plain consensus on BOJG global metrics, so it is a useful candidate safeguard, not a full fix.
Next: Decide whether to keep the sparse-anchor path as an opt-in experiment, then investigate BOJG raw SCORCH quality by stage/chemotype or constrain rescoring selection toward the less pathological stage distributions before considering a global default change.

Date: 2026-03-07 | Task: Fix Clustergrammer Zoom Color Drift and Make Sizing Element-Responsive | Owner: Agent
Change: Reworked the Clustergrammer wrapper so post-redraw tile colors are derived from the currently rendered tile data (`value` / `value_up` / `value_dn`) instead of only original-cell metadata, kept numeric overlays restricted to exact metadata-backed cells, and replaced the current window-only resize path with `ResizeObserver`-driven `sidebar_width`/`resize_viz()` updates based on the actual heatmap host width. Also switched the report shell from a fixed max-width to a full-width container with clamped horizontal padding.
Why: Zoomed-out Clustergrammer states can show aggregated tiles, so repainting from original-cell metadata can diverge from the visible tile values and turn neutral regions blue. The fixed viewport assumptions were also making the layout brittle across resolutions.
Impact: Generated heatmaps should preserve the custom white-centered palette through zoom while matching the visible aggregated tile values, and the report width should adapt to the actual rendered container instead of relying on hardcoded desktop breakpoints. Existing short PDB labels, hover metadata, and removed cell-details panel remain unchanged.
Next: Regenerate `pilotstudy` and visually verify zoom-out no longer flips neutral cells blue and that the heatmap width tracks browser/container size cleanly on both laptop and large desktop widths.

Change: Changed the Clustergrammer payload builders to map missing cells to the neutral center (`0.0`) instead of the matrix minimum, removed the `view: aggregated zoom` tooltip line, stripped SVG truncation attributes/trailing dots from visible PDB labels, removed the JS width-forcing helper that was stretching the inner heatmap root, compacted the custom legend further, and started provisioning Playwright plus a local Chromium bundle under `/stor/home/mpg2352` for browser-side inspection.
Why: The previous mode-payload path was still serializing missing cells as the most negative observed z-score, which turned broad empty regions blue. The browser-side layout also remained brittle because it was resizing the inner heatmap container instead of giving the outer heatmap section more room, and the lack of a local browser made visual debugging too indirect.
Impact: Missing cells should render at the white center again, visible PDB labels should no longer end in `..`, the extra aggregated-hover line is removed, and future debugging on this machine can use a real local headless browser rather than only HTML inspection.
Next: Finish browser validation against `data/pilotstudy/report.html`, especially checking that the compact legend is visible, the heatmap block uses the full page width, and no neutral regions are still drifting blue on zoom.
Date: 2026-03-07 | Task: Split Bulk Prep Phase And Share Crystal Prep Bootstrap | Owner: Agent
Change: Extracted bulk phase-4 scheduling/result handling into prep_ligands_bulk_prepare.py, moved shared malformed/status/protonation audit helpers into prep_ligands_reporting.py, and rewired crystal/bootstrap onto shared runtime helpers for toolchain/workspace setup.

Impact: Bulk prep is now a thin orchestrator, crystal prep reuses the same tool/workspace initialization path as bulk, and status/audit helpers have a single owner. Behavior stays the same, including TEST regeneration, resume semantics, and microstate bookkeeping.

Date: 2026-03-07 | Task: Split Bulk MOL2 Generation Orchestration | Owner: Agent

Why: The bulk MOL2 phase was still mixing SDF source discovery, deprecated-layout recovery, RDKit-vs-OBabel policy, ONLY filtering, and per-SDF progress flow in one block, making the file harder to edit safely.
Impact: Behavior stays the same, including ONLY filtering and legacy MOL2 recovery, but phase-3 ownership is cleaner. This pass did not reduce total prep LOC because the new owner module adds more lines than it removed from bulk.
Next: If the goal is actual line-count reduction rather than cleaner ownership, the next pass should consolidate duplicated low-level chemistry helpers instead of extracting another orchestration module.
Date: 2026-03-07 | Task: Consolidate Shared Chemistry Writers In Ligand Prep | Owner: Agent
Change: Removed the dead bulk _reserialize_mol_via_obabel path, collapsed the duplicate RDKit->SDF writers into prep_ligands_common_conversion._write_sdf_for_obabel(...), and rewired bulk embedding to use the shared helper.
Why: The prior bulk/common split improved ownership but still duplicated low-level chemistry I/O logic. This pass targeted real duplicate code so the total prep LOC would actually drop.
Impact: Behavior stays the same for bulk RDKit embedding and aromatic rescue, but there is now one shared SDF writer path instead of separate bulk/aromatic implementations.
Next: If further shrink is needed, target duplicated proto candidate generation and MOL2/H-count selection rather than extracting more orchestration wrappers.
Date: 2026-03-07 | Task: Deduplicate Extracted Proto Candidate Selection | Owner: Agent

Why: Crystal extracted-ligand prep still owned duplicated protoA/protoB selection policy next to the common MOL2/H-count helpers, which kept line count and drift risk higher than necessary.
Impact: Behavior stays the same for protoA/protoB generation, fallback to the base MOL2, and re-aromatization, but the extracted candidate module is materially smaller and the selection policy now has one owner.
Next: If more real shrink is needed, target the remaining duplicated candidate-generation details around crystal protonation and common MOL2 conversion rather than adding more orchestration wrappers.
Date: 2026-03-07 | Task: Reuse Shared OBabel Runner In Crystal Ligand Prep | Owner: Agent
Change: Replaced the crystal-only subprocess-based sanitized PDB -> MOL2 baseline conversion with prep_ligands_common_conversion._run_obabel(...), removing the last local execution wrapper in that candidate-generation path.
Why: The remaining crystal protonation/candidate path still duplicated command-execution plumbing even after proto candidate selection had been centralized. This change removed overlap while staying net-negative in LOC.
Impact: Behavior is unchanged for baseline extracted-ligand PDB->MOL2 generation, but crystal prep now uses the same shared OBabel execution path as the rest of ligand prep.
Next: Only continue this area if another shrink can be proven up front; otherwise stop rather than trading line count for ownership churn.
Date: 2026-03-07 | Task: Repo Professionalization Boundary Pass | Owner: Codex
Change: Added explicit repo-boundary guidance in docs/repo_boundaries.md, configured pyproject lint/type exclusions for legacy-vendor-generated paths, and fixed maintained-source Ruff/MyPy issues across docking, MMGBSA, path routing, alias loading, and calibrator helpers.
Why: Repo-wide quality gates were noisy and non-credible because protected tests, generated outputs, and legacy integration code were mixed with maintained runtime code. The maintained boundary also had small unresolved lint/type issues and one real chem alias symbol bug.
Impact: Ruff now passes repo-wide for the maintained-code boundary, mypy passes on the touched maintained modules, and the repo has an explicit maintained-vs-legacy-vs-generated policy. No scoring or pipeline behavior was intentionally changed.
Next: Normalize packaging further by moving root-level library modules behind thin wrappers and continue splitting the largest maintained runtime modules (docking_ligands, rescoring_scorch, mmgbsa_pipeline).
Date: 2026-03-07 | Task: Browser-Verified Clustergrammer Legend and Label Stabilization | Owner: Agent
Change: Used a locally installed Chromium/Playwright stack under `/stor/home/mpg2352` to inspect the generated `pilotstudy` report, then fixed the report shell and Clustergrammer template so the outer `.container`/`#heatmap.section` width rules override Bootstrap, the custom legend bar keeps a fixed visible width inside the flex rail, and column labels are re-synced via a MutationObserver after Clustergrammer re-trims them.
Why: Browser inspection showed the remaining issues were not guesses: Bootstrap was still clamping the report container to `1170px`, the legend bar had collapsed to `2px`, and the actual rendered column label text nodes were being rewritten back to values like `1O43..` after the initial sync pass.
Impact: Generated reports should now use the full page width more reliably, keep the custom color legend visible, and preserve short PDB labels without trailing dots even after Clustergrammer redraws. Local browser tooling is now available on this machine for future report debugging.
Next: Re-open `data/pilotstudy/report.html` in the local browser after regeneration and verify the legend is visibly colored, the outer heatmap section spans the page width, and the visible PDB labels no longer end in `..`.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Packaging And Monolith Ownership Pass | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved installation config loading behind src/config/runtime_config.py with installation.py reduced to a compatibility wrapper, extracted DeepCoy orchestration from src/docking/docking_ligands.py into src/docking/docking_deepcoy.py, extracted MMGBSA pose-selection helpers into src/post_docking/mmgbsa/mmgbsa_pose_selection.py, extracted SCORCH stage I/O into src/post_docking/rescoring/scorch_stage_io.py, and normalized the previously split prep modules so the worktree no longer presents them as ambiguous untracked owners.
Why: The repo was still packaging root modules as if they were primary library owners, several maintained runtime monoliths mixed unrelated concerns, and the prep refactor still looked half-migrated in the worktree.
Impact: Root installation config is now owned under src, the three highest-value monoliths each shed a cohesive subsystem into an owner module, and the prep-module split is explicit rather than ambiguous. Remaining root-wrapper debt still exists around run_manifest.py and postrun_hooks.py, and the MMGBSA monolith family still has substantial pre-existing mypy debt.
Next: Move run_manifest and postrun_hooks behind src owners the same way installation was normalized, then continue carving side-effect-heavy MMGBSA execution logic into smaller modules once their pre-existing typing debt is paid down.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Root Wrapper Normalization For Run Manifest And Hooks | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved run_manifest.py implementation to src/cli/run_manifest_runtime.py and postrun_hooks.py implementation to src/cli/postrun_hooks_runtime.py, rewired maintained imports to the src owners, and reduced the root files to compatibility wrappers. Also fixed post-run subprocess PYTHONPATH/root inference so analysis and src packages resolve correctly after the move.
Why: These two root modules were still acting as primary implementation owners, which kept packaging transitional and made the repo root look like the architecture instead of src. Moving them behind src owners makes future refactors and packaging cleanup much cleaner.
Impact: Root compatibility imports still work, maintained code now imports owner modules directly, and the fast smoke run completes with post-run hooks functioning. External callers that import run_manifest or postrun_hooks continue to work through the wrappers.
Next: The next packaging pass should trim the remaining root py-modules surface in pyproject.toml and move other library-style root modules behind src owners where practical.

Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Normalize Automate Protein Prep Config Ownership | Owner: Codex
Change: Rewired automate_protein_prep.py to import load_config/validate_config from src/config/runtime_config.py, removed its fallback root-wrapper bootstrap block, deleted a duplicated _format_ion_pairs helper, and added the small type annotations needed for standalone mypy success.
Why: automate_protein_prep.py was still treating installation.py and input_and_export_functions.py as active config owners, which kept root wrappers in the execution path and blocked a clean maintained-code quality bar.

Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Pilotstudy Heatmap Label Interactions | Owner: Codex
Change: Preserved all scored targets in the Clustergrammer payload, added a heatmap view summary, made row-label clicks reorder columns by the selected ligand, made column-label clicks focus a single selected target column and sort ligands by that column, and kept repeated column clicks as a reset back to the prior ligand-sorted view.
Why: The pilotstudy report was silently dropping a target after top-ligand filtering, and the HTML heatmap needed direct label-driven analysis controls without making cells navigable.
Impact: Pilotstudy now renders 36/36 targets in the main heatmap, ligand-driven sorting is browser-verified, single-column PDB focus is browser-verified, and cell clicks remain inert; the selected column is keyed by the clicked underlying target label so duplicate visible PDB IDs still resolve deterministically.
Next: If users want duplicate visible PDB IDs to collapse into one aggregated column instead of one clicked target column, add an explicit aggregation mode rather than reusing the current exact-column focus behavior.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Heatmap Promiscuity Hover Metrics | Owner: Codex
Change: Added full-panel ligand and target promiscuity metadata to the heatmap pipeline using the existing `BREADTH_PCT_THRESHOLD` significance cutoff, with hover payloads now carrying a robust z-scored hit count, significant-hit coverage, hit fraction, and normalized entropy for both row and column labels.
Why: The report already had threshold-based breadth logic and full `heatmap_input` rank metadata, so the clean extension was to compute promiscuity once from the full aggregated panel and expose it in hover without changing the current sort/focus interactions.
Impact: Hovering a ligand or target can now show how promiscuous it is relative to peers in the run, based on the full run matrix rather than the current top-100 row subset. Cell hover and label-click behavior remain unchanged.
Next: If the hover metrics prove useful, the next extension should be optional report tables or filters that rank ligands/targets directly by `promiscuity_z` instead of only exposing the score in hover.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Live Heatmap Analysis Controls | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended the Clustergrammer report HTML to ship the full ligand-target panel plus live analysis defaults, added client-side threshold and top-k controls, added an on-page promiscuity explanation block, and introduced synchronized scatter/network/histogram analysis panels that recompute promiscuity in-browser while keeping pose-valid filtering internal-only.
Why: The previous hover-only promiscuity metric was static and hard to interpret. The report already carried enough metadata to support live thresholding and auxiliary views without regenerating the HTML, but the payload and label-identity handling needed to be widened and normalized first.
Impact: `pilotstudy` now renders a live analysis layer on top of the existing heatmap: users can change the significance threshold, change how many ligands are shown, and inspect the same current state through scatter, network, and histogram views. Internal pose-valid filtering still affects the score but is not shown in the UI.
Next: If the new side views are useful in practice, the next cleanup should be to break the large client template into smaller owned helper blocks and add regression coverage for the live controls and analysis panel rendering.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Heatmap Sorting UI Cleanup | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed the visible `Ligands Shown` slider and `Ligands shown: ...` summary text from the Clustergrammer report while keeping the internal default row slice for stable rendering, and preserved label-click sorting so ligand clicks reorder targets and PDB clicks still collapse to a single target while re-ranking visible ligands.
Why: The user-facing ligand count was misleading because the interactive heatmap payload is a reduced panel, not the full run ligand universe, and exposing the full embedded row set at once degraded row-label rendering enough to break label-driven interactions.
Impact: `pilotstudy` now shows only target/threshold/selection state in the summary, no longer advertises the internal 602-row heatmap panel as though it were the full run, and retains working label-click sorting in the live browser.
Next: If the report should eventually expose full-run ligand coverage, the next step is to decide whether that should come from a different source artifact than `heatmap_input.csv` rather than reusing the reduced interactive panel count.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Restore Heatmap Label Sorting | Owner: Codex
Change: Rebound Clustergrammer row/column label click handling during interaction refresh so ligand-row clicks again reorder target columns and PDB-column clicks again focus a single column while reordering ligand rows.
Why: The label-click helper still existed after the live-analysis refactor, but it was never invoked after render, leaving both row and column labels inert in the browser.
Impact: Heatmap label sorting/reset behavior is restored for `pilotstudy`; cell clicks remain inert and the summary again reflects active ligand-sort or PDB-focus state correctly.
Next: If further interaction work is added, keep label click binding in the same post-render refresh path and verify it in Chromium, because static HTML inspection alone did not expose this regression.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Preserve Heatmap Reorder In Clustergrammer Init | Owner: Codex
Change: Changed the non-dendrogram Clustergrammer init mode from alphabetical ordering to `rank` ordering so the wrapper’s reordered `row_nodes` and `col_nodes` remain visible after render; verified browser-side that ligand clicks now visibly reorder PDB columns and PDB clicks visibly reorder ligand rows while preserving single-column focus.
Why: The wrapper was rebuilding the matrix in the intended custom order, but Clustergrammer was immediately overriding it with `alpha` at init. A first attempt to use `ini` exposed a v1.19.5 library quirk: `ini` is listed as supported but the corresponding order arrays are never built, causing init failure. Using `rank` is safe because the wrapper already rewrites `node.rank` sequentially on the reordered payload.
Impact: The visible heatmap ordering now matches the summary state again on regenerated `pilotstudy` reports, with no change to cell inertness, hover behavior, or target counts.
Next: If future analysis views need different ordering semantics, keep the wrapper-owned payload order and drive Clustergrammer through rewritten node ranks rather than `alpha` or `ini`.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Heatmap Sort Direction Fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Reversed the wrapper-assigned Clustergrammer `rank` and `rankvar` values so the first item in the wrapper’s sorted row/column lists renders first on screen, restoring left-to-right descending target order for ligand clicks and top-to-bottom descending ligand order for PDB clicks.
Why: Clustergrammer’s `rank` display order for this init path was visually inverted relative to the wrapper’s already-sorted payload, so the highest-scoring items were appearing on the far right or bottom even though the underlying comparator was descending.
Impact: Regenerated `pilotstudy` reports now render the best-scoring targets on the left after ligand clicks and the best-scoring ligands at the top after PDB clicks; tie handling still follows the existing stable-order fallback.
Next: If users want a different secondary tie-break than the current stable fallback, add an explicit tie policy in the wrapper comparator rather than adjusting Clustergrammer ranks again.
Date: 2026-03-08 | Task: Heatmap Sort Direction Regression Coverage | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a targeted Clustergrammer HTML regression test that locks the descending score comparator and the reversed `rank`/`rankvar` emission used to keep ligand-click sorting left-to-right and PDB-click sorting top-to-bottom.
Why: The sort-direction bug came from a subtle interaction between wrapper ordering and Clustergrammer init behavior, and the existing tests did not explicitly guard the emitted comparator/rank combination that controls visible direction.
Impact: Future template edits that accidentally flip the heatmap sort direction should now fail the focused heatmap HTML pytest file before the regression reaches regenerated reports.
Next: If browser-level regressions recur, add one lightweight Playwright smoke test that clicks one row and one column in a generated fixture and checks the first rendered labels, instead of relying only on emitted-JS string assertions.
Date: 2026-03-09 | Task: Heatmap Analysis Figure Interpretability | Owner: Codex
Change: Reworked the bottom analysis panel so each tab has its own title, subtitle, and scoped controls; replaced the unlabeled histogram with a ranked view; added explicit scatter axis labels and explanatory notes; and added regression assertions that lock the new labels and the removal of the ambiguous histogram controls.
Why: The previous analysis panel mixed scatter and histogram controls into one unlabeled button cluster, the scatter plot had no axis labels, and the histogram was not scientifically interpretable for the intended workflow.
Impact: `pilotstudy` now renders a labeled scatter view, a labeled ranking view, and a better-explained network view in the portable HTML. The bottom figures should be understandable without guessing what each panel or button means.
Next: If users want deeper quantitative summaries later, the next extension should be a small downloadable table of the currently ranked ligands or targets instead of adding more visual controls.
Date: 2026-03-09 | Task: Heatmap Target Organization Modes | Owner: Codex
Change: Added target organization metadata to the main heatmap payload (`protein_family`, `pathway_memberships`, `primary_display_pathway`) and a new `Target organization` UI with `None`, `Protein family`, and `Pathway` modes. The client now groups visible target columns by family or deterministic primary Reactome pathway without duplicating columns, and target hover/ranking views expose the same family/pathway metadata.
Why: The report needed a second organization method beyond per-PDB ordering so targets can be inspected by broader biological context. Family provides a stable one-to-one grouping, while pathway provides a many-to-many annotation with a deterministic display block for the main heatmap.
Impact: `data/pilotstudy/report.html` now ships grouped target organization controls and richer per-target metadata. Pathway grouping uses cached/live Reactome mappings when available and falls back to `Unassigned` without blocking report generation. Family grouping reuses the repo’s YAML-driven protein class rules from `chemdb/protein_names.yaml` via `druggability_evaluation`.
Next: If users want stricter family taxonomy later, the next step is to promote the current YAML-driven class inference into a dedicated report-side classifier so the heatmap and other analysis outputs share one canonical target-family mapping.
Date: 2026-03-09 | Task: Heatmap Organization Hover Cleanup | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed pathway text from all target-facing hover surfaces while keeping pathway mode available for organization, and added stable organization color cues to the block strip, visible target labels, and target nodes in the auxiliary network view.
Why: Pathway membership was useful for grouping but too noisy in hover, and the existing organization mode needed stronger visual cues so users could quickly see which visible PDBs belonged to the same family or pathway block.
Impact: `pilotstudy` target hover now shows family-level context without pathway clutter, and organization mode is easier to read visually through color-linked grouping rather than summary text alone.
Next: If users still want stronger grouping cues, the next step is a lightweight top-axis family/pathway band drawn directly over the Clustergrammer column region instead of relying only on the external strip plus label coloring.
Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Heatmap ADME and Safety Organization Modes | Owner: Codex
Change: Added local target-annotation grouping support for `ADME` and `Safety`, extended the heatmap organization payload with `adme_category`, `safety_buckets`, and `primary_display_safety`, added two new organization modes to the Clustergrammer report, and rendered separate ADME/Safety summary panels above the family/pathway controls. Target hover now exposes ADME and Safety annotations instead of pathway text.
Why: The report needed two additional biologically meaningful organization modes beyond family/pathway, but the implementation had to remain portable and useful without relying on live external queries at HTML render time.
Impact: `pilotstudy` can now group targets by ADME or broad safety buckets using a checked-in local annotation catalog plus optional local overrides, while preserving the existing PDB-focus and ligand-sort interactions.
Next: If broader target coverage is needed later, populate `pathways/cache/target_annotation_overrides.yaml` from a separate refresh script backed by Open Targets target-safety exports rather than adding live network fetches to normal report generation.
Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Tighten Maintained Runtime Boundary Wave 1 | Owner: Codex
Change: Rewired active maintained modules off root wrapper imports: pdb_fixer.py, ph_ensemble.py, src/docking/run_vina.py, src/docking/docking_ledock.py, src/docking/docking_subrun_finalize.py, src/docking/pose_validation.py, src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_cnn.py, src/prep_ligands/metabolite_resolver.py, and src/docking/docking_single_ligand_mode.py now use src owner modules directly for config, boolean coercion, score I/O, decoy annotation, and run-manifest helpers. File-scoped mypy surfaced and resolved localized pre-existing typing debt in the touched modules. The remaining wrapper-import users in this surface are the already-excluded legacy-maintenance modules capture_pose.py and dedup_sdfs.py.
Next: Continue with a second maintained-boundary wave only if needed, focusing on active root scripts and report/runtime modules that still blur ownership, but keep benchmark_mode.py quarantined unless it is explicitly reactivated.
Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Tighten Maintained Runtime Boundary Wave 2 | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved protein_functions.py, record_data.py, and druggability_evaluation.py behind src/docking owners (active_site_detection.py, ligand_metrics.py, and druggability_runtime.py). Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Trim Manifest And Postrun Runtime Owners | Owner: Codex
Change: Extracted non-mutating support logic out of src/cli/run_manifest_runtime.py into src/cli/run_manifest_support.py and extracted self-contained parse/path/scorch helper logic out of src/cli/postrun_hooks_runtime.py into src/cli/postrun_hooks_support.py. The runtime modules now retain orchestration and public entrypoints while importing shared support helpers from the new owner modules.
Why: Both runtime files had grown into mixed owner modules that combined support utilities, environment parsing, and orchestration in one place. Splitting the pure support layer reduces edit surface, makes ownership clearer, and lowers the chance of future regressions when touching runtime policy.
Impact: Behavior should remain unchanged. Validation caught and fixed one leftover run-manifest helper reference (_parse_int) after the split. The postrun and manifest public APIs remain in the same runtime modules.
Next: After the current benchmark snapshot is superseded by a fresh rerun on this code, the next high-value maintained hotspot is either artifact_retention.py or rescoring_scorch.py; both are still large runtime owners with mixed policy/orchestration responsibilities.
Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Evidence-Backed Safety Organization Mode | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis/reporting/target_safety_evidence.py` plus `analysis/cli/refresh_target_safety_cache.py` to build a local `pathways/cache/target_safety_aggregated.json` from Open Targets target safety and approved-drug adverse events. Updated `analysis/reporting/target_annotation_groups.py` to prefer the cached safety profile, demote the old YAML safety rules to low-confidence fallback/manual override behavior, and emit `safety_confidence`, `safety_sources`, `safety_bucket_scores`, and `safety_evidence_summary`. Updated the Clustergrammer report hover/panels to expose those new fields.
Why: The prior safety mode was only a local heuristic catalog. The report needed a more reliable, provenance-aware safety organization path without adding live network calls to normal HTML generation.
Impact: When the cache is refreshed, Safety grouping is now evidence-backed and the report can distinguish high-confidence Open Targets-derived calls from low-confidence heuristic fallbacks. Existing ADME, family, pathway, ligand-sort, and PDB-focus behavior remains unchanged.
Next: If broader drug-evidence coverage is needed later, extend the refresh CLI with additional curated imports such as OnSIDES or DrugCentral rather than reintroducing target-name keyword classification as a primary source.
Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Trim Artifact Retention And SCORCH Runtime Owners | Owner: Codex
Change: Extracted support logic out of src/post_docking/artifact_retention.py into src/post_docking/artifact_retention_support.py and extracted decoy/stage/parallel support logic out of src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch.py into src/post_docking/rescoring/rescoring_scorch_support.py. The runtime files now keep orchestration and public CLI flow while importing the extracted support layer.

Impact: Behavior should remain unchanged. Validation caught and fixed stale artifact-retention support imports after the split. No scoring or retention policy changes were introduced.
Next: Once the newest snapshot is validated with a single fresh -bench2 -fast run, the next maintained hotspot is whichever remains larger and more policy-heavy between docking_ligands.py and docking_gnina.py.
Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Consolidate Rescoring Prefix Helpers | Owner: Codex

Why: The rescoring stack still carried identical decoy-prefix and test-mode normalization logic in multiple maintained modules, which increased drift risk after the recent SCORCH support splits.

Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Fix pH-Only DUD Eval Manifest Filtering | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated analysis/dud_eval_core/orchestrate.py so manifest-driven target discovery drops blank-ph manifest entries when the same pdb_id/variant also has explicit pH-scoped entries.
Why: run manifests were carrying both HOLO|base and HOLO|pH* protein entries, which caused DUD-eval summaries and post-docked summaries to emit duplicate blank-pH rows for pH-only benchmark runs.
Impact: DUD-eval summaries for pH-only runs now emit only the explicit pH targets; manifest semantics remain unchanged for runtime status tracking.
Next: Keep run-manifest overall docking writes under review, because the upstream manifest still records base entries alongside pH entries in single-process runs.

Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Split Docking Subrun Finalize Support | Owner: Codex
Change: Extracted the post-engine finalize support layer from src/docking/docking_subrun_finalize.py into src/docking/docking_subrun_finalize_support.py and kept the runtime file focused on orchestration and public finalize flow.
Why: docking_subrun_finalize.py mixed engine follow-up lifecycle handling, score/report generation, screenshot logic, consensus follow-up, and manifest success updates in one owner, which made changes risky.
Impact: Behavior should remain unchanged; the finalize path is easier to reason about and future engine-specific post-processing can move into the support layer without growing the runtime owner again.
Next: Keep the next docking split focused on whichever remaining runtime owner still mixes execution policy with report shaping, rather than adding more support logic back into finalize.
Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Split Rescore Reranker Support | Owner: Codex
Change: Extracted CSV/input discovery, shared row ordering, typed pick containers, and consensus chunk-union support from src/post_docking/rescoring/rescore_reranker.py into src/post_docking/rescoring/rescore_reranker_support.py while keeping rerank orchestration and scoring logic in the runtime owner.
Why: rescore_reranker.py mixed identity normalization, CSV discovery, row I/O, field ordering, and rerank math in one file, which obscured the real reranking policy surface.
Impact: Behavior should remain unchanged; reranker support concerns are isolated and the runtime file now better reflects policy and output shaping rather than generic CSV plumbing.

Date: 2026-03-11 | Task: Biology-First Safety Bucket Tuning | Owner: Codex
Change: Reworked target safety scoring so the primary safety bucket is chosen from direct target liabilities before propagated drug adverse events, tightened the safety ontology with source-aware strong/weak/exclude rules, added primary/secondary/direct/drug safety fields plus example events to the heatmap payload, and added adverse-event search/highlight in the report UI.
Why: Broad propagated drug adverse events were overpowering biologically appropriate target liabilities, causing misleading primaries such as progesterone receptor grouping under hepatotoxicity instead of endocrine.
Impact: Safety organization is more conservative and interpretable; direct biology anchors grouping while propagated drug AE evidence remains visible as secondary hover/search detail without changing the underlying heatmap interactions.
Next: Refresh the target safety cache for production runs, inspect representative endocrine/cardiac/hepatic targets in regenerated reports, and tighten the bucket map further if common broad AE terms still over-assign secondary buckets.

Date: 2026-03-12 | Task: Heatmap Robustness Validation Discovery And Exports | Owner: Codex
Change: Added report-side coverage/validation metadata, a full-run discovery panel, analyst workflow exports/state save-load, target safety drift tracking during cache refresh, and opt-in Playwright browser regression coverage for the interactive heatmap.
Why: The heatmap had accumulated repeated browser-only regressions and the report was still ambiguous about reduced interactive coverage versus full-run discovery coverage, which made analysis less reproducible and harder to trust.
Impact: Generated reports now expose cache freshness, unresolved mappings, bucket drift, and reduced-vs-full coverage directly in the HTML; analysts can export current views/rankings/evidence and share state via URL hash or JSON. Browser interaction coverage exists but remains opt-in in restricted environments.

Date: 2026-03-12 | Task: Heatmap Public-Facing Discovery Header | Owner: Codex

Extended report metadata with a generated timestamp and added regression coverage for the new search/state UI.
Why: The report had become powerful but still assumed expert prior knowledge of the heatmap interactions. Public-facing use needed a clearer entry path, more obvious navigation, and faster target/ligand discovery without restructuring the site into multiple pages yet.
Impact: `pilotstudy` now opens with a navigable discovery surface rather than a bare heatmap. Search and quick actions drive the existing heatmap behaviors instead of introducing a parallel model, and state remains shareable via URL hash/JSON.
Next: The next productization chunk should focus on trust/onboarding content or accessibility polish rather than adding another large analysis mode, so the new discovery surface is interpretable to first-time users.

Date: 2026-03-12 | Task: Heatmap Ligand Organization Modes | Owner: Codex

Date: 2026-03-12 | Task: Massinstall PDB Query Default Alignment | Owner: Codex

Why: The user wanted `massinstall --pdb_query` to behave like a thin wrapper around `pdb_query.py` defaults rather than silently inheriting config-driven query knobs.
Impact: Operators must now pass `--genes-file` explicitly and use CLI flags for query-selection overrides; output/install config keys continue to work.
Next: Use `PYTHONPATH=. python massinstall.py --pdb_query --genes-file <file> --dry-run` to validate the intended panel before running installs.
Date: 2026-03-12 | Task: Massinstall Direct CLI Import Fix | Owner: Codex

Why: Direct script execution was failing with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'config'`, which forced a `PYTHONPATH=.` workaround despite the intended root-script usage.
Impact: `massinstall.py` now works as a direct repo-root script without extra Python path setup; query/default behavior from the earlier change is unaffected.

Date: 2026-03-15 | Task: Massinstall PDB Query Gene Source Fallback | Owner: Codex

Why: User workflow requires `python massinstall.py --pdb_query` to run without explicitly passing a genes CSV path every time.
Impact: `--pdb_query` now resolves genes source with precedence `--genes-file` > `PDB_QUERY_GENES_FILE` > repo default pilot CSV; it errors only if none of these sources exist.
Next: If you want a different default panel, set `PDB_QUERY_GENES_FILE` in `config.txt` and run `python massinstall.py --pdb_query --dry-run` to confirm selected IDs before install.

Date: 2026-03-13 | Task: Portable Heatmap Bootstrap Regression Fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Fixed standalone report startup failures in the generated heatmap client by removing stale helper references from the discovery/highlight layer, avoiding a report-extras `setAnalysisTab` override recursion, and adding regression coverage for the missing helper names plus browser-side page errors.

Impact: Generated reports now boot cleanly as standalone files again; browser regressions caused by report-extras/core-template naming drift should be caught earlier by the tightened HTML and Playwright coverage.

Date: 2026-03-15 | Task: Heatmap Accessibility and Usability Polish | Owner: Codex
Change: Upgraded heatmap interaction accessibility by adding delegated keyboard activation (`Enter`/`Space`) and focusability for row/column labels, visible focus rings across controls, stronger active/reset styling, and a colorblind-safer organization palette for target/ligand grouping; tooltip rendering was refactored to key/value rows with improved readability and less fragile hide timing.
Why: Existing interactions were pointer-centric with weak focus indication and lower-contrast grouping colors, which made exploration harder for keyboard users and less robust under accessibility constraints.
Impact: Generated reports now support keyboard-driven row/column sorting/collapse interactions, clearer active-state affordances, and improved contrast/readability in organization and tooltip UI while preserving existing sort semantics.
Next: Add an always-on accessibility smoke to Playwright (non-optional env) to assert keyboard activation and focus-visible behavior on a real emitted report fixture.
Date: 2026-03-20 | Task: Discovery vs Heatmap Control Separation | Owner: Codex
Change: Reworked Discovery panel layout so controls are grouped above the discovery table (`table controls` -> `presets` -> `annotation filters`) and converted Discovery org controls/chips/quick actions to discovery-table-only behavior (no direct mutation of heatmap organization mode or cross-panel jumps).
Why: Users reported overlapping/duplicative controls between heatmap and discovery sections, inconsistent control placement, and quick-action behavior that felt disconnected from the table they were meant to control.
Impact: Heatmap organization controls now own heatmap organization exclusively, while discovery controls own discovery filtering/presets exclusively; active-state reporting now includes discovery annotation state separately.
Next: Add a dedicated browser test that clicks each discovery org/preset control and asserts heatmap `targetOrganizationMode` remains unchanged unless a heatmap org button is clicked.

Date: 2026-03-20 | Task: Distributed Resume Array Auto-Detection Fallback | Owner: Codex
Change: Added SLURM metadata fallback in distributed context resolution so array tasks auto-enable slurm_array mode when ATLAS_DISTRIBUTED_MODE is missing, while preserving explicit env-level off behavior.
Why: Resumed runs on other machines were entering single-process mode per task despite SLURM array metadata, causing duplicate node work and reduced throughput.
Impact: Multi-task SLURM resumes now preserve distributed chunk assignment even when wrapper/env propagation omits ATLAS_DISTRIBUTED_MODE; explicit env off still disables distributed mode.
Next: Validate on a resumed SLURM run and confirm logs report distributed.mode enabled=true with distinct task_id values and no duplicated workload startup across tasks.

Date: 2026-03-20 | Task: Cross-machine distributed resume unblock (`bench2_dist_verify_20260320_b`) | Owner: Codex
Change: Normalized resume-selected protein IDs in `main.py` before INPUT_DIR matching so manifest tokens like `BNJS|HOLO` resolve to canonical IDs (`BNJS`), and adjusted `src/cli/planner_validation.py` chunk verification to degrade (not hard-fail) when a stage1 chunk marker is missing but summary-based coverage checks are available; still hard-fails when both marker and docking summary are missing.
Why: Resume simulation previously failed early with `No requested proteins found` due unnormalized resume tokens, and interrupted distributed chunks were repeatedly failing on `missing_stage1_chunk_marker` before score-based verification could proceed.
Impact: Resume now starts correctly across 4 simulated nodes for this run ID and progresses with completed chunk results without re-triggering the prior marker failure signature.
Next: Let the single active resume instance finish, then finalize per-node completion/throughput validation from distributed chunk results.

Date: 2026-03-20 | Task: Distributed chunk failure tolerance + read_chunk_result signature fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated `collect_terminal_chunk_failures` in `src/cli/distributed_chunk_runtime_helpers.py` to call `read_chunk_result` with keyword-only `chunk_id=` and to catch/log read exceptions instead of aborting the variant loop.
Why: A positional call caused `TypeError: read_chunk_result() takes 2 positional arguments but 3 were given`, and the crash could terminate distributed variant execution.
Impact: Fresh 4-node bench2-fast run (`bench2_dist_verify_20260320_c`) completed chunk execution without the `read_chunk_result` crash; all planned chunks wrote terminal `completed` results (`152/152`) and no cross-task duplicate chunk or ligand ownership was detected.
Next: Investigate throughput-integrity strict postrun mismatch (`missing_docking_unexplained`) that caused task0 exit_code=2 despite full chunk completion.

Date: 2026-03-20 | Task: Distributed Idle-Node Mitigation (Auto Dense Routing + Barrier Fail-Open) | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated `src/cli/distributed_mode_policy.py` auto selection to route dense medium and small distributed runs to `distributed_combo_hybrid` (`auto_medium_dense_tasks`, `auto_small_dense_tasks`) and updated `src/cli/run_lifecycle.py` scorch barrier handling with `ATLAS_SCORCH_BARRIER_TIMEOUT_SEC`, `ATLAS_SCORCH_BARRIER_POLL_SEC`, and fail-open default unless `ATLAS_SCORCH_BARRIER_STRICT=1`.
Why: Per-protein sharding in dense runs and long scorch barrier waits caused idle nodes and delayed completion, especially on cross-machine resumes.
Impact: Auto mode now enables chunk-level ligand distribution when tasks outnumber workable protein granularity; scorch drain no longer blocks retention for long periods by default. Verified in simulation (`bench2_dist_fix3_20260320`) that all four tasks enter combo-hybrid and all four tasks claim chunks with no cross-task duplicate chunk IDs in claims.
Next: Run a full uninterrupted multi-node bench2 resume with strict postrun checks and confirm final per-task ligand counts plus zero duplicate rows in post-scored consensus outputs.
Date: 2026-03-20 | Task: bench2 distributed resume hardening | Owner: Codex
Change: Chunk-mode verification failures now classify missing stage1 marker + missing docking summary as retryable, chunk-mode failures no longer write global per-protein failed logs on non-terminal attempts, and chunk failure reason/retryable metadata is propagated into distributed chunk result payloads; added focused regression tests for retry-window and terminal exhaustion behavior.
Why: Interrupted/distributed resumes were surfacing false unsafe signals and global failures from non-terminal chunk retries, masking true terminal issues and reducing throughput.
Impact: Resume behavior is safer for interrupted chunk work; non-terminal retries stay chunk-local and terminal failure accounting remains at chunk terminal points. Existing behavior for true terminal failures is preserved.
Next: Keep monitoring `bench2_dist_resume_hardening_20260320`-style runs for any terminal chunk failures with `chunk_reason` payloads and extend tests to assert failed payload fields directly in claim-loop integration tests.
Date: 2026-04-09 | Task: DUD Eval Class Aggregate Macro Summary Rewrite | Owner: Agent
Change: Reworked `analysis/dud_eval_core/orchestrate.py` class aggregates for docking, consensus, and post-docked SCORCH to aggregate from per-target metrics instead of pooled ligand score lists; class rows now emit macro means plus per-metric `std`, `median`, and `iqr`, and class plot output changed to `metric_boxplots.png` with box/whisker summaries. Added `chemdb/tests/test_dud_eval_class_aggregates.py` regression coverage for macro EF/stat behavior across all three modes.
Why: Pooled ligand-level class metrics overweight larger targets and assume cross-target score comparability, which is not the standard or defensible default for docking benchmark class summaries.
Impact: Class aggregate `metrics.tsv`, `class_generation_report.tsv`, and `summary_macro.tsv` now carry macro-target class metrics and appended spread statistics; downstream consumers expecting the old pooled class plots (`ef_curve.png`, `roc.png`, `pr.png`, `score_hist.png`) must switch to `metric_boxplots.png`.
Next: Regenerate dud-eval outputs for any runs where class aggregate tables or images are consumed and update any downstream tooling that hardcodes the previous class-plot filenames.
Date: 2026-04-12 | Task: DUD Eval Cross-Class Metric Boxplots | Owner: Agent
Change: Extended `analysis/dud_eval_core/orchestrate.py` to emit per-mode overall class-comparison boxplots under `class_aggregates/<mode>/overall_by_metric/`, with one plot per ranking metric (for example `ef_at_1pct_by_class.png`, `roc_auc_by_class.png`) and one box per protein class built from target-level class members. Updated `chemdb/tests/test_dud_eval_class_aggregates.py` to cover the new cross-class plot artifacts.
Why: Per-class boxplots showed metric distributions within one class, but there was no direct visual comparison across classes for a given metric.
Impact: DUD eval now writes additional cross-class boxplot PNGs for docking, consensus, and post-docked SCORCH modes; downstream consumers can compare class distributions metric-by-metric without aggregating plots manually.
Next: Re-run dud eval for benchmark runs that feed reports and update any report/gallery tooling that should expose the new `overall_by_metric/*.png` outputs.
Date: 2026-04-25 | Task: Open Source Readiness Cleanup | Owner: Codex

Date: 2026-04-26 | Task: Bench2 Post-Run DUD Eval and Heatmaps | Owner: Codex
Change: Hardened post-run DUD eval detection to fall back to manifest command metadata, enabled Vina fallback for automatic run reports, added best-effort static PNG heatmap generation for `heatmap_input*.csv`, and fixed manifest summary accounting for pH-ensemble runs with stale `base` placeholders.

Date: 2026-04-26 | Task: Public CLI and Root Script Reorganization | Owner: Codex
Change: Added the installable `atlas` console script, updated README/config/docs to advertise `atlas` as the public entrypoint, moved legacy root Python helpers into `tools/`, `analysis/`, `src/cli/`, `src/docking/`, and `src/protein_prep/`, and left only `main.py` plus `sitecustomize.py` at repo root.
Why: Open-source users expect a package-style CLI instead of running miscellaneous root scripts directly; root-level scripts also made it unclear which interfaces were supported.

Date: 2026-04-27 | Task: Publication BYOL Install Rule Guardrail | Owner: Codex

Why: Publication readiness review highlighted that the repo should preserve a clear compliance posture where users obtain licensed third-party tools independently.

Date: 2026-04-27 | Task: Core/Optional Env Split and Public Smoke Fixture | Owner: Codex
Change: Reverted the prior `AGENTS.md` policy insertion; added `environment.core.yml` plus `environment.optional.yml`; updated `tools/install_atlas_dev.sh` to default to core env, allow optional overlay installs, and accept `ATLAS_ENV_FILE`; added `tools/public_smoke_check.sh`; updated `README.md` and `docs/external_tools.md` with BYOL wording, verify-tools success criteria, and smoke-fixture instructions; and added a `cli-public-smoke` CI job in `.github/workflows/quality.yml` using micromamba + `environment.core.yml`.

Date: 2026-04-27 | Task: Expanded Exposure and Tissue-Expression Query Coverage | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended the ligand annotation refresh to query openFDA drug labels for existing exposure fields (`cmax_um`, `fraction_unbound_plasma`, `free_cmax_um`) and added a Human Protein Atlas target-expression cache plus refresh CLI for existing target-expression fields.
Why: The hypothesis table should fill Free Cmax and Tissue expression from external public databases where available without adding new report categories or invented values.
Impact: Regenerated `data/pilotstudy/report.html` now has HPA tissue-expression metadata for all 36 target columns. openFDA label parsing improved pilot Free Cmax coverage to 24/602 ligands; many FDA labels do not expose parseable numeric Cmax, and openFDA rate limits require slower or resumed refreshes for side-effect-only backfills.
Next: For higher ligand exposure coverage, add a vetted PK table/source (for example a curated PK-DB/DrugCentral-style extract) rather than trying to infer Cmax from labels that do not report it.
Date: 2026-04-28 | Task: SPD Supplementary Exposure Ingestion | Owner: Codex

Why: openFDA label parsing leaves many ligands without numeric exposure values; SPD provides curated Cmax/PPB for marketed drugs and can fill existing fields without introducing new categories.
Impact: `analysis/cli/refresh_ligand_side_effect_cache.py` now accepts `--spd-supplement-xlsx` and prefers SPD exposure values when a ligand name matches query/display identifiers. Side-effect collection logic is unchanged.
Next: Run cache refresh with the SPD workbook path and regenerate `pilotstudy` report to quantify updated Free Cmax coverage and confirm hypothesis table completeness.

Date: 2026-04-28 | Task: Publication Readiness Cleanup Phases 0-10 | Owner: Codex

Date: 2026-04-29 | Task: Atlas analysis pipeline | Owner: Agent

Date: 2026-04-29 | Task: Smoke Performance and DUD Regression Triage | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed distutils/setuptools startup from config bool parsing, deferred distributed/docking/RDKit/Biopython-heavy imports off the main entrypoint path, and changed consensus `is_decoy` output to classify rows by ligand prefix instead of DUD run mode.

Date: 2026-04-30 | Task: post_BIGprofessional2 Integrity Failure | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved deferred artifact retention out of scheduler/SCORCH shutdown and into a final post-run step after DUD evaluation, master export, reporting, throughput integrity, and rescore verification. Restored the lazy `ElementFixer` selector methods required by Bio.PDB and capped tiny SCORCH score chunks to one core.
Why: `post_BIGprofessional2` completed docking and SCORCH successfully, but retention ran before the post-run evidence consumers and removed the score CSV surface that throughput integrity/master export needed. The fast smoke then exposed a prep regression from the import-laziness change and a SCORCH multiprocessing hang on a two-ligand batch with six threads.
Impact: Post-run checks now see the docking and SCORCH outputs before any disk cleanup runs; retention still honors the SCORCH barrier status and runs after successful final verification. Small SCORCH batches avoid the multi-thread path that can wedge the external scorer.
Next: Re-run the fast smoke and confirm both SCORCH queue completion and throughput integrity pass before treating `post_BIGprofessional2` as a real docking regression.

Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Install MMGBSA AmberTools Prefix | Owner: Codex
Change: Installed conda-forge `ambertools 24.8` under `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools/envs/ambertools`, added Atlas prefix auto-discovery across `OVERALL_DIR` ancestors, and verified `MMPBSA.py`, `cpptraj`, `tleap`, `antechamber`, `parmchk2`, and `sander`.
