Change: Added `MMGBSA_PROTOCOL=screening|production`, production default expansion, frame burn-in/window controls, frame/block QC artifacts, replicate uncertainty/outlier reporting, methods JSON output, chemistry review gating, and explicit-solvent external-trajectory hooks.
Why: Users needed a single config line that distinguishes fast screening MM/GBSA from publication-oriented follow-up runs with stronger defaults and fail-fast checks.
Impact: Screening remains compatible with the existing one-frame path. Production now requires MD trajectory frames, at least 3 replicates, and `MMGBSA_CHEMISTRY_REVIEW_STATUS=reviewed`; explicit-solvent mode currently consumes a pre-equilibrated stripped trajectory via `MMGBSA_EXPLICIT_TRAJ_PATH`/`MMGBSA_TRAJIN_PATH`.
Next: Run production protocol on curated hits and review generated QC JSON/SVG/CSV artifacts before using MM/GBSA values in manuscript figures.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: MMGBSA Explicit-Solvent Planning and Pilot Smoke | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `.tar.zst` artifact extraction support, CLI root overrides for report-driven MMGBSA, `POST_DOCKED_DIR` handling in the MMGBSA pipeline, LEaP log error detection, and `docs/mmgbsa_explicit_solvent_plan.md`; ran a one-pose pilot-like smoke on `20260427_163422/TEST/HOLO/pH7_0/stage3`.
Why: Future MMGBSA jobs need to restore retained artifacts and run selected report hits without global config edits, and explicit-solvent work needs a staged plan for solvated system building, equilibration, stripping, and QC.
Impact: The smoke verified report hit selection, pH/variant/stage routing, receptor matching, ligand prep, and LEaP invocation on `actives_test_library_10_2`; it also exposed that retained PDBQT-to-SDF conversion can produce unreliable ligand chemistry, so publication MMGBSA should retain docked SDF/MOL2 poses directly.
Next: Implement the explicit-solvent builder with water/ion tleap scripts, short test MD stages, cpptraj stripping, and chemistry-authoritative pose retention before running full production MMGBSA on selected heatmap hits.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Meeko-First Ligand Prep | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a shared Meeko SDF-to-PDBQT backend, routed bulk SDF ligand prep through it by default, and changed extracted-PDB ligand prep to restore coordinate-preserving SDF chemistry before the same Meeko writer.
Why: Publication-ready Vina workflows treat PDB ligands as coordinate carriers and use chemically authoritative SDF/template inputs for PDBQT parameterization; the old parallel SDF->MOL2->ADT and PDB->MOL2->ADT paths were overcomplicated and harder to install.
Impact: `LIGPREP_WRITER=meeko` is now the default and no longer requires MGLTools/ADT for ligand PDBQT generation. `LIGPREP_WRITER=adt` preserves the legacy Open Babel/MGLTools route as an explicit fallback. Extracted PDB ligands try CCD/template bond-order restoration and fall back to RDKit PDB perception when no template is available.
Next: Run representative FDA/DUD and crystal-redock ligand prep jobs, then add regression tests for Meeko bulk SDF prep and CCD-restored extracted ligands if test edits are in scope.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Publication-Readiness Cleanup Completion | Owner: Codex
Change: Expanded the scoped quality gate, extracted the main-script notification wrapper into `src/cli/main_script_entrypoint.py`, documented runtime artifact policy, removed tracked `chemdb/bench/runs` generated outputs from the index, tightened schema docs, and confirmed release metadata files are present.
Why: The repository cleanup spec required maintained ownership boundaries, credible quality gates, explicit data schemas, and runtime artifacts treated as generated outputs without changing docking or scoring behavior.
Impact: `tools/quality_gate.sh`, the required chemdb tests, and `python main.py --test -fast` pass. Tiny tracked ligand smoke fixtures remain in `prepped_ligands/` because moving them would require protected test/runtime fixture edits; the `atlas2-preprint-v0.1` tag was not created because the worktree is dirty and tagging HEAD would not cite this cleanup state.
Next: Commit the cleanup changes, create the `atlas2-preprint-v0.1` tag on that commit, then migrate the remaining smoke fixtures only after the acceptance tests are updated to read from documented fixture locations.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Smoke Ligand Fixture Migration | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved tracked smoke ligand fixtures from runtime roots into `chemdb/tests/fixtures/`, updated tests and config docs to use the migrated fixture root, and made full-run tests copy fixtures into per-test temp prepped roots before launching the pipeline.
Why: Runtime directories such as `prepped_ligands/` and `extracted_ligands/` should stay ignored generated-output roots, while small deterministic ligand fixtures should be explicit test assets.
Impact: `python main.py --test -fast`, `tools/quality_gate.sh`, and the migrated fixture consumer tests pass. The DUD `test_library_10` payload remains manifest-only in source because the previous usable PDBQT/MOL2 payload was ignored local runtime state rather than a tracked fixture.

Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Retire Ligand ADT Prep | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed the public `LIGPREP_WRITER=adt` ligand-prep switch, stopped `doctor`/`--verify-tools` from requiring Open Babel or `prepare_ligand4.py` for ligand prep, moved the active bulk entry point to `prep_ligands_bulk_meeko.py`, and routed SDF plus extracted-PDB ligand prep through Meeko/restored-SDF writers.
Why: Publication-ready Vina ligand prep should preserve authoritative SDF chemistry and use a single open Meeko writer; the previous SDF->MOL2->ADT and PDB->MOL2->ADT paths duplicated work and made installation harder.
Impact: Active ligand PDBQT generation depends on Meeko/RDKit instead of ADT. Receptor PDBQT preparation still uses the configured MGLTools/ADFRSuite receptor helper. Legacy ADT helper code remains importable for compatibility but is no longer selected by CLI, doctor, or tool verification.
Next: Run representative FDA/DUD and crystal-redock ligand-prep jobs with curated SDF/template inputs, then delete dormant ADT helper modules in a separate breaking cleanup once downstream imports are audited.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Ligand Library Install and Run UX | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a ligand library manager for built-in HMDB and DrugCentral/FDA source SDF downloads, Meeko PDBQT preparation, source/provenance manifests, and library manifests; exposed `atlas ligands sources|fetch|prep|install|run` plus `atlas hmdb` and `atlas fda` install-if-needed run aliases.
Why: Researchers need an easy path from curated public ligand sources to prepared Atlas PDBQT libraries without manually discovering download endpoints or separate prep commands.
Impact: Download/cache state lives under `LIGAND_SOURCE_CACHE_DIR`; prepared libraries still live under existing prepped-ligand roots and are selected through existing `TEST_MODE_ENABLE` tokens. Large upstream libraries can take substantial time/storage, so source manifests record provenance and checksums.

Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Operations Status Dashboard | Owner: Codex

Next: If a pristine local checkout view is required, clean ignored runtime directories in smaller batches outside active runs; handle any future `chemdb` relocation as a dedicated import/package migration with full test coverage.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Root File Declutter Follow-up | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved the FDA mapping CSV to `chemdb/data/`, moved SPR Slurm scripts to `tools/slurm/`, moved optional Clustergrammer NPM metadata to `tools/report_assets/npm/`, moved the repo map to `docs/repo_map/`, added a repo-map generator, removed stale root helper/config-note files, and reduced `sitecustomize.py` to a top-level compatibility wrapper over `src/config/sitecustomize_support.py`.
Why: Root files should be either required project metadata, explicit compatibility entrypoints, or portable config templates. Chemistry mappings, Slurm scripts, report asset dependency pins, generated orientation docs, and machine-local backups each have clearer owner locations.
Impact: Root-level tracked files are now limited to project metadata, install/config templates, `main.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `pytest.ini`, and the required top-level `sitecustomize.py` hook. `pyproject.toml`, `pytest.ini`, and config templates intentionally remain at root for standard Python tooling and documented setup workflows; local `config.txt` backups are ignored.
Next: Keep future root additions behind an owner-directory justification; if `sitecustomize.py` is no longer needed for local test/import bootstrap after packaging changes, remove the wrapper in a dedicated verification pass.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: HMDB Download Smoke | Owner: Codex
Change: Attempted the live HMDB structures download, confirmed the direct URL is blocked by an upstream Cloudflare 403 challenge, added clean CLI error handling for forbidden source downloads, preserved bounded `--limit` preparation in `install_source`, and documented the `--source-sdf` fallback.
Why: The one-command HMDB path should not fail with a raw Python traceback when the upstream provider requires an interactive browser/form download.
Impact: `atlas ligands install hmdb` now exits with an actionable message when HMDB blocks headless download; manually downloaded HMDB SDF/SDF.GZ/ZIP files still flow through the same Meeko prep and manifest path.
Next: If fully automatic HMDB install is required, coordinate with HMDB for a stable non-interactive bulk download endpoint or mirror an approved SDF internally with documented license/citation handling.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Operations Dashboard Upgrade Pack | Owner: Codex
Change: Added deterministic Slurm `squeue`/`sacct` parser fixtures and tests, `atlas status --explain`, completed-run ETA history caching, YAML-backed root-cause rules, status JSON schema docs, and `atlas runs`.
Why: The operations dashboard needed testable scheduler behavior without a local Slurm daemon, clearer operator guidance, calibrated ETA estimates, editable failure buckets, and an easy way to rediscover recent run IDs.
Impact: `atlas status` now writes a compact `manifests/status_eta_history.json` cache while reading completed manifests, and `analysis/config/root_cause_rules.yaml` owns root-cause bucket patterns. Slurm parsing is covered with fixture outputs from documented Slurm command formats; live Slurm cluster validation is still deferred.
Next: Run `atlas status --explain` against an active Slurm array on a real cluster and add more root-cause YAML patterns from accumulated failed-run logs.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Status Browser Dashboard and Root-Cause Tuning | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas status --html [PATH]` to write a standalone browser dashboard from the same status JSON object, expanded dashboard docs/schema/tests for HTML output, and tuned root-cause YAML patterns from structured summaries of failed run `20260502_151018`.
Why: Users need a quick visual operations view without a running web service, and observed failed-run signatures for `pdb2pqr`/`apo-holo` prep failures should classify as receptor-prep issues instead of `unknown`.
Impact: `atlas status --html` defaults to `data/<RUN_ID>/status.html` and embeds the JSON payload for debugging. `analysis/config/root_cause_rules.yaml` now maps `propka_wire`, `pdb2pqr failed`, `validated.pdb2pqr.failed`, `[apo-holo] FAILED`, and failure-log signatures to `receptor_prep`.
Next: Add more YAML signatures as real failed runs accumulate and consider a live auto-refresh HTML mode only after the static status page is used in real operations.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: MMGBSA Ligand Chemistry Gates | Owner: Codex
Change: Added MMGBSA ligand chemistry provenance keys, production-mode input chemistry authority validation, ligand prep metadata for source/provenance, strict publication gating for AM1-BCC versus gas fallback, atom-type/frcmod warning checks, and documented the remaining explicit-solvent workflow gaps.
Why: Publication-oriented MM/GBSA should not treat PDBQT-reconstructed ligand chemistry as authoritative and must preserve ligand protonation, tautomer, stereochemistry, charge, GAFF/GAFF2 parameterization, and LEaP/parmchk2 provenance.
Impact: `MMGBSA_PROTOCOL=production` now requires `MMGBSA_INPUT_CHEMISTRY_AUTHORITATIVE=true` plus reviewed ligand chemistry subgates. Screening mode remains permissive, but `*.mmgbsa_prep.json` records whether a ligand prep passed the stricter publication gate.
Next: Preserve coordinate-matched docked SDF/MOL2 artifacts upstream, add RESP/QM-derived charge support, implement explicit-solvent system building/MD/trajectory stripping, and add per-target water/metal review records.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: MMGBSA Docked Pose SDF Preservation | Owner: Codex
Change: Added Meeko ligand-prep source sidecars and a Vina postprocess hook that writes coordinate-matched docked SDF/MMGBSA pose metadata beside successful docked PDBQT outputs when source SDF provenance is available.
Why: Publication MM/GBSA should consume docked ligand poses with authoritative SDF chemistry rather than reconstructing bond order and charges from PDBQT.
Impact: Future SDF-to-Meeko docking runs can retain `*.ligprep_source.json`, `*.sdf`, and `*.mmgbsa_pose.json` artifacts for report-driven MMGBSA. Legacy or ADT-only runs without sidecars still need regeneration from source SDF/MOL2 chemistry or remain screening-only.
Next: Add post-docked/report selection preference for these authoritative pose artifacts, extend the same provenance path to MOL2 and non-Vina engines, and implement explicit-solvent system building and trajectory stripping.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Expand Automatic Ligand Libraries | Owner: Codex
Change: Added built-in automatic ligand source support for ChEMBL, ChEBI, and COCONUT alongside FDA/HMDB. ChEMBL and COCONUT use lightweight current-release URL discovery before downloading, and CLI aliases now support `atlas chembl`, `atlas chebi`, and `atlas coconut` install-if-needed docking.
Why: HMDB blocks headless scripted bulk downloads; researchers still need fully automatable publication-relevant libraries for bioactive/drug-like compounds, curated chemical entities, and natural products.
Impact: New source libraries prepare through the same Meeko-only path and are selected via source-token subdirectories under `PREPPED_LIGANDS_DIR`. ChEBI automatic download plus limited Meeko prep was smoke-tested; ChEMBL/COCONUT discovery was validated without downloading their large archives.
Next: For full-size production runs, run `atlas ligands install chembl|coconut --limit <N>` first on a machine with enough storage, then run without `--limit` only after reviewing expected size/runtime and publication license/citation requirements.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: MMGBSA Authoritative SDF Selection and RESP Input | Owner: Codex
Change: Simplified docked-pose SDF preservation to default-on Vina behavior without new user-facing config toggles, made report-driven MMGBSA prefer `*.mmgbsa_pose.json` authoritative SDFs, normalized restored retained artifact prefixes during batch extraction, retained ligand source/MMGBSA pose sidecars in archives, and added RESP/QM/precharged MOL2 consumption through the existing ligand charge-method setting.
Why: Report-selected MMGBSA should use coordinate-matched docked SDF chemistry when available, including after archive restore, and publication-mode ligand prep needs a path to consume RESP/QM charges without accepting gas-charge fallback.
Impact: Future Vina/Meeko-SDF runs can flow from retained report artifacts to MMGBSA ligand prep with authoritative SDF selection and precharged MOL2 provenance. Atlas consumes precomputed RESP/QM MOL2 files and runs `parmchk2`, but does not yet perform QM/RESP fitting internally. Non-Vina provenance was intentionally left untouched because those engines are deprecated for this workflow.
Next: Implement explicit-solvent tleap system building, minimization/heating/equilibration/production, cpptraj stripping, and structured per-target water/metal review records.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: MMGBSA Explicit Production Workflow | Owner: Codex
Change: Added explicit-solvent MMGBSA helper support for solvated tleap, minimization/heating/density/equilibration/production `sander` stages, cpptraj solvent/ion stripping, structured ligand review records, and internal AmberTools RESP charge attempts when precharged MOL2 files are unavailable. Production protocol now defaults to explicit-solvent MD while screening stays one-frame/cheap.
Why: Publication-oriented MMGBSA needs solvated ensemble generation, stripped snapshots, auditable water/metal/chemistry review records, and a stronger RESP/QM charge path than AM1-BCC/gas fallback.
Impact: Production runs can now build and run explicit-solvent stages end-to-end from selected report hits, then feed stripped trajectories into the existing MMPBSA runner. Precomputed RESP/QM MOL2 files remain preferred; Mulliken or other fallback charge paths are recorded and blocked by strict publication readiness. Runtime cost is much higher for production mode.
Next: Validate a full real-system production run on curated pilot hits, then add a PDBbind/Wang-2016 benchmark manifest to compare Atlas family-level correlations and errors against experimental affinities.
Date: 2026-05-02 | Task: Target Install and Run Panels | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `src/cli/target_install.py` with built-in target panels, `tools.massinstall`-backed target selection/downloads, selected-target CSV writing, and `atlas_target_install_manifest.json` provenance. Exposed `atlas targets panels`, `atlas targets install`, and `atlas run-panel <panel> --ligands <library>`.
Why: Researchers need the same one-command setup ergonomics for target PDB inputs that ligand libraries now have; direct `tools/massinstall.py --pdb_query` is functional but too developer-facing.
Impact: Target installs now have user-facing CLI wrappers, built-in panels for kinases/GPCRs/ion channels/nuclear receptors/ADME, and provenance manifests recording selected IDs, installed IDs, query settings, and RCSB download URL template. Direct massinstall remains available for compatibility but docs now point users to `atlas targets install`.
Next: Add regression tests when protected test edits are in scope, tune panel gene lists from real research priorities, and consider richer target-quality gates for APO/HOLO suitability and cofactor policy before production panel runs.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Open Protein Prep Cleanup | Owner: Codex
Change: Replaced the default licensed loop-fill branch in receptor cleanup with optional PDBFixer/OpenMM structural repair, centralized protein-prep pH resolution for PDB2PQR/PROPKA, made the pH-ensemble `A2_SKIP_PDB2PQR` gate effective, collapsed repeated ion/metal counting in the active cleanup path, and removed the duplicate standalone `run_prepare_receptor()` call.
Why: Publication-oriented receptor prep should not require MODELLER or Phenix by default, and the previous monolithic path had duplicated diagnostics plus no-op staged wrappers that obscured the actual behavior.
Impact: PDBFixer/OpenMM is now the preferred open-source heavy-atom/missing-residue repair stage when installed; MODELLER and Phenix remain opt-in. PDB2PQR/PROPKA remains the required pH-aware protonation-state path outside explicit pH-ensemble receptor generation.
Next: Add a curated receptor-prep benchmark fixture comparing Atlas-prepared receptors against published prepared-receptor artifacts or a fixed open baseline, then tighten water-retention policy validation around conserved crystallographic waters.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Open Protein Prep Installer Alignment | Owner: Codex
Change: Added OpenMM and PDBFixer to the core conda environment and documented them as part of the legal/open publication stack.
Why: The receptor-prep cleanup made PDBFixer/OpenMM the preferred structural repair path, so fresh installs need those packages without requiring licensed tools.
Impact: New core installs should exercise the open structural-repair branch instead of falling back to copy/MODELLER. Existing environments need an update before this branch is active.
Next: Rebuild or update `docking-env`, rerun the protein-prep smoke, and verify logs show `[pdbfixer] repaired` rather than `[pdbfixer] unavailable`.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Wetlab Gene Target Search | Owner: Codex
Change: Exposed species, taxonomy-id, polymer entity type, resolution, and gene-type/category controls through `atlas targets from-genes`, `atlas targets install`, `atlas run-panel`, direct `tools/pdb_query.py`, and `tools/massinstall.py --pdb_query`.
Why: Wetlab users need one-command target discovery/downloads for custom genes without manually editing gene CSVs or relying on the previous hardcoded human-protein query path.
Impact: Existing commands still default to human protein RCSB searches, but custom installs can now document organism/type filters in `atlas_target_install_manifest.json` and label ad hoc gene sets for category-limited selection.
Next: Add protected-scope regression tests when test edits are allowed and consider organism-specific built-in panels after researchers identify priority species.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Guided Target Gene Query UX | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas targets genes` for reusable `gene,category` CSV generation and `atlas targets guide` for prompted target setup that can write genes only, query candidates, dry-run target install, or download PDBs.
Why: Researchers should be able to stay inside Atlas for gene-list creation and target query setup without remembering `pdb_query.py`/`massinstall.py` flags.
Impact: The guide reuses the same RCSB query/install code and defaults to human protein searches, preserving existing behavior while adding a safer dry-run default for interactive use. Scripted usage can pass `--mode genes|query|dry-run|install`.
Next: When protected tests are in scope, add CLI parser regression coverage for guided prompts and noninteractive guide modes.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: RCSB-Informed Target Query Filters | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `--methods` and `--allow-apo` to `tools/pdb_query.py`, `tools/massinstall.py --pdb_query`, `atlas targets from-genes`, `atlas targets install`, `atlas targets guide`, and `atlas run-panel`; target manifests now record method filters and whether ligand-bound structures were required.
Why: RCSB guidance and docking practice both emphasize filtering by experimental method, resolution/quality, polymer type, and ligand presence. Atlas already handled organism/entity/resolution and ligand-bound ranking; method selection and explicit apo inclusion were the highest-value missing controls.
Impact: Default target discovery now prefers human protein X-ray/EM ligand-bound entries for docking suitability. Users can select NMR or all methods with `--methods`, and can retain apo entries with `--allow-apo`.
Next: Consider adding optional UniProt-only input, sequence-identity de-redundancy, and R-free/R-work quality filters once the target selector is split enough to keep complexity within Xenon limits.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: SCORCH GPU Auto Device Selection | Owner: Codex
Change: Added SCORCH CPU/GPU device planning, TensorFlow GPU probing in the selected SCORCH env, AMD/NVIDIA env-prefix autodetection, optional GPU ID restriction, per-child GPU visibility binding, GPU job caps, and run-manifest device logging.
Why: SCORCH was effectively CPU-only in Atlas even on GPU nodes; AMD support requires ROCm-capable TensorFlow while NVIDIA requires CUDA-capable TensorFlow, so hardware visibility alone is not enough.
Impact: `SCORCH_DEVICE=cpu` preserves CPU behavior, while `SCORCH_DEVICE=auto`, `amd`, or `nvidia` falls back to CPU unless the selected SCORCH env reports usable TensorFlow GPUs. GPU jobs are capped per detected GPU and child processes get bounded GPU visibility; `SCORCH_GPU_IDS` restricts use to a subset of the node.
Next: Run a small SCORCH CPU-vs-GPU smoke on representative completed docking outputs and compare wall time plus output parity.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Open Protein Prep Benchmark | Owner: Codex
Change: Installed OpenMM/PDBFixer into `docking-env`, added `atlas-protein-prep-benchmark`, and made PDBFixer missing-residue building opt-in while keeping missing-atom/nonstandard repair enabled by default.
Why: A 1IEP smoke showed blind missing-residue building can create Meeko/RDKit receptor chemistry failures; the benchmark now tests this sensitivity explicitly with `--add-missing-residues`.
Impact: Default receptor repair is more conservative for docking. Benchmark outputs under `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_smoke` show pH repair works but PDB2PQR/Reduce output can still break Meeko compatibility on 1IEP.
Next: Run the 20-target benchmark and decide whether to add post-PDB2PQR receptor normalization or route Meeko from the PDBFixer pre-protonation structure while auditing pH states separately.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: SCORCH Auto GPU Small-Batch Gate | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `SCORCH_GPU_MIN_LIGANDS` with a default of 512 ligands for `SCORCH_DEVICE=auto`; smaller SCORCH chunks are downgraded to CPU while forced `amd`/`nvidia` still use the requested GPU backend after probing.
Why: AMD ROCm SCORCH smoke timings showed TensorFlow/ROCm startup and repeated Keras model loads dominate small batches, making GPU slower for 4- and 64-ligand checks.
Impact: `auto` now avoids overpaying GPU startup overhead on small rescore chunks while preserving GPU use for larger chunks and explicit user-forced GPU modes.
Next: Benchmark a real large ligand rescore to tune the 512-ligand default if the observed crossover differs on production outputs.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: SCORCH GPU Chunk Sizing | Owner: Codex
Change: Added GPU-aware SCORCH chunk sizing with `SCORCH_GPU_CHUNK_SIZE=1024` and lowered `SCORCH_GPU_MIN_LIGANDS` to 128; GPU mode now bypasses CPU-oriented adaptive chunk caps, tail splitting, and runtime rechunking that would fragment GPU batches.
Why: The previous 128-ligand CPU chunk default and 512-ligand GPU gate meant large total rescoring runs could still be split into chunks too small to use GPU under `auto`.
Impact: `SCORCH_DEVICE=auto` can now use AMD/NVIDIA GPUs for normal large rescoring runs while keeping smoke/tail chunks below 128 ligands on CPU. Slurm/deferred and local rescoring both flow through the same SCORCH subprocess orchestration and inherit the larger GPU chunk size.
Next: Benchmark a production-size rescore and tune `SCORCH_GPU_CHUNK_SIZE` upward if GPU memory has headroom.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Hide SCORCH GPU Batch Defaults | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed `SCORCH_GPU_MIN_LIGANDS` and `SCORCH_GPU_CHUNK_SIZE` from active/example configs while keeping their backend defaults in `scorch_device.py`.
Why: Normal users should only need `SCORCH_DEVICE` and optional `SCORCH_GPU_IDS`; GPU batch heuristics are implementation defaults, not routine config tuning.
Impact: Existing advanced overrides still work if injected through environment/runtime config, but the visible config surface stays simpler.
Next: Decide whether to add a separate expert-only tuning appendix after production-size GPU benchmarks.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Wetlab Target Install Short Form | Owner: Codex
Change: Added positional gene symbols to `atlas targets install` so users can run commands like `atlas targets install BRCA1 BRCA2`; changed the default method filter to `any` while retaining `--methods` for explicit X-ray/EM/NMR filtering.
Why: Routine target installation should not require users to know Atlas' internal option names or repeat default values. Gene-symbol commands are easier for wetlab users and notebook workflows.
Impact: Target discovery still defaults to human protein, ligand-bound entries, a resolution cutoff where RCSB reports resolution, and 10 PDBs per gene, but no longer excludes structures solely by experimental method unless `--methods` is provided.
Next: If users need topic/pathway searches rather than gene lists, add a separate RCSB text/annotation query mode instead of overloading positional genes.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Target Species Aliases | Owner: Codex
Change: Added common organism aliases for target discovery (`human`, `mouse`, `rat`, `zebrafish`, `fly`, `worm`, `yeast`, `ecoli`, common farm/model organisms, and selected pathogens), `--organism`/`--taxon` aliases, `--species any`, and `atlas targets species`.
Why: Wetlab users usually know organism names, not NCBI taxonomy IDs. RCSB target queries still need precise source organism filters, so Atlas now infers the taxonomy ID for common aliases.
Impact: `atlas targets install Trp53 --species mouse` now resolves to `Mus musculus`/`10090`; manifests record the resolved species and taxonomy ID. Unknown species names still pass through, and `--taxonomy-id` overrides inferred aliases.
Next: Consider RCSB text/topic search, direct UniProt input, sequence-identity de-duplication, and ligand/quality presets as the next target-query QoL layer.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Protein Prep Validity Benchmark Expansion | Owner: Codex
Change: Installed Reduce/Probe support, added benchmark validity gates for HET chemistry, metal coordination, termini, Probe contacts, pH propagation, Meeko fallback, and optional restrained minimization.
Why: Publication receptor prep needs explicit validation of the remaining PrepWizard-like gaps rather than implicit success.
Impact: Benchmark now reports hard validity failures and diagnostic gaps; default benchmark avoids slow minimization unless --run-minimize is requested.
Next: Decide whether to make the Meeko PDBFixer fallback part of production prep or keep it benchmark-only, and fix metal loss in the PDB2PQR/Reduce receptor path for metal-containing targets.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Pilot DUD SCORCH parity and report recovery | Owner: Codex
Change: Fixed DUD SCORCH selection for mixed `dud`/`fda_dud` artifact names, regenerated pilotstudy SCORCH outputs, rebuilt `master_rows.csv`, `heatmap_input.csv`, `report.html`, SPD benchmark tables, and the pilot gap audit. Fixed report inline JavaScript emitted by `heatmap_report_client_extras_core_module.py` so the standalone HTML renders instead of staying on the heatmap loader.
Why: DUD score files and pose suffixes used `fda_dud` while the active decoy prefix resolved to `dud`, leaving DUD SCORCH/FDR rows empty. The report then used stale/fallback score surfaces and a client-side syntax error blocked portable HTML rendering.
Impact: Pilot master rows now have nonempty `z_selected` for FDA and DUD rows, DUD-backed FDR fields have nonzero decoy counts, and the inline report renders in headless Chromium. FDR reliability remains false because retained DUD decoy counts per target are below the configured 200-decoy threshold. RDK display coverage still has unresolved rows where the local mapping file has no FDA name.
Next: Before ML training, use the refreshed deduplicated SPD table and keep missing openFDA/free-Cmax values unknown; decide whether to lower/report a pilot-specific FDR reliability threshold or generate more decoy rescoring coverage per target.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Target Query QoL Filters | Owner: Codex
Change: Added direct UniProt target search/install, co-crystallized ligand comp-id filters, sequence-identity de-duplication, a publication quality preset, and `atlas targets search` full-text topic mode.
Why: Wetlab users often start from a UniProt accession, a required cofactor/ligand state, or a broad biological topic rather than a prebuilt CSV; these filters make the RCSB discovery path usable without manual Advanced Search work.
Impact: `atlas targets from-genes`, `atlas targets install`, `atlas run-panel`, `tools/pdb_query.py`, and `tools/massinstall.py --pdb_query` now accept the new filters. Target install manifests record UniProt, ligand, dedupe, and quality settings. Existing gene/panel defaults remain unchanged unless the new flags are supplied.
Next: Validate sequence-grouping behavior against a larger RCSB sample and consider exposing additional RCSB validation metrics once their API attribute coverage is stable.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Default Target Sequence De-Duplication | Owner: Codex
Change: Changed target query de-duplication to default to 90% sequence-identity grouping and added `--no-dedupe` as an explicit opt-out.
Why: Publication-style target panels should avoid silently selecting many near-identical structures unless the user intentionally asks for exhaustive structural variants.
Impact: Gene, UniProt, text, install, guide, and run-panel target searches now return sequence-cluster representatives by default. Users can choose a different cutoff with `--dedupe sequence-identity <100|95|90|70|50|30>` or disable grouping with `--no-dedupe`.
Next: Consider whether panel presets should use stricter defaults, such as 70%, when the goal is target-family diversity rather than per-target structural coverage.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Remove Receptor ADT Requirement | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed prepare_receptor4.py execution from the active receptor PDBQT path and stopped doctor/setup/tool verification/runtime config from requiring MGLTools receptor keys.
Why: Publication-oriented receptor preparation should run from the open Meeko/PDBFixer/PDB2PQR/Reduce stack without requiring ADT/MGLTools.
Impact: Existing configs that contain MGLTools keys still normalize, but receptor prep no longer calls ADT; legacy ligand ADT helper code remains outside this receptor-prep change.
Next: Decide whether cached receptor PDBQT reuse after Meeko failure should remain allowed for smoke-test compatibility or become a hard publication-mode failure.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: HET State and Termini Geometry Benchmark | Owner: Codex
Change: Added Dimorphite-DL to the open environment, added benchmark HET protonation/tautomer enumeration from CCD/RDKit/Dimorphite-DL, added per-chain termini cap planning, and added severe nonbonded geometry clash metrics.
Why: Publication protein prep needs auditable chemistry-state and geometry evidence before automatic receptor chemistry mutations are justified.
Impact: Benchmark outputs now include per-target `het_state_audit.json` and `termini_audit.json`; `structure_valid` now fails complete HET-state enumeration failures and severe geometry clashes.
Next: Fix PDB2PQR/Reduce metal loss and geometry clashes on the invalid benchmark targets, then add Astex/DUD-E prepared-receptor reference comparison as an optional benchmark tier.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Production Meeko Open Fallback | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a production receptor PDBQT fallback that retries Meeko on earlier open intermediates such as the PDBFixer-repaired receptor when the post-PDB2PQR/Reduce receptor fails Meeko parsing.
Why: Removing ADT exposed that post-Reduce receptor output can be invalid for Meeko even when an earlier open repair intermediate is usable.
Impact: `main.py --test -fast` now succeeds without ADT or cached receptor PDBQT; fallback provenance is logged as `Meeko open fallback`.
Next: Decide whether publication mode should prefer the fallback PDBQT while keeping pH-state audit separately, or block until the PDB2PQR/Reduce metal/protonation path itself is Meeko-clean.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: MMGBSA RESP and Wang Benchmark Harness | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas-mmgbsa resp-plan`, `resp-finalize`, and `benchmark-wang2016`. RESP planning writes auditable AmberTools/Gaussian handoff scripts and provenance; finalization consumes completed Gaussian/GAMESS output to write RESP MOL2/frcmod. The benchmark harness downloads/parses the Wang 2016 supplementary DOCX when reachable, writes paper-level reference target metrics, and can compare Atlas MMGBSA CSVs against reference rows.
Why: Publication MMGBSA needs a reproducible RESP/QM charge workflow and a concrete external benchmark to quantify how Atlas production settings differ from a published MMPBSA protocol.
Impact: No new routine config keys. Users can generate reviewed RESP inputs and benchmark artifacts under user-selected output directories, typically `/stor/home/mpg2352/...`. Network download depends on PMC supplement availability; static paper-level target metrics are written even when the supplement cannot be fetched.
Next: Curate a first Wang-family manifest with exact PDB IDs, ligand IDs, retained waters/ions, and experimental affinities, then run a small Atlas production subset to test per-family correlation metrics.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Wang MMGBSA Representative PDB Fixture | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended `atlas-mmgbsa benchmark-wang2016` with `--download-pdbs`, which writes the representative Wang complexes manifest and downloads `1Q8W`, `2J75`, `1VZQ`, `1O2Q`, `1C5Z`, and `1LPZ` to the benchmark output `input_pdbs/` directory.
Why: The Wang full supplementary per-complex output was not directly downloadable in this environment, but the paper explicitly names representative complexes that can support a crystal-pose versus control-redocking MMGBSA comparison.
Impact: Benchmark PDB inputs live outside protected repo `input_pdbs/` and can be used by setting `INPUT_DIR=<benchmark>/input_pdbs`. Full production MMGBSA remains expensive; use control-only docking first to validate receptor/ligand extraction and pose RMSD before launching production MD.
Next: Run Atlas in `NO_LIBRARY_DOCKING` mode on the fixture input directory, then generate report-driven MMGBSA jobs for the extracted crystal/control-redocked ligands.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Outputs Runtime Layout Migration | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved the canonical runtime contract to `outputs/docked/<RUN_ID>/<PDB>/`, `outputs/post_docked/<RUN_ID>/<PDB>/`, `outputs/processed_pdbs/<PDB>/`, `outputs/configs/<RUN_ID>/`, `outputs/manifests/<RUN_ID>/`, `outputs/data/<RUN_ID>/`, and `outputs/logs/`; added shared output path helpers and updated router, CLI status/reporting, rescoring, benchmark, and docs defaults to use the new layout.
Why: Root-level runtime directories made the repository look like a working directory and obscured source ownership; grouping generated artifacts under `outputs/` gives users one visible output surface while preserving run-id/PDB docking parity.
Impact: Fresh runs write to `outputs/*` by default. Several readers keep legacy-root fallbacks for old run artifacts, but new status/report/reporting paths prefer `outputs/*`. Scientific docking/scoring logic and CSV schemas are unchanged.
Next: Run a full benchmark/resume cycle under the new layout and retire legacy-root fallbacks only after no active reports or archived runs depend on them.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Vina-GPU 2.1 AMD/NVIDIA feasibility | Owner: Agent
Change: Downloaded Vina-GPU 2.1 under tools/Vina-GPU-2.1 and built AutoDock-Vina-GPU-2.1 for the local AMD ROCm node. Patched the local Vina-GPU OpenCL loader to exclude NUL terminators from source lengths and to avoid treating ROCm's unused include-path warning as fatal for AMD builds. Verified the bundled 2bm2 single-ligand example on one MI100 via ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0; binary-kernel runtime completed with best affinity around -8.8 kcal/mol and reported AutoDockVina-GPU3 total runtime 1.306 s. A comparable AutoDock Vina CPU smoke on the same example with exhaustiveness 8 and cpu 1 took 41.11 s.
Why: Upstream Vina-GPU 2.1 supports AMD/NVIDIA through OpenCL platform macros, but the checked-in Linux Makefile defaults to NVIDIA/CUDA paths and ROCm rejects the upstream kernel source loading/build flags without small compatibility fixes.
Impact: The local AMD executable works for direct Vina-GPU invocation, but Atlas should not point VINA_EXE at it as a drop-in replacement. Atlas currently launches Vina per ligand, while Vina-GPU's useful mode is receptor/stage batching through ligand_directory/output_directory with explicit backend provenance. Mixing CPU Vina and Vina-GPU results in one unmarked ranking would create reproducibility and review risk.
Next: Add an explicit VINA_DEVICE/VINA_BACKEND path only after implementing a stage-level Vina-GPU batch runner, GPU worker limits, GPU id binding, backend/device manifest fields, and a CPU-vs-GPU score concordance benchmark for the FDA-size workload.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Target Query QoL Filters | Owner: Codex
Change: Added direct UniProt target search/install, co-crystallized ligand comp-id filters, sequence-identity de-duplication, a publication quality preset, and `atlas targets search` full-text topic mode.
Why: Wetlab users often start from a UniProt accession, a required cofactor/ligand state, or a broad biological topic rather than a prebuilt CSV; these filters make the RCSB discovery path usable without manual Advanced Search work.
Impact: `atlas targets from-genes`, `atlas targets install`, `atlas run-panel`, `tools/pdb_query.py`, and `tools/massinstall.py --pdb_query` now accept the new filters. Target install manifests record UniProt, ligand, dedupe, and quality settings. Existing gene/panel defaults remain unchanged unless the new flags are supplied.
Next: Validate sequence-grouping behavior against a larger RCSB sample and consider exposing additional RCSB validation metrics once their API attribute coverage is stable.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Protein Prep Metal Retention Rescue | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `protein_prep.metal_retention.rescue_retained_hets`, wired it into PDB2PQR rescue and post-Reduce receptor cleanup, and expanded metal-site audit output to compare input, cleaned PDB, and receptor PDBQT donor contacts.
Why: PDB2PQR/PROPKA was dropping HET metals; the old rescue appended records after terminal PDB records, so downstream tools could ignore them. Post-Reduce rescue now verifies the retained cleaned receptor still has metals/cofactors before receptor promotion.
Impact: Metal-count benchmark failures are removed for the 10-target open benchmark; remaining invalid structures are geometry/Meeko compatibility failures. Metal donor deltas remain diagnostic because ligand removal can intentionally remove ligand-metal contacts.
Next: Address geometry clashes around retained metals and Meeko rejection for metal-containing receptors; separate retained protein/water/cofactor donor loss from removed co-crystal ligand donor loss in the benchmark sidecar.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Vina-GPU backend and Bench2 comparison | Owner: Agent
Change: Added config-selected Vina-GPU stage batching with AMD/NVIDIA env detection, per-GPU locking, CPU fallback, and throughput-integrity control-row accounting fixes.
Why: Enable GPU docking without mixing CPU/GPU retries and make Bench2 control-only runs pass strict postrun accounting.
Impact: VINA_BACKEND=vina_gpu runs stage batches through Vina-GPU and disables CPU structured retries; tiny control-only workloads show modest pipeline speedup because fixed overhead dominates.
Next: For publication workloads, use one backend per comparison run and benchmark larger ligand batches before enabling broad Vina-GPU use.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Bench2 ligand-count diagnosis | Owner: Agent
Change: Added a narrow ligand-root compatibility fallback so missing Bench2 mappings like `bench_pur2`, `bench_mk01`, and `bench_fabp4` resolve to existing unprefixed roots `pur2`, `mk01`, and `fabp4`.
Why: The Vina-GPU/CPU Bench2 comparison only docked controls because the mapped `bench_*` library directories were absent in this workspace, causing non-control roots to resolve to zero.
Impact: Future Bench2 runs on this checkout should use the full mapped DUD libraries instead of silently falling back to per-PDB control/reference ligands; previous 4-ligand speed comparison is not representative.
Next: Re-run paired CPU and Vina-GPU Bench2 after this fix before drawing performance conclusions.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Canonical Bench2 library roots | Owner: Agent
Change: Removed the temporary `bench_*` to unprefixed DUD library alias fallback and copied canonical `bench_pur2`, `bench_mk01`, and `bench_fabp4` prepped ligand roots from `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas2/code/protein_automation/prepped_ligands/` into this workspace.
Why: Bench2 should only use its explicit mapped `bench_*` libraries; falling back to unprefixed DUD roots defeats the benchmark profile semantics.
Impact: Bench2 now resolves BNJS/BOJG/BNNQ to `prepped_ligands/bench_pur2`, `prepped_ligands/bench_mk01`, and `prepped_ligands/bench_fabp4`, each with 1230 PDBQT ligands.
Next: Re-run paired CPU and Vina-GPU Bench2 with the canonical roots present.

Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Canonical Bench2 CPU vs Vina-GPU timing | Owner: Agent
Change: Ran canonical CPU Bench2 to completion and started the matching one-GPU Vina-GPU Bench2 run; stopped the Vina-GPU run after it exceeded the completed CPU wall time while still in the first Stage1 batch.
Why: The current Vina-GPU integration serializes all stage batches through the first configured GPU id, and the first 1230-ligand Stage1 batch did not complete before CPU finished the entire benchmark.
Impact: CPU Vina completed strict Bench2 integrity (`3690/3690`) in 1144.92s wall; current one-GPU Vina-GPU path is noncompetitive and produced no valid BOJG Stage1 outputs when interrupted.
Next: Do not use Vina-GPU as the default Bench2 backend until GPU scheduling supports true multi-GPU distribution and the Vina-GPU batch completion/valid-output behavior is fixed and rebenchmarked.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Protein Prep Metal-Bound HET Policy Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added benchmark classification for metal-bound HET residues as competitive ligands, structural cofactors, bridging waters, buffer/salts, or unknown structural ligands; `metal_coord_retained` is now policy-aware and a separate raw first-shell donor-retention flag preserves strict donor-count diagnostics.
Why: Redocking receptors should intentionally remove metal-coordinating co-crystal ligands that occupy the docking site, while still failing unexpected loss of structural metal partners.
Impact: Benchmark rows now distinguish expected metal-contact loss, such as 2JDU MFU-Ca donor loss, from unexpected structural HET removal. Outputs include donor-contact counts for retained, expected-removed, and unexpected-removed metal-bound HET residues.
Next: Move the classifier into shared production audit code if the same policy should appear in per-run receptor-prep JSON, then address remaining geometry and Meeko failures around retained metals.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Protein Prep Metal Chemistry Typing Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `protein_prep.metal_chemistry` with heuristic metal formal-charge states, first-shell coordination geometry typing, AutoDock4Zn candidate routing for Zn, and preserve/audit/parameterize routing for non-Zn metals. Wired summaries into the benchmark and production `metal_site_audit.json`.
Why: Metal retention alone does not solve charge, geometry, or force-field treatment. Atlas needs explicit audit fields that separate useful heuristics from unsupported claims of solved PDBQT metal chemistry.
Impact: Benchmark outputs include `metal_chemistry_audit.json` and CSV columns for charge states, geometry counts, treatment counts, AD4Zn candidate count, and parameterization-required count. Production metal audits now include per-stage chemistry rows and a chemistry summary.
Next: Add an optional AutoDock4Zn/ADFR execution path only for eligible Zn sites, and route non-Zn parameterization/minimization through the existing MMGBSA AmberTools machinery rather than inventing generic pseudoatoms.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Pilot Full-DUD FDR and Safety Search Recovery | Owner: Codex
Change: Added full-DUD docking consensus fallback for target FDR when post-SCORCH retained decoys are below threshold, added `fdr_null_source` provenance, propagated FDR fields into `heatmap_input.csv`, and added bounded target `safety_evidence_terms` to report metadata/search.
Why: The DUD library was present at roughly 3.5k decoys per target, but FDR was being calculated from the intentionally retained post-SCORCH subset. Hallucination evidence for DRD2/HTR2A existed in Open Targets cache entries but was dropped from report search because only capped example lists were indexed.
Impact: Pilot `master_rows.csv` and `heatmap_input.csv` now have reliable non-decoy FDR rows backed by 3,548 DUD docking-consensus decoys per target. `report.html` renders standalone and searching `hallucinations` surfaces/highlights 6CM4 and 7WC8 target states. The FDR score field is `consensus_score`, not SCORCH `final_score`; use `fdr_null_source` to distinguish this from post-SCORCH subset calibration.
Next: Resolve the remaining 6,300 row-level unresolved RDK display mappings, improve sparse free-Cmax/openFDA coverage without treating missing exposure as negative, and train ML only from deduplicated SPD drug-target labels with leakage-controlled splits.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Pilot Search Future-Proofing and SPD Coverage Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Changed report target search and safety highlighting to recursively index target metadata values instead of enumerating only known safety fields. Added pilot SPD expected-coverage artifacts at `data/pilotstudy/spd_pilot_expected_coverage.csv` and `.summary.json`.
Why: Future Open Targets, pathway, or safety evidence refreshes can introduce useful fields that should be searchable without another report JavaScript field-list change. SPD label sparsity needed to be separated into assay-panel coverage, drug/target mapping coverage, and exposure-relevant positive counts.
Impact: Rebuilt pilot `report.html` still renders standalone and `hallucinations` finds/highlights 6CM4 and 7WC8 through generic metadata search. The local SPD supplement has 121,097 result rows, 200 assay IDs, 168 assay groups, and only 11 of 28 pilot SPD target genes represented by any SPD assay.
Next: For ML, filter or stratify by `spd_label_status` and target assay coverage; do not treat targets with no SPD assay as assay-backed negatives. Consider extending the SPD importer to emit an explicit assayed-background table from Supplementary Data 1.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Multi-Source External Label Ingestion | Owner: Codex
Change: Added local-first external source registry/cache helpers, joined external-label table generation, Papyrus/ChEMBL-style bioactivity benchmark building, richer SPD/ToxCast label-status fields, and retry/backoff for openFDA refresh requests.
Why: Atlas needs separate assay labels, exposure context, and mechanism evidence from SPD, Papyrus/ChEMBL, ToxCast, SIDER, Open Targets Safety, CTD, and Reactome without treating missing assays as negative evidence or making full analysis depend on live APIs.
Impact: `run_full_analysis` now writes `outputs/analysis/external_labels/atlas_external_labels.csv`, a deduplicated drug-target table, source registry manifest, and source coverage summary when local source files are configured. SPD no-match rows now carry `unknown_no_spd_pair_match` rather than false exposure labels.
Next: Stage current Papyrus/ChEMBL/ToxCast/SIDER/CTD/OpenTargets/Reactome local exports and mapping files, then run `python -m atlas.analysis.cli.build_external_labels --pair-table <pair_table> --config configs/atlas_analysis.yaml` before ML dataset construction.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Sawada PBAS Baseline and Report Statistics | Owner: Codex
Change: Added PBAS ingestion, Atlas drug-target profile construction, Sawada-mode drug-level side-effect baseline modeling, PBAS-vs-Atlas pair/profile comparison, Figure 3-style mechanism network export, and Sawada-vs-Atlas summary-table CLIs. Added report HTML statistical analysis panels for FDR/nulls, enrichment, ablations, matched controls, external coverage, and z_selected interpretation.
Why: Atlas needs a formal Sawada/PBAS baseline while preserving the distinction between drug-level docking-profile side-effect prediction and Atlas pair-level mechanism prioritization.
Impact: New outputs can be generated under `outputs/analysis/pbas`, `outputs/analysis/profiles`, `outputs/analysis/sawada_baseline`, `outputs/analysis/pbas_vs_atlas`, and `outputs/analysis/network_subgraphs`. Existing report HTML now surfaces statistical files when they exist next to the run or under `outputs/analysis`.
Next: Stage real PBAS matrices/mappings, rerun `build_pbas_baseline`, `build_binding_profiles`, and `compare_pbas_atlas`, then regenerate `pilotstudy` report so the new statistical panel is visible in the delivered HTML.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Atlas Doctor Target/Ligand Preflight | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended `atlas doctor` with target/ligand preflight options for raw input PDB presence, prepared receptor/ligand PDBQT syntax, multi-MODEL detection, empty/one-atom files, and Vina config path mismatches.
Why: Wetlab users were seeing raw Vina/Open Babel parse failures that are easier to prevent with an Atlas-level validation report and concrete ligand-prep repair hints.
Impact: `atlas doctor --pdb <ID> --ligands <library>` now validates a bounded sample of prepared ligand PDBQT files before docking while preserving the existing environment doctor output. One-atom and empty PDBQT files fail preflight by default.
Next: Consider wiring this as an optional automatic pre-docking gate after the run launcher has a stable way to distinguish intentionally partial prep states from true input failures.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Atlas Screenshot Command | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas screenshot <RUN_ID>` for active-site PNG rendering from selected docked poses, with `--pdb`, `--ligand`, `--top`, `--stage`, `--without-ligand`, and narrow artifact-archive restore support.
Why: Researchers need a direct way to inspect or export protein-ligand pairs of interest after a run without manually locating archived PDBQT files and invoking PyMOL helpers.
Impact: Screenshots are written under `outputs/data/<RUN_ID>/screenshots/`; pose files are restored from `_artifact_archives/archive_index.json` only when selected outputs are missing. Existing `atlas-cap-driver` and capture_pose rendering remain compatible.
Next: Add protected-scope CLI tests when test edits are allowed, and consider linking generated screenshot paths into run reports/status summaries.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Wang MMGBSA Smoke Benchmark | Owner: Codex
Change: Report-driven MMGBSA now accepts legacy post-docked SDF layout `post_docked/<run>/<pdb>/<pH>/<stage>/file.sdf` in addition to variant-scoped layout. Staged Wang 2016 smoke inputs under `/stor/home/mpg2352/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016/smoke` and completed `1O2Q` crystal/redocked one-frame MMGBSA through antechamber, parmchk2, tleap, cpptraj, and MMPBSA.py.
Why: The fixed receptor-prep path let some Wang controls redock, but the MMGBSA ligand-prep resolver still rejected legacy report-driven paths before topology generation.
Impact: `1O2Q` now has endpoint smoke results in `outputs/post_docked/wang2016_controls_20260503_retry/1O2Q/pH7_0/mmgbsa/mmgbsa_results_summary.csv`. `1LPZ` receptor prep passes but ligand charge preparation blocks on CMB chemistry/SQM and the smoke PDBQT-to-SDF conversion reports over-valent nitrogen, so it needs authoritative curated SDF/MOL2/RESP inputs before a meaningful MMGBSA comparison.
Next: Fix remaining receptor PDBQT failures for `2J75`, `1VZQ`, and `1C5Z`; add pH-tagged control-redock config emission; avoid PDBQT-to-SDF smoke conversion for production by preserving coordinate-matched authoritative SDF/MOL2 from docking.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: FDA Mapping Benchmark Name Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added shared FDA mapping benchmark index `tools/fda_mapping_index.py`, wired benchmark mode and benchmark auto-analysis to it, added NDC-like display-name detection, and generated `docs/fda_mapping_name_audit.tsv`.
Why: FDA ligand-name checks were split across benchmark scripts and quality gates, and benchmark auto-analysis treated missing RDK names as acceptable for expected-name filtering.
Impact: Benchmark details and summary CSVs now expose missing/flagged FDA mapping names via `rdk_name_missing`, `rdk_name_quality_reasons`, `num_rdk_missing_names`, and `num_rdk_quality_flagged`. Current mapping audit flags 525 rows: 481 short alias-like display names and 44 high-confidence truncated display names.
Next: Use the audit TSV to decide which short aliases are acceptable brands/codes versus rows that need mapping enrichment; rerun the FDA name fill tool for confirmed unresolved rows.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: MMGBSA Ligand Chemistry and Pose Provenance | Owner: Codex
Change: Added extracted-crystal ligand source sidecars, a CCD/MCS coordinate-transfer fallback for failed template bond-order assignment, legacy extracted-reference fallback metadata for docked pose SDF preservation, RDKit sanitize blocking for invalid SDFs, and a timeout around antechamber/SQM charge attempts.
Why: Wang controls such as `1LPZ`/CMB exposed two non-receptor blockers: PDBQT-to-SDF smoke reconstruction can create invalid ligand chemistry, and extracted PDB proximity chemistry is not reliable enough for publication MMGBSA when CCD chemistry is available.
Impact: Fresh extracted-control PDBQTs can now carry authoritative source SDF provenance when CCD/MCS restoration succeeds, so Vina docked poses can preserve docked coordinates while reusing curated bond orders. Invalid reconstructed SDFs fail before AmberTools, and long SQM attempts stop with a recorded timeout instead of hanging. `1LPZ`/CMB crystal MCS smoke now reaches one-frame MMPBSA, but it still falls back to gas charges and remains screening-only until RESP/precharged MOL2 is supplied.
Next: Re-run Wang controls after receptor PDBQT fixes land, regenerate legacy extracted ligands so sidecars are present, and provide RESP/QM or reviewed precharged MOL2 for CMB before publication-mode comparison.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Protein Prep Metal Tool Routing | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `protein_prep.autodock4zn` and `protein_prep.metal_parameterization` helpers, benchmark/production sidecars for `autodock4zn_plan.json` and `metal_parameterization_plan.json`, and metal-bound donor protonation review flags in `metal_chemistry_audit.json`.
Why: Metal retention and heuristic charge/geometry typing were visible, but Atlas had no concrete path for eligible Zn AutoDock4Zn maps, no staged open parameterization route for other metal centers, and no explicit audit of metal-bound His/Cys/carboxylate protonation cases.
Impact: Benchmarks now report AutoDock4Zn readiness/missing tools, MCPB.py readiness/missing tools, candidate/site counts, execution status, and protonation review counts. This stages open published workflows without pretending that generic PDBQT or uncurated templates solve metal chemistry.
Next: Install ADFR helper scripts plus `AD4Zn.dat` and AmberTools MCPB.py/tleap in `docking-env`, then rerun the metal benchmark with `--run-autodock4zn` and promote only topology-validated MCPB outputs into MMGBSA/minimization workflows.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: FDA Mapping Name Repair | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended the FDA name-fill script with row-aware strict quality checks, NDC-like identifier detection, existing-source replacement, trusted title promotion, and stereo-prefix false-positive suppression; applied it to `chemdb/data/fda_mapping_from_pdbqt.csv`.
Why: The pilot FDA ligand mapping still had alias, placeholder, and truncated display names even though the correct names were already present in PubChem/RxNorm/DrugCentral-style columns for most rows.
Impact: The canonical FDA mapping now repairs 529 display names and both the filler report and benchmark audit report zero remaining flagged name-quality rows. Live PubChem/UniChem/GSRS repair was attempted but stopped after endpoint latency; no additional web source was needed for the audited gaps.
Next: Add a DrugCentral `structures.smiles.tsv` local export if future FDA rows lack PubChem/RxNorm title evidence, then rerun `chemdb.tools.fill_fda_mapping_names --quality_fix_mode strict`.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Run-scoped processed PDB output layout | Owner: Codex
Change: Scoped fresh receptor-prep output roots to `outputs/processed_pdbs/<RUN_ID>` so per-protein prep paths resolve as `outputs/processed_pdbs/<RUN_ID>/<PDB>/`.
Why: The preferred publication layout keeps all runtime products grouped under `outputs/` and run-scopes processed receptor artifacts consistently with docking, post-docking, configs, manifests, data, and logs.
Impact: Fresh runs now record the run-scoped processed-PDB root in runtime config/manifest paths; old flat processed roots are not promoted as the canonical path for this section.
Next: Verify `python main.py --test -fast`, path-router tests, and full-run tests, then check the new smoke run writes receptor prep under the run-scoped processed root.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Protein Prep AutoDock4Zn and MCPB Tool Enablement | Owner: Codex
Change: Staged official AutoDock-Vina `zinc_pseudo.py`, `prepare_gpf4zn.py`, and `AD4Zn.dat` under `tools/autodock4zn/`; updated AutoDock4Zn execution to use staged scripts, MGLTools `pythonsh`, and a local `AD4Zn.dat` copy for AutoGrid. Updated metal parameterization to discover the existing AmberTools prefix, set `AMBERHOME`, write renumbered MCPB receptor PDBs, remap metal `ion_ids`, emit single-metal MOL2 commands, and add protonation-name suggestions to MCPB templates.
Why: The previous benchmark could identify the correct open routes but reported missing tools and only wrote placeholder MCPB templates. AutoGrid also requires `AD4Zn.dat` in its working context, and MCPB rejects PDB2PQR-style duplicate atom serials.
Impact: The focused benchmark `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_metal_tools_final_2` reports `1S63` AutoDock4Zn `ready` with execution `ok`, and `2JDU` MCPB status `ready_for_heuristic_mcpb_trial` with no missing tools. A one-site MCPB smoke now passes metal MOL2 generation and reaches MCPB model construction before failing inside AmberTools on sidechain/hydrogen naming, confirming the remaining gap is chemistry curation rather than tool availability.
Next: Add a curated MCPB pre-clean stage for metal-site sidechain residue naming/hydrogen normalization before attempting MCPB stage 1 automatically across all non-Zn sites.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Vina-GPU Bench2 4-GPU Benchmark and Vendor Ignore | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `.gitignore` coverage for the local `tools/Vina-GPU-2.1/` checkout and removed the staged embedded gitlink from the index. Completed `bench2_vina_gpu_4gpu_canonical_20260503` with `VINA_BACKEND=vina_gpu`, `VINA_GPU_DEVICE=amd`, and `VINA_GPU_IDS=0,1,2,3`.
Why: The Vina-GPU checkout is third-party code used as a local tool, not Atlas source, and the user wanted to avoid committing an embedded repository. Bench2 needed a 4-GPU comparison against the canonical CPU Vina baseline.
Impact: Git now ignores the local Vina-GPU repo while preserving it on disk for runtime. The 4-GPU AMD run scored all 3,690 docking ligands but took 1,993.421 seconds versus the CPU baseline's 1,144.92 seconds, so observed throughput was slower. Throughput integrity failed because Vina-GPU post-docking artifacts were not materialized under `outputs/post_docked/<RUN_ID>/` even though docking rows were complete.
Next: Fix or explicitly document the Vina-GPU post-docking output contract before using Vina-GPU results for end-to-end reporting; CPU Vina remains the faster validated Bench2 path on this node.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Vina-GPU Parallelization Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Audited the 4-GPU Bench2 run for duplicate docking and CPU fallback. Stage completion summaries showed CPU and Vina-GPU ran comparable stage counts, and Vina-GPU chunk artifacts had unique input/output ligand counts. Updated Vina-GPU chunk planning to create multiple chunks per GPU for large batches via `VINA_GPU_CHUNKS_PER_GPU` and `VINA_GPU_MIN_CHUNK_LIGANDS`.
Why: The first 4-GPU run showed a long tail where one Vina-GPU chunk continued after other GPUs went idle, indicating static one-chunk-per-GPU scheduling was correct but poorly load-balanced.
Impact: Future large Vina-GPU stages should keep GPUs busier by letting idle devices acquire additional chunks through the existing per-GPU file locks. This does not change score semantics, GPU ID binding, or CPU fallback behavior, but it may change wall time and chunk artifact layout.
Next: Rerun a focused Bench2 or single-target Vina-GPU timing comparison before treating GPU throughput as final; separately fix the Vina-GPU post-docking artifact contract.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: MCPB Metal-Site Pre-clean | Owner: Codex
Change: Added MCPB-only receptor pre-cleaning in `src/protein_prep/metal_parameterization.py`: hydrogens are removed, terminal `OXT` atoms are removed, unparameterized non-metal HET residues are dropped, residues are renumbered uniquely across chains, and unambiguous local metal-bound Amber residue-name heuristics are applied/recorded for His/Cys/carboxylates.
Why: AmberTools MCPB.py stage 1 failed after tool installation because the staging receptor still had chain-colliding residue numbers, PDB2PQR/Reduce hydrogens, untemplated competitive/buffer HET residues, and terminal `OXT` records that MCPB's model builder could not parameterize automatically.
Impact: The benchmark now reports MCPB pre-clean counts in `summary.csv`. A 2JDU Ca smoke at `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_mcpb_preclean_oxt` retained all four Ca ions and all four generated Ca MCPB templates passed `metalpdb2mol2.py` plus `MCPB.py -s 1`. The pre-clean applies only to MCPB handoff copies, not production docking receptors.
Next: Promote only topology-validated MCPB stage 2-4 outputs into minimization/MMGBSA; add curated structural-cofactor MOL2/FRCMOD handling for true metal-bound cofactors instead of dropping them in heuristic MCPB staging.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Pilot Report Atlas Score Display | Owner: Codex
Change: Hid the internal `z_selected` name from visible report labels and tooltips, displaying it as `Atlas score` while preserving `z_selected` as the data/schema key. Regenerated `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas2/code/protein_automation/data/pilotstudy/report.html`.
Why: `z_selected` is an implementation compatibility column and was confusing in report interpretation. The source/provenance field is the meaningful user-facing distinction.
Impact: Existing CSV/JSON consumers remain compatible because the underlying field names are unchanged. The report now shows `Atlas score`, `Raw Atlas score`, and `Atlas score source`; embedded metadata still contains `z_selected` keys.
Next: Continue using `Atlas score source` in report interpretation and reserve `z_selected` for internal schema compatibility.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Wang2016 PDBbind Ligand Sources | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `--pdbbind-root` and `--pdbbind-zip` options to `atlas-mmgbsa benchmark-wang2016`; the command now copies representative `*_ligand.sdf` and `*_ligand.mol2` files into `<out-dir>/pdbbind_ligands/` and writes `wang2016_pdbbind_ligands.csv` when a local PDBbind/PDBbind-derived source is supplied.
Why: Wang et al. selected complexes from PDBbind, and PDBbind-style ligand SDF/MOL2 files are cleaner benchmark starting chemistry than reconstructing every ligand from RCSB PDB/CCD artifacts.
Impact: The resolver is provenance-preserving and marks missing entries explicitly. The public PDBbind++ 2020 archive yielded ligand SDF/MOL2 files for `1Q8W`, `2J75`, `1VZQ`, `1O2Q`, and `1LPZ`; `1C5Z` was absent and still needs older PDBbind or RCSB/CCD BEN handling.
Next: Prefer copied PDBbind MOL2/SDF files in the Wang control-redock/MMGBSA staging path, with MOL2 favored when SDF sanitization or chirality metadata is questionable.
Date: 2026-05-03 | Task: Atlas Screenshot Real-PDB Framing | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated `atlas screenshot` rendering to frame the ligand-centered active-site pocket, use run-scoped cleaned receptors, support dotted ligand stems such as `G24_A463.sanitized`, and limit residue labels to the nearest requested residues. Updated the shared ligand library index so dotted ligand stems resolve consistently for `--single` docking and screenshot selection.
Why: TEST screenshots were over-zoomed/clipped and real extracted ligand names exposed selector/path handling issues.
Impact: Screenshot output names remain deterministic under `<out>/<PDB>/<ligand_stem>/<ligand_stem>_{front,side,top}.png`; `--without-ligand` still adds `_no_ligand` before the view suffix. Real-PDB validation used 1Q4X single-ligand Vina docking through stage3 and restored the archived stage3 pose before rendering.
Next: Consider adding a flat filename template option if users prefer filenames that include PDB/stage directly instead of nested directories.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Atlas Screenshot PyMOL Visibility Controls | Owner: Codex
Change: Researched PyMOL screenshot guidance and added `--protein-style cartoon|surface|mesh|lines|none`, `--view-context full|pocket`, `--protein-transparency`, `--surface-carve-cutoff`, `--surface-carve-normal-cutoff`, `--clip-slab`, and `--ray` controls. Fixed screenshot render-kwargs routing so these controls reach the active PyMOL renderer.
Why: Transparent/cut pocket surfaces were still obscuring ligands and producing black clipped surface fragments, while pocket-only residue selections hid most of the protein.
Impact: The default receptor context is now full-protein cartoon framing with no slab clipping and no residue labels. `--view-context pocket` restores tight active-site framing. Surface/mesh/lines/no-protein modes remain available for publication-specific variants. Validated on `screenshot_real_1q4x_single_final` with full cartoon, surface, mesh, and ligand-only variants.
Next: If surface-mode black cut edges remain objectionable, keep `cartoon` as default and consider a PyMOL CGO clipping/capping strategy instead of molecular surface carving.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Hide Vina-GPU Backend | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed Vina-GPU branching from the standard docking stage runner and removed public `VINA_BACKEND`/`VINA_GPU_*` defaults, env overrides, example config entries, and config docs. Left `src/docking/vina_gpu.py` as an isolated experimental adapter that is not imported by the production docking path.
Why: Focused BNJS/bench_pur2 benchmarking showed 4-GPU Vina-GPU was slower than 32-core CPU Vina for Atlas FDA-scale workloads despite full GPU utilization.
Impact: Normal Atlas docking is CPU Vina only again. Existing hidden experimental Vina-GPU code stays contained for future reference but no longer leaks through config or the main pipeline.
Next: Use CPU Vina for production FDA-vs-PDB runs; revisit GPU docking only if a distinct large-library benchmark demonstrates a clear speedup.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Pilot FDR/Exposure/ML Readiness | Owner: Codex
Change: Added pilot report FDR resolvability diagnostics, decoy-competition q diagnostics, SPD-based master exposure filling, ML drug-target deduplication options, holdout split overlap summaries, and leakage checks that block rescoring-availability features. Regenerated the atlas2 pilot master/report with the current FDA mapping resolver and local SPD supplement exposure values.
Why: The pilot report showed zero q-value hits despite high Atlas z scores because BH FDR over empirical decoy p-values is mathematically unresolvable with the current decoy count/test count. Free-Cmax was also absent from master rows, and ML training needed explicit safeguards against duplicated drug-target rows and SCORCH top-10% availability leakage.
Impact: The report now exposes `min_possible_bh_q`, BH resolvability, and decoy-competition summaries instead of implying all non-significant q-values are equally interpretable. `free_cmax_um`, `cmax_um`, `fraction_unbound_plasma`, and `exposure_source` can be populated from local SPD data without treating missing exposure as negative evidence. ML dataset/model outputs can now enforce deduplicated rows and audited drug/target/scaffold holdouts.
Next: Stage local Papyrus/ChEMBL, ToxCast, SIDER, CTD, OpenTargets, and Reactome exports at the configured paths before publishable ML claims; add a post-SCORCH decoy null if formal FDR should be computed on SCORCH/final z-score rankings.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: SCORCH Conditional FDR Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis/reporting/fdr_methods.py` and wired master/report generation to emit `target_scorch_fdr_summary.csv` plus row-level `scorch_fdr_*` fields. The report now includes a SCORCH Conditional FDR table next to the original full-library consensus/DUD FDR table.
Why: Comparable docking work generally emphasizes decoy enrichment/rank metrics and target-decoy style FDR, while the prior Atlas BH-over-empirical-decoy-p-values test was too coarse for thousands of FDA rows and only ~3.5k full-library decoys. SCORCH is the preferred scoring signal but only exists for the rescored top tranche, so it needs a clearly labeled conditional null.
Impact: Pilot report users can now inspect SCORCH-backed target-decoy competition q-values while preserving the conservative full-library consensus FDR. The SCORCH table reports both ordinary target-decoy q and a +1 finite-sample version to expose small-decoy sensitivity.
Next: For manuscript-grade SCORCH FDR, run SCORCH on a larger retained DUD decoy tranche per target or use repeated matched-decoy/permutation nulls so the SCORCH null has enough resolution without relying on a small conditional subset.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Report Statistical Formula Documentation | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a generated formula panel to `report.html` through `analysis/reporting/statistical_formula_html.py`, covering empirical decoy p-values, BH q-values, resolvability, target-decoy competition q, SCORCH conditional FDR, enrichment factors, AUPRC, AUROC, permutation p-values, bootstrap intervals, matched-control p-values, SPD exposure margins, and ablation reranking.
Why: Future researchers need the report to document the exact calculations behind the displayed statistical tables without reverse-engineering the Python implementation.
Impact: The report HTML now includes a visible, expandable formulas section in the Statistical Analysis block. No CSV schemas changed.
Next: Keep this panel synchronized if new statistical outputs or altered null-model definitions are added.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Code-Owned Statistical Formula Registry | Owner: Codex
Change: Refactored the report formula panel to collect formula metadata from module-level `STATISTICAL_FORMULAS` exported by the statistical implementation modules, with `analysis/reporting/statistical_formula_registry.py` aggregating FDR, ranking/enrichment, matched-control, and SPD formula definitions.
Why: Static report HTML text can drift from the code. Keeping formulas beside the functions that implement them makes future statistical changes easier to keep synchronized with the generated report.
Impact: Formula text now includes source-function references in the report and can be updated by editing the implementation module's formula metadata. No output CSV schemas changed.
Next: When adding or changing statistical methods, update the adjacent `STATISTICAL_FORMULAS` entry in the same implementation module and rerun the report.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: MCPB Cofactor Parameterization Automation | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended `src/protein_prep/metal_parameterization.py` so MCPB planning loads metals/waters/cofactors from `aliases.yaml`, detects canonical metal-bound cofactors from metal donor rows, attempts MMGBSA AmberTools antechamber/parmchk2 MOL2/FRCMOD generation when a matching local SDF is available, and wires successful cofactor parameter files into MCPB `naa_mol2files`/`frcmod_files`.
Why: The previous MCPB pre-clean dropped all unparameterized non-metal HETs, which was fine for competitive ligands/buffers but did not distinguish unresolved structural cofactors from ordinary solvent-like residues.
Impact: Benchmark summaries now include `mcpb_cofactor_parameterization_*` and `mcpb_dropped_unparameterized_cofactor_atom_count` fields. The 2JDU Ca benchmark at `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_mcpb_cofactor_auto` has no aliases.yaml canonical metal-bound cofactors, keeps metal retention at 4/4, and all four Ca MCPB stage-1 model generations still pass.
Next: Add a benchmark target with a true metal-bound canonical cofactor plus local SDF chemistry to exercise the auto-parameterization branch, then promote only topology-validated MCPB stage 2-4 outputs into MMGBSA/minimization.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: MCPB Mg-ATP Cofactor Benchmark | Owner: Codex
Change: Used 4AFF as an Mg/ATP benchmark target with local `ATP_ideal.sdf`, staged MCPB-local cofactor MOL2/FRCMOD basenames, and normalized the staged MOL2 residue name from antechamber `MOL` to the PDB cofactor residue name.
Why: Heme-only 1A6M did not exercise the current MCPB branch because its Fe is internal to the HEM residue rather than a standalone audited metal ion. 4AFF has a separate Mg ion coordinated by canonical ATP, so it tests the metal-bound cofactor path directly.
Impact: `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_mcpb_atp_mg_probe_resname` reports `structure_valid=True`, Mg retained, ATP retained/parameterized, Meeko OK, and no geometry clashes. MCPB stage 1 now gets past file-token and residue-charge lookup, but still fails on `KeyError: ATP-PG` because SDF/antechamber atom names do not match PDB/CCD ATP atom names.
Next: Add CCD/PDB atom-name reconciliation or curated cofactor templates before promoting Mg/ATP MCPB stage 1, then require stage 2-4/QM/RESP/tleap topology validation before production use.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: MCPB Cofactor Atom Name Reconciliation | Owner: Codex
Change: Added graph-based MCPB MOL2 atom-name reconciliation for metal-bound cofactors. Atlas now matches the local SDF heavy-atom graph to the receptor PDB residue graph, records the generated-name to PDB-name mapping, and rewrites only the staged MCPB-local MOL2 atom names while preserving antechamber types/charges.
Why: 4AFF Mg/ATP reached MCPB but failed on `KeyError: ATP-PG` because antechamber generated generic names (`P1`, `O1`, etc.) while MCPB looked up receptor atom names (`PG`, `O3G`, etc.).
Impact: `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_mcpb_atp_mg_atomnames` maps all 31 ATP heavy atoms, keeps 4AFF `structure_valid=True`, and MCPB stage 1 now passes for `MG_A_1118`. Stage 2/3 still require generated QM logs (`small_fc.log`, `large_mk.log`); stage 4 can generate tleap inputs but no production topology is validated.
Next: Automate or explicitly stage the QM/RESP execution and topology validation gates before promoting MCPB outputs into production MMGBSA/minimization.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: MCPB QM Gate Planning | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended MCPB planning with optional Gaussian/GAMESS discovery, explicit QM required outputs for stage 2/3, generated QM command hints when a supported engine is present, an explicit `mcpb_qm_required_manual` marker when no supported QM engine is visible, and `ion_info` in generated templates so MCPB `4n2` nonbonded fallback can run.
Why: Mg/ATP stage 1 passed after atom-name reconciliation, but default MCPB stage 2/3 failed on missing `*_small_fc.log` and `*_large_mk.log`. MCPB's default bonded route requires Gaussian or GAMESS-US logs; GAMESS-US is free but not open-source/redistributable, so Atlas should not silently vendor it.
Impact: `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_mcpb_atp_mg_qmplan` reports `mcpb_cofactor_atom_name_map_status_counts=mapped:1`; `metal_mol2_from_pdb`, stage 1, and `4n2` execute for 4AFF Mg/ATP. Stage 2/3 remain gated on missing QM logs, with missing optional tools reported as `rungms,g16,g09,g03,formchk`.
