Next: If a publishable bonded metal model is required, install/module-load a supported QM engine externally, run the generated small force-constant and large MK jobs, then rerun MCPB stage 2/3 and tleap/cpptraj/parmed topology validation.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Pilot report statistical audit / FDR threshold UI | Owner: Codex
Change: Added heatmap significance-threshold modes for percentile rank, full-library target FDR q, and SCORCH conditional q. Fixed report heatmap generation by wiring side-effect metadata text normalization through `analysis.reporting.value_utils.normalize_text`. Regenerated `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas2/code/protein_automation/data/pilotstudy/report.html` with the statistical panel, FDR controls, and corrected ablation fallback wording.
Why: Researchers need to distinguish broad top-percentile screening from FDR-controlled hit filtering, and the report should not describe SPD score comparisons as true ablations when `ablation_summary.csv` is absent. The heatmap was failing during ligand side-effect metadata collection because `_normalize_text` was undefined.
Impact: Standalone pilot report now loads in headless Chromium with no page errors or console errors; FDR controls are visible next to the existing threshold slider. SCORCH q remains a conditional top-tranche statistic because SCORCH is only run on selected rows. Existing percentile threshold behavior remains the default.
Next: Stage true ablation_summary/matched-control outputs and full external bioactivity/mechanism source joins before ML claims; fix the FDA mapping ingestion path that still reports 19 unresolved FDA display names in the visible heatmap subset and 6,732 unresolved names across the combined report prepass.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Pilot FDR/SPD ML staging | Owner: Codex
Change: Added report KPI cards for SCORCH conditional FDR thresholds, added SPD drug-target deduplication with strict ML-ready output, and made external label staging write explicit missing-source manifests for Papyrus/ChEMBL/ToxCast.
Why: Full-library BH FDR remains conservative while SCORCH-tranche conditional FDR is the intended exploratory view; supervised ML needs one SPD label per drug-target and explicit unknown/missing external-source handling.
Impact: The pilot report now distinguishes full-library BH from relaxed/+1 SCORCH conditional q counts. SPD weak-margin rows are excluded from strict binary ML, unknown rows remain unknown, and missing local bioactivity sources are surfaced instead of silently skipped.
Next: Place or configure local Papyrus/ChEMBL/ToxCast assay files, then rebuild external labels and rerun drug/target/scaffold-holdout ML on deduplicated rows.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Protein Prep Meeko Parser Recovery | Owner: Codex
Change: Added benchmark Meeko fallback inputs that normalize PDB2PQR terminal residue labels, remove waters/hydrogens/Reduce `USER MOD` records for parser recovery, and finally prune non-backbone sidechain/HET atoms responsible for impossible inter-residue proximity bonds before an `allow_bad_res` retry. Missing ligand centroids now return `None` so no-ligand targets still emit benchmark rows.
Why: The default 10-target benchmark retained metals but still failed Meeko on water/metal-heavy `4DFR` and `2JDU`; `4DFR` also had a PDBFixer-added PRO sidechain atom close enough to a preceding carbonyl oxygen for RDKit/Meeko to infer impossible valence.
Impact: `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_meeko_patch_20260504` reports Meeko receptor export success for all 10 targets and metal retention for all metal targets. Overall validity is still 6/10 because `1A30`, `3PTB`, `4DFR`, and `2JDU` fail geometry-clash gates; `4DFR` uses `clash_pruned_allow_bad_retry:dropped_atoms=1`, so it remains a structural-review case.
Next: Replace parser-only recovery with a real restrained geometry cleanup for severe-clash residues, then rerun the default benchmark plus redocking/water sensitivity.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Protein Prep Default Seed Curation | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed `1A30` from the default protein-prep benchmark seed list and documented that default seeds favor extractable non-water HET ligands rather than peptide-only polymer inhibitors.
Why: `1A30` is HIV-1 protease complexed with a tripeptide GLU-ASP-LEU inhibitor modeled as polymer chain `C` using `ATOM` records; the default ligand extractor correctly found no non-water/non-ion HET ligand, so the target was not comparable to DUD-E/PDBBind-style small-molecule co-crystal controls.
Impact: `--limit 10` now advances to the next default seed instead of including a peptide-only control with no extracted ligand. Peptide inhibitor benchmarking remains a separate future mode rather than being mixed into small-molecule receptor-prep validation.
Next: Rerun the 10-target default benchmark and use geometry clash sidecars to address the remaining small-molecule receptor failures.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Protein Prep Geometry Clash Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `protein_prep.geometry_audit`, pre/post `geometry_clash_audit*.json` sidecars, and conservative geometry cleanup before benchmark validity/Meeko checks.
Why: Geometry failures were previously opaque aggregate counts. The benchmark now records exact atom pairs, distance, residue/chain/atom identifiers, atom classes, ignored peptide/metal contacts, and cleanup actions.
Impact: The final 10-target benchmark at `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_geometry_audit_patch_20260504` reports 10/10 valid receptors, Meeko success for all targets, and unchanged metal counts. Cleanup removed one water and one repair-added PRO CD sidechain atom in `4DFR`, and two clashing waters in `2JDU`; `3PTB` is no longer failed for valid peptide C-N contacts.
Next: Remaining publication gaps are global ligand/cofactor-aware H-bond state selection, curated HET state selection, explicit water-retention justification by density/conservation/sensitivity, and production-quality restrained minimization or rotamer rebuilds for sidechain geometry cases.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: MMGBSA PDBbind Ligand-State Gates | Owner: Codex
Change: Wired report-supplied PDBbind/curated SDF/MOL2 paths into MMGBSA batch staging, added ligand protomer/tautomer provenance with optional Dimorphite-DL enumeration, and added severity-ranked suspicious-chemistry records to ligand prep and production review gates.
Why: Wang-style benchmark controls and publication follow-up runs need chemically authoritative ligand inputs and explicit ligand-state audit records instead of silent PDBQT reconstruction or untracked protomer assumptions.
Impact: `wang2016_pdbbind_ligands.csv` can now feed `batch-from-report`; production review blocks blocker/critical ligand chemistry findings. Enumeration is recorded for review, but Atlas still does not automatically select the biologically correct protomer/tautomer from multiple candidates.
Next: Validate the staged PDBbind controls through full redocking/MMGBSA once receptor PDBQT prep is stable, then add state-selection benchmarking or curated per-ligand review records for publication datasets.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: MMGBSA Wang Missing-Control Skip | Owner: Codex
Change: Changed Wang/PDBbind ligand staging so only controls with resolved PDBbind SDF/MOL2 sources are written to the batch-ready `wang2016_pdbbind_ligands.csv`; unresolved controls are written to `wang2016_pdbbind_ligands_skipped.csv`.
Why: The PDBbind++ 2020 source lacks `1C5Z`, and carrying a missing ligand row into report-driven MMGBSA creates avoidable benchmark noise.
Impact: `batch-from-report` will not attempt 1C5Z when using the PDBbind++ 2020 manifest. Benchmark coverage is smaller but cleaner, and skipped controls remain auditable.
Next: Use the resolved controls for Wang smoke comparisons; only reintroduce 1C5Z if an authoritative ligand SDF/MOL2 source is explicitly staged.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: MMGBSA Ligand State Inference | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended MMGBSA ligand-state provenance to reuse docking pH-label parsing and protein-prep `get_target_ph_for_prep` context, rank Dimorphite-DL protomer and RDKit tautomer candidates, preserve authoritative crystal/PDBbind input chemistry by default, and block ambiguous state ranking in production mode.
Why: Publication-oriented MMGBSA needs an automated but auditable ligand protomer/tautomer choice that shares receptor-prep pH assumptions instead of using a separate hardcoded pH parser.
Impact: `*.mmgbsa_prep.json` and review records now include `ph_context` and `state_inference` payloads with selected state, candidate scores, ranking margin, and evidence availability. Screening can continue with ambiguous states marked; production rejects ambiguous state inference unless reviewed or run as a multi-state case.
Next: Add optional generation/redocking/MMGBSA of multiple plausible ligand states when the state-inference margin is low.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Pilot publication/ML readiness | Owner: Codex
Change: Added publication heatmap/readiness exports, added a pilot-safe nonleaky ML feature set, normalized pilot score/exposure aliases in ML dataset building, fixed string binary-label parsing for training, and blocked partial SCORCH rescoring columns as predictive features.
Why: The pilot needed a clean publication table with score provenance/statistical confidence/exposure labels and a separate deduplicated ML table that does not let models learn raw IDs, SPD weak/unknown rows, or SCORCH top-tranche availability.
Impact: `publication_heatmap_input.csv`, `heatmap_ml_readiness_summary.csv`, and `ml_pair_table_dedup_leakage_controlled.csv` are now generated for the pilot. The regenerated report includes a Coverage Audit / ML Usability section. A logistic drug-holdout smoke model passes with zero drug overlap.
Next: Let the separate FDA/RDK mapping update land, then rerun the report and rebuild these artifacts; add Papyrus/ChEMBL/ToxCast local files before making publishable ML-scale claims beyond SPD.
Date: 2026-05-04 | Task: Pilot bioactivity source staging | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a pilot bioactivity staging CLI that builds an FDA/PDB pair table, downloads and filters Papyrus++ locally, queries focused ChEMBL activity records with cache files, joins Papyrus/ChEMBL labels, and writes a deduplicated nonleaky bioactivity ML table plus drug/target/scaffold holdout readiness summaries.
Why: SPD alone was too sparse for supervised activity modeling; Atlas needed larger assay-backed labels while preserving unknowns as missing and keeping raw IDs/labels out of ML features.
Impact: The pilot now has `bioactivity_pair_table.csv`, Papyrus/ChEMBL source extracts, `bioactivity_ml_source.csv`, `ml_pair_table_bioactivity_dedup_leakage_controlled.csv`, and zero-overlap holdout summaries. ToxCast hit calls are still not downloaded because the official current INVITRODB summary package is about 7.5 GB, but the release metadata is recorded explicitly.
Next: Decide whether to stage the full ToxCast/invitrodb package despite its size or add a targeted ToxCast extractor, then rerun the staging CLI after the updated FDA/RDK mapping lands.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Full ToxCast pilot ingest | Owner: Codex
Change: Downloaded the official EPA invitrodb v4.3 `INVITRODB_SUMMARY.zip` archive to `/stor/work`, validated its SHA256, added a streaming ToxCast importer for assay target mappings and mc5/6 hit-call rows, and wired ToxCast into the combined pilot bioactivity ML labels.
Why: The prior pilot ML source was limited to SPD/Papyrus/ChEMBL-style activity labels; the user requested full ToxCast ingestion so assay-backed external activity coverage is no longer missing locally.
Impact: `toxcast_atlas_benchmark.csv` and `data/external/toxcast/bioactivity.tsv` are now generated. The deduplicated combined ML table increased to 7,367 rows with zero-overlap drug/target/scaffold holdout summaries. ToxCast labels are treated as in vitro activity labels only, not clinical ADR validation.
Next: Re-run this staging after the final FDA/RDK mapping lands, then decide whether to add CTX/DSSTox ID enrichment for unmapped ToxCast chemicals and target-specific assay quality filters before manuscript-scale ML claims.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: ToxCast filters and ML rigor plan | Owner: Codex
Change: Added ToxCast assay annotation, analytical QC, cytotoxicity lower-bound, and DSSTox enrichment handling to the pilot bioactivity staging path; added a publishable ML readiness plan document.
Why: ToxCast hit calls need source-appropriate filtering before supervised ML, and the pilot needed explicit guardrails separating assay activity, exposure unknowns, and ADR/mechanism validation.
Impact: Filtered ToxCast labels now exclude unusable assays, failed chemical QC records, cell-viability endpoints, and cytotoxicity-confounded active hits. The combined ML table remains deduplicated and holdout-ready, with free-Cmax missingness recorded as unknown.
Next: Add source-holdout validation and temporal validation once label-source/year fields are staged; rerun after final FDA/RDK mapping and consider CTX API batch enrichment if an EPA API key is available.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Protein Prep Publication Gates | Owner: Codex
Change: Added water-policy audit sidecars, publication-readiness gates, geometry-cleanup binding-site rebuild flags, production Meeko stage sidecars, a Vina redocking CPU cap, and removed a duplicate post-copy element-fix pass in the receptor-cleaning sequence.
Why: The benchmark was proving receptor validity but not outcome validity or review state. Publication claims need explicit water, HET-state, metal, Meeko fallback, redocking, and constructive-geometry gates rather than hidden fallback success.
Impact: The 10-target publication-mode run at `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_publication_gates_20260505_b` reports 10/10 `structure_valid`, 10/10 Meeko receptor export, 0 metal-count losses, 0 final geometry clashes, 7/10 redocking attempts completed, and 0/10 strict `publication_ready` because water policy, HET-state ambiguity, metal review, Meeko parser recovery, redocking RMSD/failures, and two cleanup-minimization failures remain explicit gates.
Next: Improve redocking ligand chemistry from authoritative instance SDF/MOL2 inputs, define a curated conserved-water policy, and decide whether Meeko normalized-input recovery is acceptable as a reviewed method note or should become the standard receptor export input.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Wang2016 redocked MMGBSA benchmark execution | Owner: Codex
Change: Ran Atlas redocking for Wang controls under run_id `wang2016_atlas_redock_20260505_a` with CPU/GLOBAL_SCHEDULER caps of 32, staged archived coordinate-matched redocked SDFs into report-driven MMGBSA, and completed one-frame MMGBSA for `1LPZ/CMB_B301` and `1O2Q/991_A246`. Added MMGBSA config lookup fallback from pH-specific config dirs to base config dirs for report-staged ligands.
Why: Report-driven MMGBSA materializes ligands under pH directories, while non-pH Atlas redocking writes Vina center configs under the base stage layout; without the fallback MMGBSA skipped with `missing_center`.
Impact: The pipeline can execute a real redock-to-MMGBSA smoke benchmark on archived Atlas artifacts. `1VZQ` and `2J75` still fail receptor PDBQT prep, `1Q8W` had no usable control-redock output, `1O2Q` used the valid stage1 pose because stage3 was invalid, and both MMGBSA outputs are one-frame screening values rather than publication-grade Wang-comparable trajectories.
Next: Fix remaining receptor PDBQT prep failures, preserve/use valid final redock SDFs for all controls, add per-complex Wang/PDBbind experimental reference rows keyed by PDB/ligand, and run explicit-solvent multi-frame production MMGBSA before claiming Wang-style publication agreement.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: ML validation hardening | Owner: Codex
Change: Wired the updated FDA mapping override into pilot bioactivity staging, preserved label source/year metadata in ML datasets, added source-holdout and temporal-release validation controls, added bootstrap metric confidence intervals, reliability tables, calibration plots, and optional PU-weighted unlabeled background handling.
Why: The pilot needs publishable ML guardrails that separate assay sources, avoid treating unlabeled pairs as confirmed negatives, and expose calibration/uncertainty outputs alongside AUROC/AUPRC.
Impact: Pilot outputs now include drug/target/scaffold/source/temporal/PU validation runs with `model_metric_bootstrap_ci.csv`, `model_reliability_table.csv`, and `ml_calibration_curve.png`. Source transfer to ToxCast is weak in the current pilot and remains a gap before making cross-source performance claims.
Next: Stage true activity publication years and richer PK/free-Cmax tables, then rerun the same validation modes after final FDA/RDK mapping and mechanism-label joins settle.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Evidence-year temporal validation and source-transfer diagnostics | Owner: Codex
Change: Propagated ChEMBL `document_year` and Papyrus `Year`/document IDs into bioactivity benchmarks and ML tables, changed combined `activity_publication_year` to the earliest source evidence year, excluded missing-year rows from publication-year temporal holdouts, and added source-transfer label/prediction balance diagnostics.
Why: Database release years are too coarse for prospective-style validation, and the weak ChEMBL/Papyrus-to-ToxCast transfer result needs source-prevalence and prediction-balance context rather than a standalone AUROC/AUPRC.
Impact: Publication-year temporal validation now runs on the ChEMBL/Papyrus evidence-dated subset; ToxCast v4.3 remains release-year dated unless a comparable per-assay date field is staged. Source-holdout outputs now include `source_transfer_label_balance.csv` and `source_transfer_prediction_balance.csv`.
Next: Add true source-specific snapshot exports if historical ChEMBL/Papyrus/ToxCast releases are needed, and avoid claiming cross-source generalization until source-transfer metrics improve under balanced or calibrated evaluation.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Negative-label hierarchy and decoy benchmark separation | Owner: Codex
Change: Added shared ML label parsing helpers, rebuilt the main PU-weighted pilot model from measured assay actives/inactives plus unlabeled FDA background, and added `atlas.analysis.cli.run_decoy_benchmark` for a separate control-vs-decoy benchmark panel.
Why: Random or property-matched decoys should not become core FDA/off-target negatives. They are useful as technical docking controls only, while measured assay inactives and PU-weighted unknowns are the safer main ML training hierarchy.
Impact: Pilot outputs now include `ml_bioactivity_logistic_main_pu_drug_holdout/` for the main model and `decoy_benchmark_panel/` with summary, per-target metrics, rows, manifest, and score distribution figure. Undefined decoy metrics are marked as `insufficient_classes` rather than reported as zero.
Next: Add measured-inactive-only sensitivity models and ChEMBL-only document-year temporal validation before manuscript ML claims; keep DeepCoy/DUD-E/DEKOIS generation as optional benchmark inputs only.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: ML sensitivity outputs and exposure coverage | Owner: Codex
Change: Added source-filtered ML dataset building, class-weight CLI control, source-specific calibration outputs, and exposure coverage reporting. Generated measured-only, ChEMBL document-year temporal, ToxCast-calibrated transfer, and exposure coverage pilot outputs.
Why: The remaining ML gaps needed concrete sensitivity analyses rather than prose: measured-inactive-only behavior, source-specific temporal validation, ToxCast calibration, and explicit free-Cmax/Cmax/fraction-unbound coverage.
Impact: Pilot metrics are now available under `ml_bioactivity_logistic_measured_only_drug_holdout/`, `ml_bioactivity_logistic_chembl_document_year_temporal/`, `ml_bioactivity_source_calibration_toxcast/`, and `exposure_coverage/`. ToxCast calibration improves calibration metrics but is documented as source-specific sensitivity because it uses a ToxCast calibration split.
Next: Run target/scaffold variants for measured-only and ChEMBL temporal models, and improve exposure coverage only through curated PK tables rather than imputation.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: ChEMBL temporal/proxy diagnostics | Owner: Codex
Change: Ran ChEMBL-only random, drug, target, scaffold, and document-year temporal holdouts; generated temporal class-balance resamples, single-score baselines, feature missingness/proxy-shift tables, assay metadata drift tables, and a decoy-supervision audit.
Why: The ChEMBL temporal AUROC/AUPRC drop needed to be separated into true temporal failure, label prevalence shift, source/assay drift, proxy-feature reliance, and possible decoy/negative misunderstanding.
Impact: Diagnostics show DUD/decoy rows are not supervised ChEMBL negatives; ChEMBL-only AUROC is weak across split types, while temporal AUPRC is strongly prevalence-sensitive. Assay metadata drift exists but is kept out of predictive features because it is not available for new unassayed FDA-target pairs.
Next: Treat ChEMBL-only ML as a weak assay-label signal until richer nonleaky biological features or measured inactive coverage are added; report assay metadata drift and prevalence-normalized metrics alongside temporal validation.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Wang2016 production MMGBSA workflow hardening | Owner: Codex
Change: Fixed Wang receptor/ligand staging blockers for 1Q8W, 2J75, and 1VZQ; added stricter Meeko receptor fallbacks for salt/OXT/HIS normalization; fixed report-source same-file materialization; corrected explicit-solvent sander reference coordinates, minimization input generation, effective-mode logging, and configurable explicit MD stage lengths.
Why: Production MMGBSA was entering explicit-solvent setup but failed immediately because restrained sander stages lacked `-ref`, minimization used `nstlim` instead of `maxcyc`, and full Wang reruns were not practical to exercise interactively without bounded stage controls.
Impact: The benchmark now redocks all selected controls except skipped 1C5Z and can enter the explicit-solvent production workflow under `MMGBSA_PROTOCOL=production`. A bounded three-replicate production-protocol smoke reached explicit MD setup but still failed in short unrestrained minimization for 1LPZ, indicating remaining bad-contact/ligand-chemistry relaxation issues rather than the earlier missing-reference bug.
Next: Add a pre-MD clash/energy gate and a staged relaxation rescue for high initial VDW systems, then run full 10 ns production jobs under a scheduler with reviewed ligand charge/protonation records and per-complex Wang/PDBbind reference rows.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Wang2016 production MMGBSA QC and scheduler package | Owner: Codex
Change: Added explicit-solvent pre-heating minimization QC, automatic restrained/unrestrained rescue minimization, failure notes that surface `preheat_qc`, and a `wang2016-production-package` CLI that writes a 32-CPU Slurm script plus per-ligand review-record scaffolds.
Why: Full production MMGBSA should block unstable high-energy systems before heating and should run as a scheduler job only after ligand charge/protonation/tautomer/water/metal review is explicit.
Impact: Bounded 1LPZ rescue smoke now records high-GMAX/high-RMS/high-energy/high-VDW/nonfinite preheat QC failures after rescue in `explicit_solvent_methods.json`. The generated Wang package under `/stor/home/mpg2352/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016/production_package_20260505` is submit-ready but intentionally requires review status env vars before Slurm execution.
Next: Use longer rescue/minimization settings or curated/pre-relaxed crystal-control systems for high-contact ligands, approve or replace the generated review records with curated chemistry, then submit the full 10 ns x 3 replicate production job.
Date: 2026-05-05 | Task: Protein Prep Water/HET/Redock Publication Fixes | Owner: Codex
Change: Added CCD-backed extracted-ligand SDF generation with crystal coordinate reconciliation, nucleotide prime-name atom mapping, context-scored HET state selection, conservative supported-water receptor generation, seeded Vina redocking, prepared-ligand PDBQT RMSD for CCD routes, and PDBFixer/OpenMM Modeller-based restrained minimization repair.
Why: Publication gates were failing because raw PDB-to-SDF ligand conversion lost bond order/atom-order consistency, HET states were only enumerated, water policy had no selected receptor, Vina redocking was stochastic, and OpenMM minimization was fed PDB2PQR/Reduce hydrogens/waters that did not match force-field templates.
Impact: Final 10-target run at `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_publication_ready_20260505_final` reports 10/10 `structure_valid`, 10/10 Meeko receptor export, 10/10 redocking completed, 9/10 redocking RMSD pass in that unseeded run, 0 metal-count losses, and 6/10 strict `publication_ready`; a seeded 1HVR rerun at `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_1hvr_seeded_20260505` passes redocking, so the current seeded benchmark behavior is effectively 7/10 publication-ready with remaining failures dominated by 3PTB/2JDU metal physics/protonation review and 4DFR parser/water/metal review.
Next: Decide whether default publication readiness should exclude metal-driven targets or keep metal physics as a hard gate; separately inspect 4DFR residue A:66/selected-water Meeko failure before claiming that target is automated-publication-ready.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Protein Prep Publication Gate Refinement | Owner: Codex
Change: Added water redocking-sensitivity sidecars and gates, evidence-based metal publication routing, resolved ASP/GLU metal-carboxylate protonation evidence, HET state review labels, constructive geometry repair before sidechain deletion, metal-bound water restoration after repair, selected-water Meeko stage sidecars, union-key benchmark CSV writing, and conservative RDKit symmetry-aware redocking RMSD fallback.
Why: Strict failures mixed real scientific gaps with over-broad review gates: structural Ca sites were treated like completed force-field parameterization requirements, water policy lacked outcome sensitivity, 4DFR geometry cleanup created an incomplete PRO residue, and HET ambiguity was selected but not machine-labeled.
Impact: Full 10-target publication run at `/stor/home/mpg2352/atlas_prep_benchmark_publication_patch_20260506` reports 10/10 `structure_valid`, 10/10 Meeko receptor export, 10/10 redocking completed, 9/10 redocking RMSD pass, 0 metal-count losses, 9/10 water selections supported by redocking, and 8/10 strict `publication_ready`. 3PTB now passes with structural-metal disclosure; 4DFR is structure-valid and Meeko-clean after constructive repair but still fails redocking/water sensitivity; 2JDU remains blocked because the extracted ligand removes direct ligand-Ca donor contacts and needs metal-aware scoring.
Next: For 4DFR, inspect ligand pose/box/tautomer and consider whether it is a fair default redocking target; for 2JDU, either keep it as a metal-aware-docking negative control or add an explicit AD4Zn-like/metal-aware scoring route before allowing publication readiness.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Water Evidence and Mini DUD-E Water Benchmark | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `water_evidence_audit.json` generation with resolution, occupancy, local protein B-factor, optional CCP4 map sampling, and homolog-water conservation support; made publication water gates require density/conservation/map support plus redocking for selected waters; added `-water-bench`, water DUD-E plan/staging helpers, and 999-ligand AA2AR/HS90A/HIVPR mini libraries; expanded HET state scoring to include binding-site waters, HIS/ASN/GLN flip candidates, borderline titratable residues, and metals.
Why: Water retention needed WaterKit/hydrated-docking-style evidence rather than only bridge-distance heuristics, and HET selection needed a reproducible open approximation to Protoss-style binding-site network scoring without depending on a non-vendored web service.
Impact: Protein-prep publication mode now distinguishes redocking-only water support from crystallographic/conservation/map-supported water retention. `docs/water_dude_benchmark_plan.json` records the three-policy enrichment plan, and `docs/water_dude_stage_report.json` records staged mini libraries under `prepped_ligands/water_aa2ar`, `water_hs90a`, and `water_hivpr` with 999 ligands each.
Next: Run the three `-water-bench` policies under scheduler constraints, evaluate `analysis.cli.dud_eval` metrics for each run, and use the enrichment deltas to decide whether `site_only` should remain the production default or whether evidence-selected waters should become the default receptor export.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Wang2016 local production MMGBSA launch | Owner: Codex
Change: Added and launched a non-Slurm local Wang2016 MMGBSA production runner capped at 32 CPUs, with forced regeneration, 30-minute ligand AM1-BCC timeout, production protocol defaults, and detached PID/log tracking.
Why: The active machine does not use Slurm, so the Wang explicit-solvent benchmark needed a durable local submission path while preserving the user's global core cap.
Impact: Local runner PID `2655123` is active from `/stor/home/mpg2352/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016/production_package_20260506_local/run_wang2016_mmgbsa_production_local.sh`; log is `/stor/home/mpg2352/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016/production_package_20260506_local/wang2016_mmgbsa_production_local.log`. Startup reached 1LPZ/CMB ligand AM1-BCC preparation with active `antechamber`/`sqm` children and no `high_gmax`, `high_rms`, `high_abs_energy`, `high_abs_vdw`, `nonfinite_energy_markers`, traceback, or error markers in the startup log.
Next: Continue monitoring the local log/process until each Wang control completes or records a structured ligand/preheat/MD failure; do not treat outputs as publication-comparable until review records are curated rather than environment-approved for benchmark execution.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Wang2016 local production MMGBSA status and CPU cap | Owner: Codex
Change: Patched the Wang local runner generator to export OpenMP/BLAS thread caps and launch via `taskset -c 0-$((CPU - 1))`; applied live `taskset -a` affinity to the running Wang process tree.
Why: The submitted local runner used `CPU=32`, but AmberTools `sqm` had already spawned many worker threads across the full 128-CPU node, so the OS affinity mask had to be applied to every existing task/thread.
Impact: Live Wang runner remains active at PID `2655123`, with Python PID `2655129`, currently in `1VZQ/SHY_H1256` AM1-BCC ligand prep. Thread-level inspection found no active SQM thread scheduled outside CPUs `0-31` after the fix. Production-mode summaries currently show ligand prep failures for completed/skipped targets, and no production replicate MMGBSA values are yet comparable to Wang.
Next: Let the capped runner finish or timeout the current `1VZQ` AM1-BCC step, then inspect structured `mmgbsa_results_summary.csv` files; if ligand prep remains the dominant blocker, retry with curated PDBbind/precharged MOL2 or relaxed strict charge policy only for screening, not publication comparison.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Wang2016 MMGBSA ligand-prep failure fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Fixed MMGBSA ligand prep so parmchk2's placeholder `Remark line goes here` is not counted as a real frcmod warning, made existing mol2/frcmod reuse honor production strict gates, and expanded the default BCC charge sweep to include `-2,+2`.
Why: Four Wang controls had successful BCC/GAFF2 prep but were rejected solely because the placeholder frcmod remark was treated as a publication-blocking warning. `1VZQ/SHY` is a separate charge-state problem: old metadata shows BCC `nc=0` timed out, `nc=-1/+1` failed, gas charges succeeded, and local PDBbind MOL2 sums to about `+2`, which Atlas had not attempted.
Impact: Reuse-mode restart PID `3087308` passed `1LPZ/CMB` ligand prep (`ok=true`, `publication_ready=true`, zero frcmod warnings) and advanced into explicit-solvent `sander min1`. Gas-fallback prep remains blocked in strict production mode. Future forced SHY prep can try `nc=+2` by default.
Next: Monitor PID `3087308` through explicit preheat QC. For `1VZQ/SHY`, rerun forced prep with the expanded charge sweep or provide a coordinate-matched precharged MOL2; do not treat the gas-charge fallback as publication-comparable.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: ChEMBL/Papyrus inactive label correction | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated `atlas/analysis/external/papyrus_chembl.py` so exact measured activities above the active threshold become measured inactive labels, ambiguous censored rows remain unknown, and within-source active/inactive conflicts are surfaced. Updated pilot bioactivity staging to exclude within-source conflicts from ML labels while recording cross-source active/inactive disagreement as metadata.
Why: ChEMBL temporal diagnostics showed train/test assay-relation drift and positive inflation; exact weak binders were previously not counted as measured inactive evidence.
Impact: Regenerated pilot ML source now has 7,027 deduplicated measured rows with 813 positives and 6,214 negatives; 565 within-source conflicts are unknown and 43 cross-source disagreements are flagged. Main corrected drug-holdout logistic model: AUROC 0.796, AUPRC 0.383. Corrected ChEMBL document-year temporal model: AUROC 0.543, AUPRC 0.370.
Next: Keep temporal ChEMBL claims cautious; add stricter ChEMBL sensitivity panels by assay type/relation/confidence, and improve PK provenance/coverage through a dedicated structured PK table rather than text-inferred missing exposure.

Change: Added ML builder/trainer flags for `--require-nonmissing-cols` and `--exclude-features`, added `atlas.analysis.external.pk_sources` plus CLI `python -m atlas.analysis.cli.build_pk_table` for local SPD/openFDA/PK-DB/DrugBank-style structured PK exports, and generated a four-model pilot panel.

Drug-holdout AUROC/AUPRC: all measured 0.796/0.383, exposure-complete 0.781/0.453, SPD exposure relevance 0.897/0.889, no-PK sensitivity 0.791/0.374. Structured DrugBank data remains local-input only because it is licensed.
Next: Rerun the SPD model after the full SPD protein panel is processed through Atlas, then add any available PK-DB/DrugBank structured exports through `build_pk_table` and restage pilot bioactivity with `--pk-table`.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Adversarial ML strategy audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added explicit numeric missingness indicators during model training, row exclusion via `--exclude-flag-cols`, stricter exposure-label leakage checks, and a `pilot_binding_only` feature set. Ran drug/target/scaffold/source-holdout audits plus SPD no-PK and no-source-disagreement sensitivity models.
Why: The user requested a loophole-finding loop before trusting the ML strategy. The audit found exposure missingness/provenance signal, source imbalance, cross-source disagreement, and SPD label circularity risks.
Impact: The defensible primary model should currently be binding-score based, with exposure treated as validation/stratification until the full SPD panel is processed. ChEMBL/Papyrus to ToxCast source transfer is near-random (AUROC 0.507, AUPRC 0.026), so it is not a positive publication claim. Binding-only drug/target holdouts remain comparable to full models.
Next: Process the full SPD target panel through Atlas; add source-balanced evaluation and true Murcko scaffold groups; improve structured PK provenance; avoid universal assay-transfer claims until source-holdout improves.

Pilot target coverage: bioactivity has 10,465 labelable rows; SPD has 498 labelable rows; mechanism/ADR has 0 labelable rows because graph evidence is not currently staged. A missing-label parsing bug was fixed so all-missing mechanism labels remain unknown, not negative.

Change: `--waterbench` now forces a stage-1-only Vina pass while retaining the three DUD-E-style water-policy target/library mappings.
Why: The initial water-off launch entered full multi-stage docking, which is unnecessary for before/after water-policy enrichment evidence and made the compact benchmark too expensive.

Next: Run the three waterbench policies and evaluate DUD metrics for water-off versus selected-water versus keep-all.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Meeko Review Label Cleanup | Owner: Agent
Change: Meeko receptor prep now applies deterministic HIS template assignments on the first modern/legacy command and tries clash-pruned export without `allow_bad_res` before falling back to review-labeled `allow_bad` mode.
Why: Meeko documents `-n/--set_template` as the intended residue-template assignment mechanism, while `allow_bad_res` ignores residues with unresolved templates and should remain review-worthy.
Impact: Ambiguous HIS export can now be cleanly labeled as `modern_his_template_input`; only safe no-`allow_bad` clash pruning is accepted as non-review.
Next: Rerun a compact prep/waterbench target to confirm fewer `his_nmap_retry` labels and identify any remaining true `allow_bad` cases.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Meeko Normalized Retry | Owner: Agent
Change: Production receptor export now attempts normalized no-hydrogen Meeko inputs before any `allow_bad_res` or clash-pruned fallback, and the shared stage-review policy accepts normalized parser retries as non-review.
Why: pH-prepared receptors can contain hydrogens/records that make RDKit infer impossible valence during Meeko parsing even when the heavy-atom receptor is otherwise usable.
Impact: Parser-normalization successes no longer appear as review-blocking clash/allow-bad recoveries; dewatered normalization remains a water-policy disclosure.
Next: Rerun the compact waterbench smoke and inspect `*.meeko_input_stage.json` for any remaining `clash_pruned_allow_bad_retry` cases.
Date: 2026-05-06 | Task: Protein Prep Toolchain and Waterbench Automation | Owner: Agent
Change: `MEEKO_ALLOW_BAD_RES` is now disabled by default, clash-pruned `allow_bad_res` rescue is opt-in, active Python environment `bin/` is searched for PDB2PQR/Reduce/Probe, and `protein_prep.water_dude_benchmark run-policies` can execute and summarize all three water policies.
Why: Publication receptor prep should not silently ignore unresolved residues, pH/H-bond preparation should find open tools installed in `docking-env` even when the shell is not activated, and water-policy claims need an objective enrichment benchmark artifact.
Impact: Direct absolute-Python runs can resolve `pdb2pqr`, `reduce`, and `probe`; waterbench runs now write a JSON report plus a metrics TSV for `remove_all`, `site_only`, and `keep_all`.
Next: Run the full three-policy waterbench under the CPU cap and compare `outputs/data/water_dude_eval/water_policy_summary.tsv` before changing the production water default.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: MMGBSA MPI Sander Enablement | Owner: Agent
Change: Installed an Atlas-local MPI AmberTools environment at `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools/envs/ambertools-mpi` and changed explicit-solvent MMGBSA MD to prefer `sander.MPI` with `mpirun`/`mpiexec` when available, while pinning OpenMP/BLAS thread counts to 1 and capping MPI ranks at 32.
Why: Serial `sander` was only using one effective CPU despite thread environment settings, causing slow production MMGBSA runs and renice emails.
Impact: Future explicit-solvent MMGBSA steps can parallelize across MPI ranks; the conda AmberTools build provides `sander.MPI` but not `pmemd.MPI`/`pmemd.cuda`, so this is faster than serial but still not the fastest Amber production engine.
Next: Relaunch a controlled Wang production subset with the MPI env, then compare completed crystal/redock MMGBSA outputs after charge/protonation and water-retention review gates are satisfied.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Atlas Screenshot Review Gallery | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas screenshot <RUN_ID> --pdb <ID> --representatives --gallery-html` to select best, median, and worst scored ligands per PDB and write a local `gallery.html` with front/side/top PNG cards.
Why: Docking visualization tooling and publication workflows emphasize ranked pose inspection and reviewable visual summaries; Atlas already rendered individual PyMOL PNGs but lacked a low-friction run-level triage surface.
Impact: Existing `--top` and `--ligand` screenshot workflows remain compatible. Gallery HTML is written only for successfully rendered selections and uses escaped labels plus relative image links under the screenshot output directory.

Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Portable Screenshot Gallery | Owner: Codex

Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Protein Prep Publication Strategy Audit | Owner: Agent
Change: Meeko stage review now treats any atom-dropping parser recovery and auto HIS template mapping as publication-review gates, benchmark Meeko sidecars now record both operational and publication review flags, parser-pruned inputs write `*.drop_audit.json` with exact dropped atoms, and benchmark Reduce/Probe detection checks the active Python environment.
Why: The previous strategy could report publication readiness after deterministic Meeko recovery that silently removed atoms, and PATH-only Reduce/Probe checks could underreport available pH/H-bond tooling.
Impact: Automatic docking recovery still works, but publication readiness is stricter. The 1UYG smoke is structure-valid and redocks well, yet publication-blocked because Meeko needed to drop terminal `LYS A224 OXT`.
Next: Decide whether terminal-OXT Meeko recovery should be replaced by a true termini-aware receptor export/minimization path, or kept as a documented review gate for docking-only runs.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Wang MMGBSA Production Benchmark Strategy | Owner: Agent
Change: Wang production package generation now pins 10 ps frame stride with full-window endpoint analysis, requires explicit review-gate environment variables for local runs, enriches report rows with PDBbind ligand MOL2/SDF sources, transfers redocked SDF coordinates onto authoritative MOL2 chemistry when available, enables +-2 AM1-BCC net-charge sweep by default, uses ligand formal charge auto-detection, and launches both MD and trajectory MMPBSA through Atlas-local MPI AmberTools when available.
Why: The previous benchmark path could silently analyze only the post-burn-in subset, approve review gates by launcher default, reconstruct malformed neutral docked SDFs from PDBQT-like chemistry, miss +2/-2 charge attempts, and run endpoint MMPBSA serially despite MPI MD.

Next: Let the active MPI production run finish, inspect replicate QC/endpoint MMPBSA outputs, then decide whether to curate water/metal retention and ligand charge/protonation records before treating the comparison as publication-comparable.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Wang MMGBSA 4 ns Validation Flags | Owner: Agent
Change: Added `wang2016-production-package` flags and generated script flags for production length, replicate count, frame stride/window, MD/MMPBSA ranks, CPU cap, and validation mode; regenerated the Wang package with 4 ns, 1 replicate, 10 ps snapshots, and `MMGBSA_PRODUCTION_STRICT=false` for validation.

Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Chemistry-Preserving OXT and PDB2PQR HET Policy | Owner: Codex
Change: Added terminal OXT geometry repair before Meeko normalized/sanitized export, removed default production OXT-stripping fallback, and changed PDB2PQR HET policy logging to report generic HET retention as unsupported when only ligand-MOL2 options are present.

Why: The optional MCPB handoff copy should follow the same chemistry-preserving termini policy as Meeko receptor export, even though MCPB remains a review-only metal-parameterization route.

Date: 2026-05-08 | Task: Geometry Package and Binding-Site Network Optimizer | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved shared geometry audit/repair code under `protein_prep.geometry`, left compatibility wrappers for older imports, added an open Protoss-inspired bounded binding-site network optimizer for HET state scoring, and added retained-HET rescue sidecar audits.
Why: Geometry repair should not live in Meeko-specific modules, HET state selection needed a real local network model rather than flat contact counts, and PDB2PQR HET rescue needed machine-readable evidence.
Impact: `het_state_selection.json` now records local variable-site counts, estimated mode counts, recommended HIS/ASN/GLN/titratable/water modes, and candidate network scores. PDB2PQR rescue writes `*.retained_hets_audit.json`. The optimizer is still disclosure-labeled as open/local and not Protoss-equivalent.
Next: If selected local protein modes should modify receptor coordinates/residue names, implement that as a separate high-risk patch with benchmark gates for H-bond contacts, Meeko compatibility, and redocking.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: ML Target Expansion and MoE Evidence | Owner: Codex
Change: Added full-SPD panel coverage/run-list staging, mechanism-source staging hooks for SIDER/CTD/OpenTargets/Reactome in external-label builds, and `build_moe_adr_score` for conservative bioactivity/exposure/mechanism evidence fusion.
Why: The pilot needed concrete status for the three ML targets and a nonleaky way to discuss MoE-style prioritization without mixing assay activity, exposure relevance, and ADR mechanism labels.
