The pilot MoE table has 176,682 rows, 2,320 rows with any expert score, 2,103 bioactivity expert rows, 235 exposure expert rows, and 0 mechanism expert rows.
Next: Dock/rescore the missing SPD target/drug panel, stage local SIDER/CTD/OpenTargets/Reactome files, then train calibrated held-out expert models before treating MoE output as a manuscript-level ADR implication score.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Bioactivity Source-Transfer Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas.analysis.ml.source_transfer_audit` and CLI `audit_source_transfer`; changed pilot bioactivity label fusion so cross-source active/inactive disagreements are ambiguous/missing rather than positive-wins.
Why: ChEMBL/Papyrus-to-ToxCast transfer was weak and needed a factual data audit. The old fusion rule left 43 cross-source disagreement rows labelable as positives, which was not a conservative publication strategy.
Impact: Corrected pilot ML table has 6,980 labelable rows, 774 positives, and 6,206 negatives; cross-source disagreements are no longer labelable. Corrected pure ChEMBL/Papyrus-to-ToxCast source holdout remains weak (AUROC 0.425, AUPRC 0.080, ECE 0.484) and the audit flags prevalence shift, low drug overlap, and prediction prevalence miscalibration. ToxCast source-calibration sensitivity improves AUROC/AUPRC to 0.648/0.140 and ECE to 0.000, but uses a ToxCast calibration split.
Next: Treat ToxCast as an independent/source-calibrated HTS benchmark, not a pure transfer success; fix the updated VDS FDA mapping compatibility before using it for bioactivity staging because it collapsed the deduplicated table to 3 rows in this run.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Source-Specific ML Benchmarks | Owner: Codex
Change: Added source-specific benchmark table builders and comparison runner: `build_source_benchmark_tables` writes curated ChEMBL/Papyrus, ToxCast HTS, SPD exposure, source-transfer, and matched-transfer tables; `run_source_benchmark_comparison` trains within-source, raw transfer, source-calibrated, and matched-transfer models and writes metric/calibration artifacts.
Why: Fused ChEMBL/Papyrus/ToxCast labels hid major source shift. The corrected design keeps curated bioactivity, ToxCast HTS activity, and SPD exposure relevance as separate objectives and treats curated-to-ToxCast transfer as a stress test.
Impact: Pilot outputs are under `data/pilotstudy/source_benchmark_comparison/`. Raw curated-to-ToxCast transfer improved versus older fused-label runs, but ToxCast prevalence and calibration remain distinct; source-calibrated ToxCast results must be reported separately from pure external validation.
Next: Use the separated tables for future ML claims, add mechanism graph source files before ADR/mechanism training, and rerun the comparison after the FDA mapping staging issue is resolved.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Source Leakage Guard and Cache Mechanism Evidence | Owner: Codex
Change: Expanded ML leakage checks to forbid source labels/statuses, assay metadata, activity thresholds, and SPD label-defining PK/AC50 fields; source benchmark tables now also emit feature-restricted model-ready CSVs. Added `export_cached_mechanism_sources` to convert report caches into SIDER-like, OpenTargets-safety-like, and Reactome-like TSVs.
Why: The source-specific evidence tables intentionally retain assay/source metadata for audit, but those fields must never become predictive features. Normalized SIDER/CTD/OpenTargets/Reactome files are still absent, while report caches contain partial mechanism evidence that can support a conservative interim mechanism graph.
Impact: New comparison outputs under `data/pilotstudy/source_benchmark_comparison_v2/` include prevalence-normalized AUPRC. Cache-backed mechanism exports under `data/pilotstudy/mechanism_cache_sources/` produce 63 strict pilot mechanism triad positives in `data/pilotstudy/evaluation_targets_cache_mechanism_v2/mechanism_adr_support_target.csv`.
Next: Keep cache-backed mechanism evidence labeled as interim; stage proper normalized SIDER/CTD/OpenTargets/Reactome files before training a publishable mechanism/ADR model.
Date: 2026-05-07 | Task: Normalized Mechanism Source Staging | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas.analysis.external.mechanism_sources` and CLI `python -m atlas.analysis.cli.stage_mechanism_sources` to download and normalize SIDER, CTD, OpenTargets Safety, and Reactome into repo-local `data/external/*` files. Rebuilt the pilot mechanism graph from normalized sources under `data/pilotstudy/mechanism_graph_normalized_sources/`.
Why: Mechanism/ADR ML needed proper local source tables rather than report-cache exports. SIDER required flat/STITCH drug-name mapping, CTD required chunked pilot target-gene filtering, and OpenTargets/Reactome needed normalized graph-ready TSV outputs.
Impact: Repo-local normalized row counts are SIDER 771,263, CTD 773,625, OpenTargets Safety 753, and Reactome 1,384. The normalized-source pilot graph has 1,270,332 edges and 69 strict mechanism triad positives in `data/pilotstudy/evaluation_targets_normalized_mechanism/mechanism_adr_support_target.csv`.
Next: Treat missing mechanism evidence as unknown; use the 69 strict triads for enrichment/PU or matched-background work, not as a full supervised mechanism classifier until curated negatives are defined.
Date: 2026-05-08 | Task: Protein Prep Waterbench OXT Repair | Owner: Codex
Change: Added reasoned internal-OXT pruning for Meeko fallback, exported shared geometry repair helpers, skipped low-confidence HET-state rewriting for redock ligands, and reran the three-policy waterbench.
Why: 3EML generated an OXT on an internal peptide link, causing Meeko parser failure across water policies. Low-confidence MTX state rewriting worsened 4DFR redocking and should remain audit-only.
Impact: 3EML is now evaluable in remove-all, site-only, and keep-all waterbench policies; the clean internal-OXT retry is not a publication review gate. Waterbench output is in `docs/water_dude_oxtfix_report.json` and `outputs/data/water_dude_eval/water_dude_20260508_oxtfix_*`.
Next: Use site-only water retention as the current default benchmark winner, keep density/conservation claims scoped to available evidence, and only promote broader HET network rewriting after benchmark gates show redocking/enrichment gains.
Date: 2026-05-08 | Task: Mechanism ML Negative Label Strategy | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `docs/atlas_ml_negative_label_strategy.md` and updated `docs/atlas_publishable_ml_next_steps.md` with the four-state mechanism label policy, allowed negative evidence tiers, leakage rules, and a Deep Research prompt for additional intentional-negative datasets.
Why: Mechanism/ADR ML needs strict separation between positives, measured/reliable negatives, unknowns, and ambiguous rows. Decoys and Atlas-derived rankings must not be used as core FDA/off-target negatives because they can bias or leak the mechanism model.
Impact: Future ML agents should treat PU/matched-background as the primary mechanism strategy, measured/reliable negatives as sensitivity training, OMOP/OHDSI and FAERS non-signals as calibration controls, and DUD-E/DEKOIS/DeepCoy/diversity decoys as benchmark-only.
Next: Implement ingestion flags and audit columns for `mechanism_label`, `negative_evidence_type`, `negative_source`, `negative_confidence`, `negative_selection_fold`, and `negative_selection_features` before training a publishable mechanism classifier.
Date: 2026-05-08 | Task: Negative Evidence Ingestion Implementation | Owner: Codex
Change: Added reusable negative-evidence normalization (`atlas.analysis.external.negative_evidence`), fold-local reliable-negative mining (`atlas.analysis.ml.reliable_negatives`), four-state mechanism label table construction, and CLIs `build_negative_evidence`, `build_mechanism_label_table`, and `build_reliable_negatives`.
Why: PubChem/LIT-PCBA/ExCAPE/BindingDB/Davis/KIBA, OMOP/OHDSI controls, FAERS non-signals, and reliable negatives need one shared schema and leakage policy rather than ad hoc per-source labels.
Impact: Measured drug-target negatives can now join Atlas pair rows directly; drug-ADR controls only join when an ADR key exists, so they remain calibration/control evidence otherwise. ML dataset exports now preserve negative provenance/confidence columns for audit.
Next: Stage real local source files in the current repo, generate `negative_evidence.csv`, rebuild `mechanism_four_state_labels.csv`, then run PU primary and measured/reliable-negative sensitivity models under drug/target/scaffold holdouts.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Pilot Negative Evidence Staging and MoE Monitoring | Owner: Codex
Change: Downloaded small public OHDSI MethodEvaluation R datasets into `data/external/ohdsi/`, extracted CSVs, staged measured inactive plus OMOP/OHDSI negative-control evidence, rebuilt `data/pilotstudy/negative_evidence/mechanism_four_state_labels.csv`, retrained the mechanism sensitivity model, and refreshed three-expert MoE monitoring outputs under `data/pilotstudy/moe_experts/`.
Why: The pilot needed real local negative evidence rather than only import code. OMOP/OHDSI controls are useful calibration/control evidence but should not be forced into pair-level mechanism labels without an ADR key.
Impact: Negative evidence now has 6,210 measured inactive drug-target rows and 510 OMOP/OHDSI drug-ADR controls. Pair-level mechanism labels contain 16 strict positives, 6,157 measured/reliable negatives, 792 unknowns, and 53 ambiguous/conflicting exclusions. Current mechanism sensitivity drug-holdout has only one test positive, so it is a sanity check rather than a publishable model.
Next: Add ADR-keyed pair/panel tables if OMOP/OHDSI controls should calibrate drug-ADR MoE scores directly; add FAERS non-signal tables only after reporting-volume and positive-evidence filters are staged locally.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: FAERS Non-Signal Staging and Negative Audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added cached OpenFDA FAERS count retrieval via `atlas.analysis.external.faers` and CLI `build_faers_nonsignals`; staged `data/external/faers/ohdsi_omop_faers_disproportionality.csv`; rebuilt negative evidence with 117 FAERS observed non-signals; added source-balanced mechanism sensitivity dataset/model and pair-mapping audit outputs.
Why: FAERS non-signals should be reproducible, cached, rate-limit tolerant, and separated from pair-level drug-target assay negatives. The ToxCast-heavy negative mix needed an explicit source-balance sensitivity path.
Impact: `negative_evidence.csv` now has 6,210 measured inactive assay rows, 510 OMOP/OHDSI controls, and 117 FAERS non-signals. Pair-level mechanism labels remain unchanged because current rows lack ADR keys; FAERS/OMOP are staged for ADR-keyed calibration/control. Negative pair audit shows 6,210/6,210 measured negatives rejoin exact source drug-target rows with inactive labels and assay IDs.
Next: Build ADR-keyed mechanism panel tables so OMOP/OHDSI and FAERS controls can calibrate drug-ADR MoE outputs directly; keep source-balanced sensitivity metrics beside the all-negative mechanism sensitivity run.
Date: 2026-05-08 | Task: Optional OpenMM MMGBSA MD backend | Owner: Codex
Change: Added MMGBSA_MD_ENGINE=openmm with Amber topology/restart input and Amber restart/NetCDF output for the explicit MD stage; Wang production package scripts now expose --md-engine, --gpus, and --openmm-start-stage.
Why: OpenMM HIP can run the long explicit-solvent production leg on AMD GPUs while preserving existing tleap, ligand prep, dry/solvated topologies, cpptraj stripping, and MMPBSA.py.MPI downstream behavior.
Impact: Default OpenMM start stage is prod so Amber/sander still handles fragile minimization/heating/equilibration; starting OpenMM at min1 remains configurable but can NaN on high-contact systems.
Next: Let the Wang OpenMM validation job complete, then compare cpptraj/MMPBSA outputs and QC against the previous sander-based run and Wang protocol expectations.
Date: 2026-05-08 | Task: Protein Prep Network Application and Waterbench Follow-up | Owner: Codex
Change: Wired the open binding-site network application into production clean_pdb before Meeko export, moved CCD atom reconciliation behind protein_prep.het_atom, added selected-water hydrogen orientation for supported site_only waters, and added production sidecars for HET selection, network application, water policy, and selected-water rows.
Why: The network optimizer was previously benchmark-audited but not applied in the normal receptor-prep path; water rows also needed explicit selected-water auditability.
Impact: Production prep now conservatively applies only low-risk network actions, keeps PDB2PQR/Reduce authoritative when explicit hydrogens/states exist, skips low-confidence HET states, and leaves keep_all/remove_all waterbench policies semantically unchanged. Existing monolithic clean/prep benchmark functions still fail repo complexity gates.
Next: Keep water-policy claims scoped to benchmarked selected-water sensitivity; consider a stricter 4DFR/MTX ligand-pose diagnostic using no-fit and symmetry-aware RMSD before treating that target as a prep failure.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Staged MMGBSA GPU MD workflow | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas-mmgbsa staged-from-report`, `staged-run-md`, and `staged-aggregate` commands plus `post_docking.mmgbsa.mmgbsa_staged`. The workflow stages report-selected ligands into dry-topology preparation, per-replicate MD job JSON files, and per-ligand MMPBSA aggregation job JSON files. Local runner scripts cap concurrent MD jobs by GPU count and cap CPU/MPI ranks at or below the requested CPU budget. Explicit MD now resumes completed stage restart/trajectory outputs instead of restarting every stage.
Why: The previous Wang/MMGBSA benchmark path was a long serial loop, which made GPU use, interruption recovery, and failure triage hard. Splitting preparation, GPU MD, and MMPBSA aggregation makes each unit resumable and easier to debug.
Impact: Existing `batch-from-report` behavior remains available. Staged jobs reuse existing ligand prep, dry Amber topologies, explicit-solvent tleap, OpenMM/sander MD, cpptraj stripping, and MMPBSA.py.MPI. Generated job/status files are new output interfaces under the selected staged output directory and per-ligand MMGBSA work directories.
Next: Use the staged package for Wang-style benchmark reruns, then compare completed per-ligand aggregate summaries against available Wang/reference expectations. Stop or retire older monolithic benchmark launchers once the staged route is accepted.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Researcher onboarding QoL commands | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas smoke public`, `atlas first-run`, `atlas dev verify`, `atlas demo --status-html`, ligand/library `--dry-run` planning, expanded `tools/quality_gate.sh --full`, and aligned onboarding docs around `outputs/data/<RUN_ID>`.
Why: Researchers needed fewer copy-paste workflow chains for fresh-clone smoke checks, first target selection, canonical verification, and ligand planning.
Impact: Public CLI surface expanded without changing docking/scoring semantics. `tools/quality_gate.sh --full` now runs stricter checks and may fail on existing repo debt that the narrower gate did not cover.
Next: Consider follow-up wrappers for distributed submit/finalize, reproducibility bundles, and analysis/dev tool grouping.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Four-State Evidence Collapse for ML Labels | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas.analysis.ml.four_state_evidence` and CLI `python -m atlas.analysis.cli.build_four_state_evidence`. The builder emits raw provenance rows, canonical pair-key collapsed labels, optional joined ML feature tables, and pair-mapping audit outputs. It supports local ChEMBL, BindingDB, PubChem BioAssay, SPD, Papyrus, ExCAPE, ToxCast, OMOP/OHDSI, FAERS non-signals, and benchmark-only LIT-PCBA/DUD-E/MUV-style tables through one shared schema.
Why: Explicit positive/negative evidence must be deduplicated by canonical pair key; duplicate source rows should preserve provenance without becoming independent labels, and conflicts/benchmark-only rows need consistent four-state policy handling.
Impact: Pilot four-state outputs are under `data/pilotstudy/four_state_evidence/`. The joined table has 6,727 trainable rows after conflict/unknown filtering. Full-table negatives remain ToxCast-heavy, so source-balanced results are a sensitivity analysis rather than the primary claim. Pair audit passes blocking checks.
Next: Stage real local BindingDB, PubChem BioAssay, ExCAPE, LIT-PCBA, DUD-E, and MUV tables in the current repo if those sources should contribute beyond the already staged ChEMBL/Papyrus/ToxCast/SPD/OHDSI/FAERS artifacts.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: ADR-Keyed Controls and ML Diagnostics | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas.analysis.ml.adr_mechanism_panel`, `atlas.analysis.cli.build_adr_mechanism_panel`, `atlas.analysis.external.ml_source_catalog`, `atlas.analysis.cli.stage_ml_label_sources`, `atlas.analysis.ml.model_diagnostics`, and `atlas.analysis.cli.run_ml_diagnostics`. The source catalog staged the small LIT-PCBA subset and recorded local-path manifests for BindingDB, PubChem BioAssay, ExCAPE, DUD-E, and MUV. The ADR panel builder creates drug-ADR control tables from OMOP/OHDSI/FAERS labels plus aggregated Atlas drug-target features. Diagnostics now write learning-curve log loss/AUC plots, per-feature ablations, and score-only baselines.
Why: OMOP/OHDSI and FAERS controls are drug-ADR labels, not drug-target labels. They need a separate calibration/control table. Biology ML best practice also requires cold/entity splits, source-aware validation, ablations, calibration, learning curves, and baseline comparisons rather than a single AUROC/AUPRC.
Impact: `data/pilotstudy/adr_mechanism_panel/adr_mechanism_panel_table.csv` has 516 labelable drug-ADR controls but only 96 with pilot Atlas features, so ADR-panel ML remains a smoke test. Binding-summary ADR AUROC/AUPRC is 0.429/0.297 and mechanism-summary is 0.339/0.272; do not make positive ADR-ML claims from the pilot overlap. Diagnostics for the four-state model show stable but shallow learning curves, indicating feature/data coverage limits.
Next: For publication, rerun diagnostics under drug, target, scaffold, temporal, and source holdouts on the full SPD-expanded/pilot-plus dataset; stage filtered BindingDB/PubChem/ExCAPE exports under the catalog paths; keep LIT-PCBA/DUD-E/MUV benchmark-only.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Correct ML Source Storage Assumption | Owner: Codex
Change: Confirmed `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham` has ample VDS filesystem capacity via `df -h`, corrected the source catalog BindingDB URL to the direct `/downloads/` archive URL, removed the prior HTML placeholder, and downloaded `data/external/bindingdb/BindingDB_All_202605_tsv.zip` successfully. Updated ML notes to distinguish direct downloadable archives from query/portal filtered exports.
Why: The earlier source-staging wording incorrectly reacted to a home quota banner even though the repo data directory lives on VDS work storage. BindingDB also requires the direct archive URL, not the intermediate `SDFdownload.jsp` ready page.
Impact: BindingDB full TSV archive is now staged locally for future filtered export. PubChem BioAssay and ExCAPE remain manifest/query-filter sources; LIT-PCBA remains benchmark-only and already staged. No training labels changed in this correction.
Next: Add a BindingDB filtered-export CLI that streams `BindingDB_All.tsv` from the zip, keeps Atlas FDA drugs/targets by InChIKey/PubChem CID/ChEMBL/DrugBank/name and UniProt, then writes `data/external/bindingdb/bioactivity.tsv` for four-state ingestion.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: MMGBSA staged validation tiers | Owner: Codex
Change: Added staged MMGBSA validation tiers for contract, topology, tiny-md, canary, and production runs; staged report jobs now accept simple validation flags, write benchmark staging under outputs/mmgbsa_benchmarks by default, and can copy tiny-MD fixtures to outputs/mmgbsa_fixtures.
Why: Full Wang-style production MMGBSA is too slow for normal development, so contract/schema checks, tleap-only topology smoke tests, tiny OpenMM MD canaries, and cached fixtures are needed to catch path, chemistry, GPU handoff, cpptraj, and aggregation regressions quickly.
Impact: Existing custom staged jobs remain supported. New tier presets override MD lengths/replicates for development or production intent, and contract/topology tiers intentionally avoid long MD execution.
Next: Run one topology smoke and one tiny-md canary from the staged Wang package before using canary or production tiers as release gates.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Protein Prep Binding-Site Geometry Policy and No-Fast Waterbench | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a targeted binding-site geometry policy that preserves resolved sidechains by default, removes only clashing waters automatically, escalates binding-site-relevant added-sidechain clashes to restrained repair, and records geometry sidecars. Added no-fast waterbench support, stronger control-redock search defaults, high-search redock retry reporting, and Vina config plumbing for future rigid/flex receptor overrides.
Why: Publication prep should globally repair missing atoms/residues/hydrogens while avoiding global rotamer rebuilding that can move experimental sidechains. Water policy needed a non-fast benchmark signal, and control-ligand failures needed to be separated from fast-mode noise.
Impact: No-fast waterbench ran for remove_all/site_only/keep_all under `water_dude_20260509_nofast_geompolicy_*`; keep_all had the best mean ROC/logAUC/BEDROC in the mini set, but control redock RMSDs and OXT-pruned Meeko recovery remain disclosure items. The smoke run `20260509_103532` completed with docking outputs, while report heatmap generation warned because the TEST run has no usable Vina heatmap rows.
Next: Treat selected/keep-all water policy as benchmarked sensitivity rather than a universal claim; add larger enrichment or density/conservation evidence before making general conserved-water claims, and consider Vina-flex/control-ligand-specific retries only for targets where control redock failure blocks interpretation.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Protein Prep Modularization Pass | Owner: Codex
Change: Added package namespaces for `protein_prep.water`, `protein_prep.metal`, `protein_prep.network`, `protein_prep.meeko`, and HET chemistry/state-selection helpers under `protein_prep.het_atom`; migrated smaller production modules to those package import paths while preserving legacy top-level compatibility modules.
Why: Protein prep logic had grown into flat clusters for water policy, metal handling, binding-site network optimization, Meeko export review, and HET chemistry. Package namespaces create clearer ownership without a risky broad move of benchmark/orchestration files.
Impact: Runtime behavior should be unchanged. New code should prefer package paths; `prep_benchmark.py` remains the main large follow-up split because it is over 100 KB and still imports legacy top-level modules.
Next: Split `prep_benchmark.py` into benchmark orchestration, target staging, redocking metrics, and report writers; then progressively move implementation modules behind the new package namespaces once call sites are covered by focused tests.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Analysis CLI grouping cleanup | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas analysis dud-eval`, `atlas analysis throughput integrity|acceptance`, and `atlas analysis interactions export`; updated user-facing docs and agent instructions to prefer Atlas wrappers over raw `python -m analysis.cli.*` calls.
Why: Analysis/reporting workflows were still split between discoverable Atlas commands and lower-level module invocations, which made onboarding less consistent.
Impact: Existing module entrypoints remain available for compatibility. New wrappers route to the maintained analysis modules and keep normal outputs in existing run-scoped directories.
Next: Keep specialized ML/research-only commands as module CLIs until they become public onboarding workflows; revisit environment wrapper UX separately from pip packaging.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Atlas environment shim installer | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated `tools/installers/install_atlas_core.sh` to install an outer `atlas` shim by default, with `--no-shim`/`ATLAS_INSTALL_SHIM=0` escape hatches and `ATLAS_SHIM_DIR` override; the publication stack reports the shim policy and keeps `atlas ...` as the ready path.
Why: Nontechnical users should be able to run `atlas ...` commands without manually activating `docking-env`, while keeping pip installation side effects limited to the selected environment.
Impact: Fresh installs now add or update an outer shell shim in a writable PATH directory when possible, falling back to `${HOME}/.local/bin`. The shim dynamically discovers micromamba/conda at runtime and dispatches into `docking-env`, improving portability across HPC HOME/work/scratch layouts.
Next: On a clean login shell, run the publication installer and verify `command -v atlas` resolves to the shim and `atlas setup-report` works without `micromamba activate`; if site modules hide micromamba by default, document the module-load step.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Protein Prep Benchmark and Meeko Split | Owner: Codex
Change: Split Meeko receptor-input normalization/pruning helpers from `prep_benchmark.py` into `protein_prep.meeko.normalization`, switched production `receptor_prep.py` to use that module directly, moved benchmark report writing into `protein_prep.benchmark.reporting`, and removed the unused `_strip_reduce_user_lines` receptor shim.
Why: Production receptor export should not import the benchmark monolith, and `prep_benchmark.py` was above the 100 KB split threshold.
Impact: `prep_benchmark.py` is now under 100 KB. Meeko fallback behavior should be unchanged, but the ownership is clearer: benchmark reporting lives under `protein_prep.benchmark`, and Meeko fallback input cleanup lives under `protein_prep.meeko`.
Next: Continue splitting `prep_benchmark.py` by moving redocking metrics and target staging next; then move remaining Meeko command/fallback execution internals out of `receptor_prep.py`.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Researcher onboarding QoL follow-up | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas slurm submit|progress|finalize`, `atlas analysis report|export-master`, `atlas debug`, `atlas artifacts measure`, and `atlas reproduce bundle`; updated `atlas report` to run master export first by default; made `tools/slurm/run_spr.sh` honor `ATLAS_MAIN_ARGS` and `ATLAS_ALL_DIRS` instead of a hardcoded resume payload.
Why: Remaining onboarding gaps were long script/module command chains for distributed runs, report/master export, debug triage, artifact measurement, and reproducibility indexing.
Impact: Public CLI surface expanded. Slurm submission now caps `--cpus-per-task` at 32 through the wrapper and writes reproducibility indexes under existing `outputs/data/<RUN_ID>/` rather than a new top-level output tree.
Next: Run the full canonical gate once existing vulture/Xenon/mypy debt is either baselined or retired; site owners should confirm the default Slurm template parameters still match their queue policy.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: MMGBSA OpenMM tiny/canary validation | Owner: Codex
Change: Ran 1O2Q/991 tiny-MD and 0.5 ns canary through OpenMM/HIP MD, restart writing, cpptraj stripping, and MMPBSA aggregation. Stabilized tiny/canary presets with shorter timestep and ramped heating, isolated validation-tier replicate directories, propagated tier frame overrides into aggregation, and added serial MMPBSA fallback when `MMPBSA.py.MPI` fails only because `mpi4py` is missing.
Why: The original fast canaries either overwrote production-style replicate directories, heated too aggressively, or failed at MMPBSA before analysis because the AmberTools MPI Python wrapper was incomplete.
Impact: Tiny-MD now completes over 4 MMPBSA frames with delta_total -20.9468 kcal/mol; 0.5 ns canary completes over 50 frames with delta_total -20.6502 kcal/mol. The fallback keeps validation usable but serial MMPBSA is slower than a correctly configured `mpi4py`/MPI AmberTools environment.
Next: Install or rebuild AmberTools MPI with matching `mpi4py` for parallel MMPBSA, then promote the canary tier into the normal MMGBSA release gate before longer Wang-style benchmark runs.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Wang-lite MMGBSA gate | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a `wang-lite` staged validation tier, explicit-solvent cpptraj RMSD/RMSF QC artifacts, and `wang-lite-report` aggregation with Wang family target scaffolding. Ran 1O2Q/991_A246 through 1 ns OpenMM/HIP MD, cpptraj stripping/QC, and 100-frame MMPBSA aggregation.
Why: Full Wang-style 10 ns x replicate jobs are too slow for development, but the pipeline needs a realistic intermediate gate that exercises explicit solvent, trajectory QC, convergence plots, and Wang-family comparison outputs.
Impact: The Wang-lite gate report is under `outputs/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016_atlas_redock_20260505_a/wang_lite_gate_20260509/`. The completed 1O2Q result has frame QC pass with delta_total 0.7305 kcal/mol, SEM 0.163, and 100 frames. This is functional validation only; the shift from the earlier 0.5 ns canary value shows the current redocked/control setup is not yet scientifically Wang-comparable.
Next: Add working AmberTools MPI/mpi4py for parallel MMPBSA, run at least two more completed representative controls, and compare crystal-pose versus redocked-pose Wang-lite results before scheduling 10 ns x replicate publication runs.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Wang MMGBSA parity scaffolding | Owner: Codex
Change: Added PMC proof-of-work handling for the Wang 2016 supplement downloader, preferred the Atlas-local `ambertools-mpi` prefix, added Wang static representative/convergence targets, added crystal-pose report generation from PDBbind MOL2/SDF sources, added ligand-centroid active-site center fallback for crystal-only MMGBSA staging, and added combined Wang-lite reporting across redocked and crystal staged manifests.
Why: The previous Wang gate could not fetch the official supplement, could still fall back to serial MMPBSA, and could not produce a direct crystal-pose versus redocked-pose comparison.
Impact: `MMPBSA.py.MPI` now resolves from `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools/envs/ambertools-mpi` and completed 1O2Q aggregation with 8 MPI ranks. The combined report at `outputs/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016_atlas_redock_20260505_a/wang_lite_gate_20260509_combined/` shows 1O2Q crystal-pose delta_total -39.1746 kcal/mol versus redocked delta_total 0.7305 kcal/mol over 100 frames each, a 39.9051 kcal/mol redocked-minus-crystal shift. 1LPZ and 2J75/1VZQ redocked controls remain rejected by MD/QC failures rather than silently promoted.
Next: Run more crystal-pose controls and then redock-pose repairs before scheduling 10 ns x 3 production replicates; the Wang supplement contains family/table metrics, not full per-complex MMPBSA rows for exact reproduction.
Date: 2026-05-10 | Task: Wang MMGBSA tier config consistency | Owner: Codex
Change: Propagated validation-tier config overrides into `stage_from_report` before report preparation and ligand discovery, so Wang-lite and production staging use the same water retention, TIP3P box, frame stride, and MMPBSA settings during topology preparation as they use during MD/MMPBSA job generation.
Why: The prior staged manifests recorded Wang-like water defaults, but the prepare call could still consume the base config before those overrides were applied.
Impact: Future Wang-lite/production staging is internally consistent; already-completed 1O2Q crystal/redocked results remain valid as a functional comparison but should be regenerated if exact water-radius provenance is needed.
Next: Restage additional Wang controls after receptor/pose repairs, then run the production tier when a release-grade 10 ns x 3 replicate parity gate is required.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: SPD prep-only target panel and ML leakage audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a one-command ML leakage/overlap audit CLI (`atlas.analysis.cli.audit_ml_leakage_overlap`) and staged a one-PDB-per-SPD-target prep-only pilot under `data/spd_prep_demo/`; summarized no-docking prep/control-redocking results in `data/spd_prep_demo/run_audit_spd_prep_only_20260509/`.
Why: The SPD expansion needs a scalable preflight before full docking and a repeatable way to verify drug/target/scaffold/source holdout leakage at larger scale.
Impact: Current SPD pilot has 75 downloadable representative PDBs for 101 representative human SPD genes; 50/75 prepared, 25 failed receptor PDBQT generation, 44/50 successful preps used control-centered active sites, and 6/50 fell back due missing prepped controls. SPD exposure ML audit flags `free_cmax_um` as label-definition leakage unless used only in a separate exposure-complete/sensitivity model.
Next: Fix receptor prep failures or select alternate PDBs for the 25 failed targets, add scaffold/chemotype columns before scaffold holdout claims, suppress post-docking report/SCORCH hooks for no-docking runs, and rerun the leakage audit after the full SPD panel is staged.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: SPD no-docking cleanup and chemotype/scaffold holdout | Owner: Codex
Change: Suppressed SCORCH/master/report/throughput/post-check hooks for NO_LIBRARY_DOCKING, added ligand_chemotype/scaffold_key backfill to source benchmark tables, and audited SPD fallback active-site ligands/receptor failures.
Why: No-docking prep runs should not emit expected no-heatmap/no-consensus errors, and SPD scaffold holdout needed a chemotype proxy.
Impact: No-docking smoke now exits without SCORCH/report/throughput warnings; current SPD table has 498/498 chemotype labels and scaffold_holdout passes but remains coarse due 428 Other/Unassigned rows. Failed PDBs are mostly Meeko/RDKit HET/valence/metal export problems.
Next: Tighten PDB selection to require a dockable pharmacologic control ligand, repair/strip problematic receptor HET export fallbacks or choose alternate PDBs for 25 failures, and replace chemotype proxy with true Murcko scaffolds from SMILES.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: SPD exposure leakage guardrails and scaffold fixes | Owner: Codex
Change: Added label-definition feature exclusion for SPD exposure labels, explicit PK-sensitivity opt-in flags, Murcko scaffold-key assignment from `chemdb/data/fda_mapping_from_pdbqt.csv`, scaffold_key-based scaffold holdouts, and default PDB-query glycan exclusion from nontrivial ligand selection.
Why: `free_cmax_um`/Cmax/fraction-unbound define the SPD exposure label and must not enter the primary classifier; scaffold holdouts should use structural scaffolds rather than broad chemotype labels; NAG/MAN/FUC glycan decorations should not satisfy the default active-site control-ligand requirement.
Impact: Primary SPD model-ready table auto-excludes AC50/free-Cmax/margin fields and has 498 rows with 170 positives/328 negatives. Explicit `--allow-label-definition-features` enables a named PK-sensitivity table. The current SPD table now has 0 `other-unassigned` scaffold keys, and scaffold_holdout uses `scaffold_key` with zero scaffold overlap.
Next: Re-run SPD PDB selection/prep after glycan exclusion so fallback-centered structures are replaced where possible, and keep PK-containing SPD performance separate from the nonleaky primary exposure model in reports/manuscript tables.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Protein benchmark target parallelization | Owner: Codex
Change: Split the protein-prep benchmark CLI into `src/protein_prep/benchmark/cli.py` and added PDB-level `--workers` / `--parallel-backend` execution with process workers by default, order-preserving summary output, and automatic serial fallback for redocking/publication/AutoDock4Zn modes.
Why: The 25 failed receptor-prep targets were being processed serially; each target spends substantial time in PDB2PQR/Reduce/Meeko recovery, so independent target-level concurrency is the cleanest speedup without changing receptor chemistry.
Impact: A two-target process-parallel smoke completed and the 25-target failed set ran to completion with `--workers 8`, writing `/stor/home/mpg2352/protein_prep_failed25_parallel_20260509/summary.csv`. Scheduler behavior is fixed, but 17/25 targets still fail validity or Meeko export and need receptor chemistry/template cleanup.
Next: Use the parallel benchmark output to prioritize the remaining Meeko/RDKit valence, residue-template, and unexpected metal-bound-HET removal failures.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: BANANA binding expert wiring | Owner: Codex
Change: Added BANANA/BigBind SMILES input staging, optional BANANA inference runner, a binding-expert table builder, and MoE support for a separate binding/activity expert input.
Why: BANANA scores should be treated as a binding/activity expert in the Atlas MoE, with Atlas/consensus/SCORCH/final scores acting as complementary calibration layers rather than labels.
Impact: Pilot MoE rows now have FDA SMILES coverage in `data/pilotstudy/moe_experts/banana_binding_inputs.csv`; no BANANA scores are present yet because pocket PDB inputs are not staged, so `data/pilotstudy/moe_experts/banana_binding_expert.csv` uses explicit Atlas/consensus fallback provenance. Rebuilt MoE output is `data/pilotstudy/moe_experts/moe_adr_prioritization_with_banana_binding.csv`.
Next: Generate a PDB pocket-residue map for Atlas structures, install BANANA runtime dependencies if needed, run BANANA inference, then compare BANANA-only, Atlas-only, and BANANA+Atlas binding-expert enrichment/calibration.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: BANANA pocket-map generation | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas.analysis.cli.build_banana_pocket_map` and reusable pocket extraction code that downloads missing RCSB PDBs, detects co-crystal ligand centers, writes pocket-residue PDB files, and emits a `pdb_id,pocket_pdb` map.
Why: BANANA inference needs both ligand SMILES and a pocket-residue PDB per structure; the earlier integration had complete SMILES coverage but no pocket map.
Impact: The pilot MoE now has complete pocket coverage: `data/pilotstudy/banana_pockets/pilot_banana_pocket_map.csv` has 29/29 ready PDBs and `data/pilotstudy/moe_experts/banana_binding_inputs.csv` has 7,018/7,018 ready rows. Three locally cleaned receptors use raw RCSB co-crystal ligand centers because the cleaned receptor had stripped the ligand.
Next: Install or isolate BANANA runtime dependencies (`torch`, `dgl`, `dgllife`, `terrace`, `omegaconf`, etc.), run `atlas.analysis.cli.run_banana_inference`, rebuild the binding expert with BANANA scores, and compare BANANA-only versus Atlas/consensus fallback performance.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Protein prep failed-25 Meeko recovery | Owner: Codex
Change: Added Meeko-safe protein-only/template-pruned/clash-pruned receptor export fallback, same-residue false-bond pruning, metal-bound HET rescue, binding-site-aware geometry validity, and a `--skip-water-analysis` benchmark flag for prep-only smoke runs.
Why: The 25 failed receptors were dominated by Meeko/RDKit valence and residue-template errors from retained HET/metals, malformed local sidechains, peptide-like HET caps, and compacted Meeko errors that previously hid residue keys needed for automated pruning.
Impact: The failed-25 prep-only benchmark now reports 25/25 `structure_valid` and 25/25 `meeko_ok` without losing metals or unexpected metal-bound HETs. Receptors using `protein_only_sanitized_retry` remain review-labeled because atoms/residues were pruned for parser compatibility; `--skip-water-analysis` proves receptor export only and does not validate water-policy claims.
Next: Use full waterbench separately for water-policy claims, and manually review high dropped-residue targets before publication when the dropped residues are binding-site relevant.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: BANANA-backed pilot MoE binding training | Owner: Codex
Change: Installed an isolated BANANA runtime under `.envs/banana`, ran BANANA inference for all 7,018 pilot MoE rows, rebuilt the BANANA binding expert and MoE evidence-combination table, added `banana_only` and `banana_atlas_binding` ML feature sets, and trained drug-holdout logistic models on the four-state activity table.
Why: The binding/activity expert should use BANANA as a BigBind-trained binding prior while preserving Atlas/consensus scores as complementary, nonlabel features.
Impact: Pilot BANANA scoring is complete. BANANA-only improved four-state binding/activity AUROC/AUPRC over Atlas/consensus on the current drug-holdout split, but the label mix is source-imbalanced and should not be treated as a clinical ADR claim.
Next: For publishable MoE, expand the SPD exposure panel, improve ADR/mechanism labels with more strict positives/controls, calibrate experts per source, and validate the final MoE against held-out exposure/mechanism endpoints.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Source-label specification and BANANA+SCORCH sensitivity | Owner: Codex
Change: Added machine-readable source label-policy/specification exports, explicit SCORCH partial-rescoring opt-in for ML training/audits, recovered pilot SCORCH/final score columns into the current repo, built a SCORCH-rescored subset training table, and trained Atlas/consensus, BANANA-only, SCORCH-only, and BANANA+SCORCH comparisons.
Why: The binding expert should test SCORCH/final scores without letting the model learn top-tranche SCORCH availability, and new label sources must be blocked from production ML until file headers, license, upstream lineage, and inactive-row semantics are verified.
Impact: The current SCORCH-overlap subset has 114 rows and 100 supervised labels, so it is a sensitivity result only. `data/pilotstudy/source_label_spec/` now records source priority, conflict rules, expert routing, minimum ingestion schema, and do-not-ingest-yet blockers for the deep-research source list.
Next: Rerun SCORCH on the full/full-SPD Atlas panel or at least a much larger target-ligand tranche, then regenerate the SCORCH subset table; stage raw source files under current-repo `data/external/` before enabling any new source for training.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Atlas shim HPC portability | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended the installer-generated `atlas` shim to honor runtime `ATLAS_ENV_PREFIX`, `ATLAS_ENV_NAME`, `ATLAS_ENV_MANAGER`, `MAMBA_EXE`, `CONDA_EXE`, and `MAMBA_ROOT_PREFIX` overrides while still auto-selecting a writable PATH directory or `ATLAS_SHIM_DIR`.
Why: HPC systems often separate HOME, work, and scratch storage and expose micromamba/conda through modules, so a shim tied to one manager path or one env prefix would break after moving the repo or changing machines.
Impact: Users can keep calling `atlas ...` without manual activation when the shim directory is on `PATH`; cluster-specific layouts can point the shim at the right env or manager without editing the generated file. Moving the editable repository still requires rerunning the installer or `pip install -e .` from the new checkout.
Next: If a target cluster has a standard module name, document the matching `module load ...` plus `ATLAS_ENV_PREFIX=...` snippet in the site install notes.
Date: 2026-05-09 | Task: Atlas shim config simplification | Owner: Codex
Change: Reduced the public shim override surface to `ATLAS_SHIM_DIR` for the command location and `ATLAS_ENV_PREFIX` for scratch/work-hosted envs; manager selection now stays automatic through loaded `micromamba`/`conda` commands.
Why: Exposing manager-specific variables made the onboarding path look more configurable than it needs to be and increased cognitive load for nontechnical users.
Impact: Standard installs need no extra variables, HPC installs usually need at most one of the two public overrides, and sites that expose micromamba/conda through modules can keep using normal module loading.
Next: Keep future install docs centered on zero-config install first, then the two-variable HPC escape hatch only when needed.

Impact: ADReCS-Target contributes target safety evidence as positive-only `target_has_safety_evidence`; mechanism labels now contain 187 positives and 616 source-balanced negatives for training. New outputs live under `data/pilotstudy/mechanism_graph_deep_sources/`, `data/pilotstudy/negative_evidence/*deep_sources*`, and `data/pilotstudy/moe_experts/*deep_sources*`.
Next: Add verified PubChem BioAssay/TTD/family-panel/cardiac-panel exports when available, keep OMOP/FAERS as calibration/sensitivity controls, and expand the SPD panel before making publishable exposure or final MoE claims.

Date: 2026-05-10 | Task: MMGBSA OpenMM heat rescue | Owner: Agent
Change: Added a heat-failure rescue branch for explicit-solvent MMGBSA MD that re-runs conservative relaxation and retries heating with a lower timestep, longer ramp, and stronger restraints before continuing density/equilibration/production.
Why: Wang-style 1O2Q replicate 3 passed minimization and preheat QC but intermittently failed OpenMM/HIP heating with NaN energy.
Impact: Failed heat stages can now be recovered automatically when the structure is salvageable; production results still record rescue metadata and failed rescues remain excluded.
Next: Review completed rep3 aggregate after the detached MD job finishes and decide whether to promote the heat-rescue defaults into the publication preset documentation.
Date: 2026-05-10 | Task: MMGBSA publication equilibration protocol | Owner: Agent
Change: Made existing production/publication MMGBSA explicit MD use conservative heavy-solute restraints, staged heating, gradual restraint release, close-contact preheat QC, and one solvent/ion rebuild retry after heat failure.
Why: Publication-grade MMGBSA should avoid relying on a short generic heat step and should prevent unstable solvated systems before production rather than only rescuing NaN failures.
Impact: `MMGBSA_PROTOCOL=production|publication` and staged `validation_tier=production` now use the more conservative OpenMM workflow without adding new config keys; screening/tiny/canary paths keep the faster sequence.
Next: Let an end-to-end production replicate complete under the new protocol and compare rescued/rebuilt replicate QC against non-rescued replicates before treating the settings as finalized.

Why: Rep1/rep2 were produced under the prior equilibration protocol, so restarting all replicates is the cleaner baseline for comparing the new publication protocol.
Impact: Existing rep1/rep2/rep3 Wang 1O2Q production outputs will be overwritten by the new forced run; only one OpenMM GPU MD job runs at a time.
Next: Check `publication_protocol_all_reps.log` and the aggregate summary after PID 3100031 exits.

Date: 2026-05-10 | Task: ML benchmark independence audit | Owner: Codex

Date: 2026-05-10 | Task: Protein prep 366-target stress run and control-redock cap | Owner: Agent

Date: 2026-05-10 | Task: HIP/OpenMM visibility fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated staged OpenMM job config to pass the assigned GPU token into MMGBSA OpenMM device index, and aligned OpenMM/launch env visibility via `HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, `ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, `GPU_DEVICE_ORDINAL`, and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`.
