Date: 2026-05-10 | Task: SPD strict target install consistency fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Added mmCIF fallback support in `tools/massinstall.py` for ligand-bound PDB downloads (try `.pdb`, fallback `.cif` + `gemmi` conversion), then re-ran strict one-PDB-per-target SPD target install in-place. Re-synced strict SPD manifest artifacts so “missing/unavailable” outputs are derived directly from actual download status.
Why: Some SPD targets (e.g., 9EHS/9I52/9LLG/9UWI/8VBY) had valid structures only as CIF, and stale artifact files previously overstated missing entries.
Impact: SPD strict coverage is now 76/101 unique SPD target genes with valid non-trivial holo structures downloaded as `.pdb` in `input_pdbs/spd_all_targets_strict_no_additive`; 25 targets remain unresolved due zero non-trivial cofactor hits under strict filtering.
Next: Decide whether to add a “relaxed” follow-up mode that accepts broader ligands/cofactors for the remaining 25, while preserving a strict clinical-mode audit track.

Date: 2026-05-10 | Task: SPD assay target normalization fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Hardened SPD target-id extraction in `analysis.ml.spd_panel` so assay strings like `ABCB11 INHIBITION` no longer leak into manifest `target_id`; added regression test `test_spd_assay_text_normalizes_target_id` in `chemdb/tests/test_atlas_analysis_expanded.py`.
Why: Previous parsing used broad string fallbacks and could retain assay-activity suffixes from source metadata, causing mismatched target manifests and inflated strict/relaxed target maps.
Impact: `build_spd_target_assay_manifest` now emits compact gene tokens (e.g., `ABCB11`, `ADRA1A`, `AR`) from the local SPD assay file, with parenthetical/cofactor terms stripped. A manifest built from `data/external/spd/sutherland_2023_spd_supplementary_data_1_15.xlsx` now reports 101 target IDs and zero `target_id` values containing spaces.
Next: Rebuild final SPD manifests after any future assay metadata refresh and keep this normalization behavior behind a single parser helper so future source-format changes are one-line audited.

Date: 2026-05-11 | Task: Wang Crystal MMGBSA Production Launch | Owner: Codex
Change: Staged Wang crystal-pose MMGBSA production jobs under `outputs/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016_crystal_controls_prod_20260511_a/`; 5 controls were evaluated for readiness, 4 were stage-ready (`1LPZ`, `1O2Q`, `1VZQ`, `2J75`), and `1Q8W` failed dry topology generation at tleap. Initial 3-GPU launch failed on HIP device token 1, so the remaining production queue was relaunched as single-GPU device 0 in tmux session `wang_crystal_prod_remaining_20260511`, pinned to CPUs `0-31`, running `1O2Q`, `1VZQ`, and `2J75` sequentially with 3 replicates each.
Why: User requested crystal-pose Wang/PDBbind controls, replicate production protocol, QC outputs, and relative comparison scaffolding without exceeding 32 cores. Multi-GPU HIP visibility was not reliable on this node, and generated staged runners stop on first per-job failure.
Impact: The active queue should produce production MD, stripped trajectories, MMPBSA aggregation, convergence/QC artifacts, and replicate summaries for as many of the remaining three controls as pass preheat QC. `1LPZ` crystal-pose rep1 failed preheat QC with severe close contacts and extreme minimized energy/GMAX even after rescue; it should be treated as a structural-prep blocker, not a completed benchmark point.
Next: Check `outputs/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016_crystal_controls_prod_20260511_a/staged_production_single_gpu/wang_crystal_prod_remaining_tmux.log`, then aggregate successful summaries and compare crystal-pose versus existing redocked-pose results. Investigate `1Q8W` tleap failure and `1LPZ` close contacts before using either in Wang parity claims.

Date: 2026-05-11 | Task: OpenMM HIP Multi-GPU Fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Fixed staged OpenMM/HIP GPU isolation by using Linux-recommended `ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES` as the physical GPU mask and OpenMM logical `DeviceIndex=0` inside each masked worker. Removed the previous staged-worker setting of `HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, `GPU_DEVICE_ORDINAL`, and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`, and stopped `mmgbsa_openmm.py` from rewriting GPU visibility variables from the OpenMM device index. Regenerated Wang production helper behavior so masked OpenMM jobs default to logical device 0. Cancelled the older single-GPU Wang queue and launched `wang_crystal_prod_multigpu_fixed_20260511`, using the staged 3-GPU jobs for `1O2Q`, `1VZQ`, and `2J75` with max parallelism 3 and CPU affinity `0-31`.
Why: On this MI100 Linux node, setting all HIP/CUDA compatibility visibility variables to a nonzero token caused `hipErrorNoDevice`; direct probes showed `ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES=1|2|3` plus OpenMM `DeviceIndex=0` works. ROCm documentation recommends `ROCR_VISIBLE_DEVICES` for Linux GPU isolation.
Impact: Atlas can now run independent staged OpenMM MMGBSA MD jobs concurrently across multiple AMD GPUs. `rocm-smi` confirmed three MI100s busy while the fixed Wang queue was running, and active workers were pinned to CPUs `0-31`.
Next: Monitor `outputs/mmgbsa_benchmarks/wang2016_crystal_controls_prod_20260511_a/staged_production/wang_crystal_prod_multigpu_fixed_tmux.log` for per-replicate structural QC failures and final MMPBSA aggregation.
Date: 2026-05-11 | Task: Modular ML leakage audit and current MoE retraining | Owner: Codex
Change: Added reusable `analysis.ml.audit_utils`, new `analysis.ml.audit_suite`, and CLI `analysis.cli.run_ml_audit_suite`; expanded leakage audits with feature missingness, source label balance, high-risk column scans, semicolon-aware source holdouts, and train-only applicability-domain reporting in `train_classifier`.
Why: The prior ML audits were split across separate scripts and did not provide one practical pass-level command after each label/model rebuild. Applicability-domain reporting is a standard external-validation guardrail for chemistry/biology ML and was not first-class in model outputs.
Impact: Existing audit CLIs still work, while `run_ml_audit_suite` can be run after every data pass. New model predictions include `applicability_distance` and `applicability_domain`; model directories also include `model_applicability_domain_summary.csv`.
Next: Use the audit suite on final full-SPD and mechanism-label tables before any publication claim, and treat source-holdout failures/large source prevalence shifts as reporting caveats rather than silent pass conditions.

Date: 2026-05-12 | Task: AGENTS ML audit workflow and ADR/DTI precedent review | Owner: Codex
Change: Added an `ML` section to `AGENTS.md` requiring `python -m analysis.cli.run_ml_audit_suite` after every ML data ingestion, label-collapse, feature-table rebuild, or model-training pass.
Why: Future agents need the leakage/source-balance/applicability-domain audit command at the repo instruction level, not only in a chat transcript.
Impact: ML work now has a standing post-pass audit checklist. Source holdout remains conditional on at least two label sources, and audit failures should be treated as caveats or blockers.
Next: Use ADRtarget/DTIAM/MoseDTI/EnsDTI-style precedent review to add explicit ADR-target panel tasks only after source licensing, label semantics, and train/test independence are verified.

Date: 2026-05-12 | Task: Paper-source DTI/ADR ingestion and MoE retraining | Owner: Codex
Change: Added paper-source DTI staging for ADRtarget/T-ARDIS/DTIAM/MoseDTI/EnsDTI downloads, added SIDER/openFDA positive drug-ADR ingestion to the four-state builder, rebuilt `data/pilotstudy/four_state_evidence_paper_sources_v8/`, rebuilt mechanism and ADR-panel tables, and retrained binding, SCORCH-subset binding, SPD exposure, mechanism, and ADR-panel models under `data/pilotstudy/ml_rerun_20260512/`.
Why: The ML pipeline needed paper-precedent sources wired into the same four-state provenance/collapse rules rather than treated as independent ad hoc labels.
Impact: EnsDTI contributed 44 mappable DTI rows; ADRtarget/T-ARDIS/DTIAM/MoseDTI were downloaded/audited but did not provide usable current Atlas-overlap pair-label tables. SIDER/openFDA added many drug-ADR positives, but only 112 drug-ADR rows currently overlap Atlas pilot pharmacology features, so the ADR-panel model remains calibration/control-grade.
Next: For publishable ADR mechanism ML, expand Atlas feature overlap with the full SPD/ADR target set and add stricter target-ADR controls or keep the primary claim PU/matched-background with ADR-panel calibration as sensitivity evidence.
Date: 2026-05-12 | Task: Paper DTI/ADR source ingestion v9 | Owner: Codex
Change: Replaced stale ADRtarget/T-ARDIS/DTIAM placeholder statuses with source-specific parsers in `analysis.external.paper_dti_sources`, added CLI flags for DocTOR and DTIAM GitHub roots, made ADReCS target graph loading accept TSV/CSV inputs, and fixed ML audit data-card positive-rate reporting to use observed labels only.
Why: ADRtarget paper supplements, DocTOR/T-ARDIS seed rows, and DTIAM GitHub MoA files were present locally but were not being converted into Atlas evidence rows, so mechanism/ADR training was under-reporting usable paper-linked evidence.
Impact: New normalized outputs live under `data/external/paper_sources/normalized_v2/`: ADRtarget adds 220 target-ADR positives, DocTOR/T-ARDIS adds 434 seed target-ADR positives, DTIAM adds 65 mapped DTI positives, and EnsDTI remains 44 mapped rows. Rebuilt v9 outputs under `data/pilotstudy/four_state_evidence_paper_sources_v9/`, `data/pilotstudy/mechanism_graph_paper_sources_v9/`, `data/pilotstudy/negative_evidence/mechanism_four_state_labels_paper_sources_v9*.csv`, `data/pilotstudy/adr_mechanism_panel/adr_mechanism_panel_paper_sources_v9.csv`, and `data/pilotstudy/ml_rerun_20260512_v9/`. Leakage audits pass requested split checks, but ADR/mechanism supervised claims remain limited by small Atlas-feature-overlapping positives and non-causal negative controls.
Next: Proceed with full SPD docking for exposure expert scale. For publication-grade ADR linkage, add more drug-ADR/target-ADR overlap or keep ADR mechanism claims as PU/matched-background plus calibration/control analyses rather than a definitive supervised target-causality classifier.

Date: 2026-05-12 | Task: Report statistics and ADR audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added Vina-fallback target-level decoy FDR export, added statistics/formula rendering to Vina fallback report HTML, split heatmap threshold controls into full FDR q, SCORCH q +1, and SCORCH relaxed q, and added local target-ADR fallback metadata from staged ADReCS/OpenTargets/paper-source TSVs when the Open Targets cache is absent.
Why: Older Vina fallback reports contained high-scoring protein-ligand rows but no FDR fields or statistical section, so significant rows could not be flagged in report.html. ADR search also depended on a missing target_safety_aggregated.json cache despite local target-ADR evidence being staged.
Impact: `analysis.reporting.run_report_core` now writes `target_fdr_summary.csv` and FDR columns for `master_rows_vina.csv` before heatmap export. Regenerated fallback reports expose the same code-wired formulas as full reports and provide explicit relaxed-vs-corrected SCORCH threshold modes. Target ADR terms are available from local staged evidence without live web calls when UniProt/gene symbols are available.
Next: For publication reports, prefer master/SCORCH exports over Vina fallback; run target/ligand cache refresh when network access is desired for Open Targets/openFDA enrichment.

Date: 2026-05-12 | Task: HOLO pH protein-prep stress rerun stabilization | Owner: Codex
Change: Made altloc filtering model-aware and order-preserving for multi-model/NMR PDBs, serialized local pH sibling prep by default, and downgraded recoverable active-site fallback misses from error to warning.
Why: The large HOLO+pH no-docking rerun exposed two prep reliability bugs: global altloc sorting collapsed MODEL blocks for NMR entries, and concurrent pH siblings shared prep/control artifacts and could truncate intermediate PDBs.
Impact: Multi-model entries such as 1UJL/2L27/2LLY now retain atoms through element fixing, and default pH ensemble execution avoids same-PDB/variant filesystem races. Future runs should no longer count recoverable missing control-center fallbacks as failed prep errors.
Next: Monitor `spd_relaxed_no_docking_95_fullinput_holo_ph_patch2_20260512`; if real failed proteins appear, classify by artifact stage and patch the next root cause before relaunching.

Date: 2026-05-12 | Task: Protein-prep stress summary HOLO/pH layout support | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated `tools/protein_prep_stress_summary.py` to prefer variant-scoped output directories such as `PDB/HOLO/receptor`, count extracted control ligands, and report `control_ligand_missing` separately from missing geometry sidecars.
Why: The HOLO+pH stress run completed successfully, but the summary tool only checked legacy `PDB/receptor` paths and misclassified all targets as missing cleaned receptors; no-ligand targets were also being reported as geometry-audit misses.
Impact: Stress summaries now match variant-scoped production outputs and make active-site-control limitations explicit. The completed run `spd_relaxed_no_docking_95_fullinput_holo_ph_patch2_20260512` has 90/90 manifest completion, with stricter summary gates reporting 44 passed, 37 residual geometry-clash audits, and 9 missing extracted control ligands.
Next: Decide whether residual clash audits should remain publication-review gates or trigger a targeted geometry-repair rerun for the 37 affected ligand-bearing targets.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Residual geometry clash escalation policy | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a post-final-geometry residual clash escalation in `protein_prep.geometry.constructive`: residual clashes are classified against all extracted control ligands, off-site mild clashes become `offsite_geometry_review`, and severe or binding-site clashes trigger a restrained minimization attempt before final audit/reporting. The stress summary now reads `*_geometry_residual_escalation.json` and treats off-site review clashes as passable while keeping severe/binding-site clashes as hard geometry failures.
Why: The 90-target HOLO+pH stress run had no prep/export failures, but 37 ligand-bearing targets retained raw non-ignored clash annotations. Most were far outside the control-ligand site, while a small subset required true repair or exclusion.
Impact: Future protein prep runs preserve raw clash counts, avoid deleting backbone/resolved sidechains, keep Meeko/metal/control-ligand gates hard, and avoid failing otherwise screenable targets for off-site mild clashes. Existing run classification predicts 32 off-site review targets, 3 binding-site review targets, and 2 severe review targets before rerun.
Next: Rerun the HOLO+pH stress or a 37-target subset to produce new residual-escalation sidecars and confirm Meeko export/metal retention remain unchanged after any accepted minimization repairs.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Automatic off-site residual clash minimization | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated the residual geometry escalation so off-site residual clashes also trigger bounded restrained OpenMM minimization instead of being labeled first. Candidate repairs are accepted only when the residual gate does not worsen, binding-site/severe blocker counts do not increase, and total residual clashes improve.
Why: Standard virtual-screening prep guidance includes restrained protein minimization followed by repeated quality control; Atlas should try to resolve even off-site clashes automatically while preserving conservative acceptance rules.
Impact: Off-site mild clashes remain screenable review states if minimization cannot improve them, but future prep runs now attempt to remove them. A smoke check on 1B41 resolved the off-site residual clash to `ok`; a 6W25 smoke check improved the severe clash state to binding-site review but still correctly left it as a hard blocker.
Next: Run a targeted 37-protein geometry rerun or full HOLO+pH stress rerun to quantify how many residual clashes are fully removed and verify no Meeko/metal/control-ligand regressions.

Date: 2026-05-12 | Task: Pilotstudy report statistics/ADR audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Staged real `pilotstudy` report artifacts into the current repo, copied Clustergrammer `report_assets`, regenerated `data/pilotstudy/report.html`, and added target-name backfill in `analysis.reporting.run_report_core` so reruns preserve existing target labels when protected `input_pdbs/` sources are absent.
Why: The meaningful study is `pilotstudy`, not `bench2`; the current repo lacked the pilot report inputs/assets, so regenerated reports could either hang/unrender the heatmap or blank target names when source PDBs were not present.
Impact: `data/pilotstudy/report.html` now opens standalone in headless Chromium with no JS errors, contains statistical formulas/FDR/enrichment/ablation sections, exposes full FDR q, SCORCH q +1, and SCORCH relaxed controls, and preserves ADR search metadata such as hallucination terms for 6CM4/7WC8 target states. Eight displayed pilot rows still use unresolved `rdk_*` ligand names because the current FDA mapping has no better name for those two ligand bases.
Next: Use `python -m analysis.cli.run_report --run-id pilotstudy --repo-root . --combined --fda-mapping-csv chemdb/data/fda_mapping_from_pdbqt.csv --html-path data/pilotstudy/report.html --heatmap-csv-path data/pilotstudy/heatmap_input.csv --emit-html --emit-heatmap-csv --no-emit-pathway-html --report-asset-mode inline` for future pilot report refreshes in this repo.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Pilotstudy side-effect match overlay fix | Owner: Codex
Change: Restored the aggregate ligand openFDA side-effect cache under `pathways/cache/`, fixed ligand report metadata indexing to use `ligand_display` and `ligand_base` when `ligand_name` is absent, and widened the side-effect overlap JavaScript to compare `side_effects`, `safety_evidence_terms`, adverse-event examples, safety buckets, and drug AE buckets.
Why: The pilot report had target ADR terms embedded, but the "Highlight Side-Effect Matches" button only searched `side_effects` and row metadata had zero ligand side-effect terms, so the overlay reported no matches despite known target/ligand side-effect evidence.
Impact: Regenerated `data/pilotstudy/report.html` now reports 138 highlighted protein-drug pairs at the default 1% threshold in headless Chromium, with no browser JS errors. Matches include literal AE terms and broad bucket terms, so this remains an exploratory ADR concordance overlay rather than a supervised label.
Next: If the overlay is too broad for manuscript figures, add a UI toggle to require exact AE-term matches and exclude broad safety buckets such as CNS/GI/Cardiotoxicity.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Report-time ligand side-effect cache preflight | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `run_report` ligand side-effect cache preflight flags and wired the report HTML path to auto-refresh the openFDA ligand side-effect cache when it is missing or below a minimum hit count. Also fixed `--no-emit-heatmap-csv --heatmap-csv-path` so HTML generation uses the explicit heatmap CSV instead of silently falling back to master rows.
Why: The pilot report side-effect overlay depended on a repo-local cache that could be absent after moving artifacts, and the explicit heatmap CSV override was ignored in no-emit mode.
Impact: Future `analysis.cli.run_report`/`atlas analysis report` runs should regenerate the ligand side-effect cache if it is missing while still writing the report if openFDA refresh fails. The regenerated pilot report keeps the expected 138 side-effect matches and 138 highlighted tiles.
Next: Consider adding a separate artifact-prune command for `data/<run_id>/` that moves superseded ML/debug directories to `data/<run_id>/artifacts/archive/` without changing canonical report filenames.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Short side-effect cache flags and unified ML pass output | Owner: Codex
Change: Renamed report cache controls to `--side-effect-cache*` flags while keeping the older `--ligand-side-effect-cache*` aliases hidden/backward-compatible. The same cache preflight now covers ligand openFDA side effects and target/PDB safety evidence. Added `analysis.cli.run_ml_training_pass`, a single-output-dir ML wrapper for binding, exposure, and mechanism experts, and removed pilotstudy-specific defaults from BANANA pocket-map and paper-DTI staging CLIs.
Why: Future SPD-scale runs need cache behavior that is easier to invoke and ML outputs that can be pointed at one run-specific directory instead of scattering repeated rerun folders under `data/pilotstudy/`.
Impact: `python -m analysis.cli.run_ml_training_pass --out-dir <dir>` can train the default three experts from `data/<run_id>/` and place model/audit artifacts under `<dir>`. BANANA pocket generation now defaults raw/pocket folders to siblings of the requested output map.
Next: For the full SPD run, use a fresh run directory and call `run_ml_training_pass --out-dir data/<spd_run>/ml_training_pass --run-id <spd_run> --run-ablations` after docking/scoring tables are staged.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: HOLO pH geometry-fix stress rerun | Owner: Codex
Change: Ran `spd_relaxed_no_docking_95_fullinput_holo_ph_geomfix_20260513` across the 90-target set with residual geometry escalation active. The run completed 90/90 proteins and 125/125 pH/variant stage entries with zero prep failures and zero error signatures; stress summary reported 81 strict passes and 9 `control_ligand_missing` failures.
Why: The geometry escalation needed objective validation under the same HOLO+pH no-docking stress conditions that exposed the original residual clash annotations.
Impact: Severe and binding-site geometry blockers were eliminated in the 90-target rerun. Remaining strict failures are no-control-ligand targets, not receptor-prep crashes. Nine ligand-bearing targets retain off-site review clashes after standard minimization, so they are screenable with disclosure but not raw-clash clean.
Next: Do not run relaxed off-site minimization by default; an experimental 9-target relaxed protocol was stopped after about 26 minutes with only 3 prep entries completed. Keep `ATLAS_GEOMETRY_RELAXED_MINIMIZATION=1` as an opt-in expensive cleanup path until a faster local/rotamer repair backend is added.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Moderate residual geometry polish pass | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a default second-chance residual geometry polish pass after standard minimization: standard uses restraint `k=10`, 50 iterations; polish uses restraint `k=5`, 125 iterations and only runs if standard minimization does not clear the residual geometry gate. The expensive relaxed protocol remains opt-in via `ATLAS_GEOMETRY_RELAXED_MINIMIZATION=1`.
Why: A roughly 15% prep-time increase is acceptable for publication prep if it clears more raw off-site clashes, but the earlier relaxed protocol was too slow as a default.
Impact: Clean receptors still skip minimization; different proteins still minimize concurrently under the worker pool; pH siblings remain serialized for filesystem safety. A 5ZBQ smoke check cleared the off-site residual clash to `ok` with `standard:ok;polish:ok`.
Next: Run a full 90-target no-docking rerun when time permits to quantify whether the remaining off-site review targets become raw-clash clean and measure the new wall-time delta.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Relaxed residual minimization default gating | Owner: Codex
Change: Made the relaxed residual geometry minimization pass default for real runs while automatically skipping it for test contexts (`TEST_MODE_ENABLE`, `PDB_ID=TEST`, `PDBS=TEST`, `SPECIFIED_PROTEINS=TEST`, or `ATLAS_GEOMETRY_TEST_PDB`). Explicit `ATLAS_GEOMETRY_RELAXED_MINIMIZATION=0/1` still overrides the default.
Why: Full docking/rescoring runtime is expected to dominate real runs, so an additional geometry cleanup chance is acceptable, but smoke/test PDB runs should remain fast.
Impact: Real protein prep now uses standard, polish, and relaxed residual minimization protocols when residual clashes remain. Test runs use only standard plus polish unless explicitly overridden.
Next: Rerun the 90-target no-docking stress with default relaxed minimization enabled to measure wall time and whether the remaining off-site review clashes become raw-clash clean.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Relaxed geometry 90-target stress rerun | Owner: Codex
Change: Ran `spd_relaxed_no_docking_95_fullinput_holo_ph_relaxed_geom_20260513` across the same 90-target HOLO+pH no-docking set with relaxed residual minimization enabled by default. The run completed 90/90 proteins and 125/125 pH/variant stage entries with zero prep failures and zero error signatures.
Why: The remaining question was whether the longer default minimization path can remove the nine off-site residual geometry review clashes without breaking prep.
Impact: Stress summary reported 81 passed and 9 `control_ligand_missing` failures. All 81 ligand-bearing targets now have raw geometry status `ok`; the previous nine `offsite_geometry_review` cases were cleared. Wall time was 43.23 minutes versus 33.75 minutes for the standard geometry-fix run and 30.34 minutes for the pre-geometry-fix run.
Next: Treat the no-control-ligand targets separately from receptor-prep geometry; they need an alternate active-site definition or exclusion from control-ligand validation.

Date: 2026-05-14 | Task: Relaxed geometry default confirmation | Owner: Codex
Change: Confirmed `protein_prep.geometry.constructive` defaults relaxed residual minimization on for real protein-prep runs. `ATLAS_GEOMETRY_RELAXED_MINIMIZATION=0` disables it, test contexts skip it automatically, and explicit `ATLAS_GEOMETRY_RELAXED_MINIMIZATION=1` enables it even in test contexts.
Why: Relaxed minimization cleared all ligand-bearing geometry clashes in the 90-target stress rerun with acceptable no-docking runtime, so it should be the default publication-prep behavior.
Impact: Real runs use standard, polish, and relaxed residual minimization passes when residual clashes remain. Smoke/test runs remain fast unless explicitly overridden.
Next: Keep no-control-ligand targets classified separately from geometry failures.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: Demo status/output reviewer behavior | Owner: Codex
Change: `atlas demo` now writes the public demo report under canonical `outputs/data/<run_id>/`, defaults report assets to inline offline-friendly mode, and `atlas status` reports a manifest-free demo report as completed report output instead of unknown 0/0 progress.
Why: Reviewer-facing `atlas demo --status-html` could show `status: unknown` and `0/0 done` even when `report.html` existed, and legacy-aware demo path selection could continue using `data/atlas_demo` when that stale root was present.
Impact: Demo report/status artifacts are generated in `outputs/data/atlas_demo/`; existing legacy roots are still readable through status fallback but are no longer selected for new demo writes. Report-only statuses now show 1/1 completed with the report path as provenance.
Next: If reviewers need richer demo progress semantics, add a small demo manifest rather than inferring status from `report.html`.

Date: 2026-05-13 | Task: First-run dry-run portability cleanup | Owner: Codex
Change: Routed `atlas first-run ... --dry-run` target planning files to `outputs/data/<run_id-or-first_run_plan>/first_run_plan/` and changed setup-report, doctor, target, and ligand text output to display repo-relative or abbreviated paths instead of raw local absolute paths where possible.
Why: Reviewer-facing dry-run/setup summaries were dirtying source-ish tracked planning directories and exposing machine-local path prefixes that were not needed for ordinary diagnostics.
Impact: Real target installs keep their existing defaults, while first-run dry-run planning artifacts are output-scoped. Text summaries are more portable; JSON diagnostics and internal path values remain exact for tooling.
Next: If additional reviewer commands expose raw local paths, reuse the same display-path behavior rather than changing stored artifact paths.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Atlas throughput benchmark harness | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas throughput bench` for bench2-based local, simulated Slurm-array, and Slurm dry-run throughput validation. Extended `tools/simulate_slurm_array.py` with `--main-args-file`, `--summary-json`, and Slurm-style `--array` concurrency syntax such as `0-31%8`.
Why: Throughput work needs repeatable orchestration/chunking/scheduling coverage without changing protein prep or requiring a local Slurm install.
Impact: Benchmark artifacts are written under `outputs/data/<RUN_ID>/throughput_bench/`; Slurm dry-runs now also print exported `ATLAS_MAIN_ARGS`, `ATLAS_ALL_DIRS`, and finalizer dependency preview lines. Real Slurm performance still requires cluster validation.
Next: Use `atlas throughput bench --profile smoke --mode slurm-sim --array 0-2%3 --cpus-per-task 4` for bench2-compatible local array validation and `atlas throughput bench --profile medium --mode slurm-dry-run --array 0-31%4 --cpus-per-task 8` for cluster submission planning within the 32-concurrent-core cap.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Retire DUD-E flat summary mirroring | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed the `DUD_EVAL_LEGACY_FLAT_LAYOUT` summary-copy/sync path so DUD-E eval writes run-scoped artifacts under `analysis/dud_eval/<RUN_ID>/` only. Amended the latest commit to drop the tracked `.envs/banana` virtualenv and ignore `.envs/`.
Why: New Atlas workflows should converge on run-scoped output layouts, and local virtual environments should not be versioned.
Impact: DUD-E eval no longer mirrors run outputs back into a flat legacy `analysis/dud_eval/` root. Existing read-only top-level runtime discovery remains in place for older fixtures and runs.
Next: Audit remaining explicit legacy-root compatibility in DUD-E discovery and report exporters before removing command-line compatibility flags.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Move BANANA runtime out of repo | Owner: Codex
Change: Moved the BANANA code checkout from `data/external/banana` to `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools/banana` and moved the isolated BANANA Python environment from `.envs/banana` to `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools/envs/banana`. Updated the BANANA inference wrapper to discover those defaults through `ATLAS_TOOLS_DIR`, `BANANA_ROOT`, and `BANANA_PYTHON`.
Why: BANANA runtime code and dependencies are large external artifacts and should not live inside the Git working tree where they can be accidentally committed.
Impact: `analysis.cli.run_banana_inference` defaults to the external tools checkout/env on this Atlas layout while preserving explicit `--banana-root` and `--python-executable` overrides.
Next: Keep future third-party runtimes under `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools/` or another ignored external tools root, not under repo-local `.envs/` or `data/external/`.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Bench2 throughput smoke validation | Owner: Codex
Change: Ran bench2 throughput smoke against baseline `bench2_fast_cpu_scorch_20260506`; completed `throughput_smoke_bench2_local_20260515_f` with 4/4 combos, 4,920/4,920 post-scored ligands, and integrity pass. Added local `--total-cpus` pass-through plus bench2 scheduler share enforcement, fixed util-bench synthetic worker command generation, and reset throughput bench default execution mode to `auto`. Slurm-sim combo attempts exposed a BOJG pH-sibling combo-prep race, so medium was kept to dry-run planning.
Why: The fastest bench2 baseline is still the target; local smoke must remain under the 32-core rule, and combo-mode Slurm simulation should not be promoted to medium while prep-scope failures are reproducible.
Impact: Smoke correctness is good, but performance did not meet acceptance (`1527.735s` candidate vs `1364.832s` baseline, `-11.94%` wall improvement). Medium plan `throughput_medium_plan_bench2_20260515_b` validates `0-31%4` with 8 CPUs/task and finalizer dependency, but no real medium run was launched.
Next: Fix combo pH-sibling prep serialization before using `distributed_combo` for Slurm simulation; then rerun smoke, require acceptance against `bench2_fast_cpu_scorch_20260506`, and only then launch the full medium benchmark on a cluster or a node with a proven cap.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Reduced combo throughput validation | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a per-PDB/variant distributed prep lock around combo owner prep to serialize pH siblings that share receptor-prep work directories. Ran `throughput_bench_small_combo_20260515_a` with `-bench-small`/`test_library_20`, Slurm simulation `0-2%3`, 4 CPUs/task, and `distributed_combo`.
Why: BOJG pH7.2 and pH7.7 previously raced in shared prep artifacts and produced `owner_completed_without_receptor_artifact`; the user requested a smaller test-library validation instead of another full-library run.
Impact: Runtime combo execution passed the targeted failure mode: 4/4 chunks completed, including BOJG pH7.2 and BOJG pH7.7, and all 4 combo prep states are `ready` with no structured errors. The harness still returned nonzero because strict throughput integrity expects post-scored consensus rows; `-bench-small` produced completed chunk outputs but no consensus files for report/integrity (`expected=220`, `post=0`).
Next: Treat the pH-sibling prep race as fixed for runtime scheduling, then either teach throughput integrity/reporting how to consume `test_library_20` reduced combo outputs or run the next acceptance check on full bench2-compatible libraries.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Publication-Grade ML Audit Infrastructure | Owner: Codex
Change: Added chemical-cluster and target-family holdout support, immutable split manifests, optional nested validation model selection, source-lineage/assay-context audit outputs, grouped calibration, fingerprint/target-family applicability-domain summaries, decision-focused metrics, and a standard baseline panel for trained ML runs.
Why: Atlas ML runs need stronger OOD validation, source-lineage leakage controls, assay-context separation, reproducible row-level split records, and baseline/decision metrics before publication claims.
Impact: `train_ml_model` now writes `split_manifest.csv/json`, `standard_baseline_panel.csv`, `model_decision_metrics.csv`, `model_grouped_calibration.csv`, and AD summaries. `run_ml_audit_suite` now emits split manifests plus assay-context and evidence-composition audits; optional split modes with missing metadata are skipped explicitly instead of silently passing.
Next: Populate `chemical_cluster` and `target_family` columns in full SPD/ADR model-ready tables so the new holdouts run as true OOD splits rather than scaffold/protein-class fallbacks or skips.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: PU, Decoy Bias, Temporal, and Source-Transfer ML Hardening | Owner: Codex
Change: Added fold-local `bagging_pu` training mode, negative-confidence sample weighting, `pu_training_manifest.json`, decoy-bias audit outputs, stricter temporal-year coverage checks, source-transfer calibration metrics, and default benchmark-independence candidates in the one-command ML training pass.
Why: Publication-grade ML claims needed concrete safeguards for four remaining gaps: unknowns must not be global pseudo-negatives, decoy benchmarks must be audited for ligand/property/scaffold bias, temporal claims require row-level year metadata, and external/source-transfer claims need explicit independence and calibration checks.
Impact: `train_ml_model --pu-mode bagging_pu` samples unlabeled rows as temporary negatives inside the training fold only; `run_ml_audit_suite` writes `decoy_bias/decoy_bias_summary.json`; temporal splits fail on low year coverage; source-transfer prediction audits now report calibration metrics. `AGENTS.md` now requires PU and decoy-bias manifests in ML handoffs.
Next: For full publication runs, use real unlabeled-background tables for `bagging_pu`, provide row-level availability years for temporal validation, and stage independent benchmark candidates with proven source-lineage separation.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: ML publication-readiness gate | Owner: Codex
Change: Added split-conformal prediction-set outputs, model/audit claim-readiness manifests, richer calibration metrics, temporal holdout in default ML audits, and updated AGENTS.md ML instructions for required readiness artifacts.
Why: The ML pipeline had strong split/calibration/baseline pieces, but publication claims still required a compact gate that turns missing validation, calibration, uncertainty, and OOD metadata into explicit blockers or caveats.
Impact: `train_ml_model` now writes `conformal_summary.json`, `conformal_predictions.csv` when calibration rows exist, `model_claim_readiness.json`, and `model_claim_readiness.csv`; `run_ml_audit_suite` writes `ml_audit_claim_readiness.json/csv`; the one-command training pass uses nested model selection by default and audits temporal holdout when year metadata is available.
Next: Add a true fold-local PU estimator and decoy-bias audit module before using weak/reliable negatives as more than sensitivity analyses.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Seamless ML Feature Metadata Refresh | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis.cli.refresh_ml_feature_metadata` and wired `analysis.cli.run_ml_training_pass` to refresh `chemical_cluster`, `target_family`, source lineage, and optional dropped columns into `_feature_metadata/` before training by default.
Why: New OOD split and audit features should not require manual CSV edits whenever model-ready tables are rebuilt or new feature metadata is added.
Impact: A single `run_ml_training_pass --out-dir ...` now trains on refreshed copies of selected expert datasets. The standalone refresh command can update all discovered run-dir model tables or a selected dataset, with flags for chemical clustering, target-family derivation, source-lineage refresh, in-place updates, and feature exclusion.
Next: For the full SPD run, prefer explicit target-family metadata from curated target annotations when available; the current fallback uses conservative gene-symbol family heuristics when no curated family column exists.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: AGENTS ML Workflow Update | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated the AGENTS.md ML section with the one-command `run_ml_training_pass` workflow, standalone `refresh_ml_feature_metadata`, refresh flags, expanded OOD audit splits, and explicit leakage/performance audit expectations.
Why: Future agents should refresh chemical/target/source metadata and run the modular audit before interpreting ML metrics without rediscovering the workflow.
Impact: AGENTS.md now documents the default metadata refresh and audit path, including chemical-cluster and target-family holdouts. This was an explicit user-approved override of the usual AGENTS edit restriction.
Next: Keep this section current when new model-ready metadata columns, split modes, or required performance panels are added.

Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Relaxed Protein Prep Stress Resume | Owner: Codex
Change: Added stress-summary clash/failure print options and changed constructive geometry minimization to prefer OpenMM CPU with bounded threads instead of the very slow Reference platform for large receptors.
Why: The 8-core relaxed protein-prep stress run confirmed relaxed minimization was active but became CPU-bound on a large 7KS9 in-process minimization after the old code forced OpenMM Reference.
Impact: Relaxed minimization remains enabled, but future runs should avoid indefinite large-receptor stalls and produce easier-to-review clash/failure target lists.
Next: Resume `protein_prep_stress_20260514_366_relaxed8` with `CPU=8`, `ATLAS_GEOMETRY_RELAXED_MINIMIZATION=1`, and `ATLAS_GEOMETRY_OPENMM_THREADS=2`; then summarize remaining clashes with `tools/protein_prep_stress_summary.py --print-clashes --print-failures`.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Duplicate Metal Site Gate | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `protein_prep.metal.duplicate_sites` and wired a default-on duplicate/overlapping metal-site policy into the cleaned receptor pipeline before binding-site geometry repair. Obvious duplicate metal records are resolved with a JSON audit; ambiguous overlapping metal identities now raise a prep review failure. The stress summary reports duplicate-metal review status and uses `duplicate_metal_site_review` as a root cause.
Why: Physically impossible metal-metal overlaps such as Mg/Ca at nearly identical coordinates should not be relaxed by minimization or silently screened. Only defensible occupancy/identity-based resolutions should proceed automatically.
Impact: Risky mixed-metal PDBs now stop before receptor export/screening unless the duplicate site can be resolved conservatively. Runs gain a new sidecar `<PDB>_duplicate_metal_sites.json` in the protein-prep work directory.
Next: Rerun the failed `5KFN` stress target to confirm the Mg/Ca overlap is either resolved from occupancy/identity evidence or flagged as `duplicate_metal_site_review`.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: P2Rank Site-Only Stress Classification | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated `tools/protein_prep_stress_summary.py` so targets without extracted control ligands can pass the prep stress gate when P2Rank produced at least one predicted pocket. The target CSV now includes `active_site_identification_route`, P2Rank output flags, pocket counts, top score/probability, and top center.
Why: No-control-ligand targets are still usable for general docking trend analyses when the active site is explicitly prediction-derived rather than crystal-ligand-derived. They should not be mixed with receptor-prep failures.
Impact: Re-summarizing `protein_prep_stress_20260514_366_relaxed8` changes the strict summary from 326/366 to 351/366 passed. P2Rank-site targets remain distinguished from control-ligand targets, and empty P2Rank outputs are still hard failures.
Next: Use P2Rank-site targets only for scoped/general trend claims unless validated by known binders, redocking in homologous structures, literature site evidence, or enrichment controls.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Throughput bench test-library SCORCH accounting | Owner: Codex
Change: Fixed SCORCH consensus discovery for prefixed test-library consensus files and added environment-only `-bench-small` controls (`ATLAS_BENCH_SMALL_PDBS`, `ATLAS_BENCH_SMALL_TARGET_LIGANDS`) for smaller validation runs.
Why: `test_library_20_consensus_docking_scores.csv` was not matched by the prior `consensus*.csv` lookup, so SCORCH skipped all reduced-library combos as missing consensus and strict throughput integrity reported `post=0`.
Impact: Existing default bench-small behavior is unchanged; reduced validation runs can now limit PDBs/ligand caps without changing protein prep or chemistry policy. Regenerating the report/master export after SCORCH is required before strict throughput integrity sees newly written post-scored rows.
Next: For sub-5-minute end-to-end smoke, use an already cached/prepped target or add a true one-ligand fixture profile; Vina/prep overhead still exceeded five minutes for a BOJG-only six-ligand combo simulation.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Throughput benchmark micro/HPC readiness | Owner: Codex
Change: Added a micro throughput profile, benchmark-only Vina seed export/stage seeding, Slurm readiness artifact, and HPC throughput benchmarking docs.
Why: Reduced real runs were still too slow for development, and benchmark comparisons needed deterministic Vina seeds without changing production docking defaults.
Impact: `atlas throughput bench --profile micro` drives a bOJG/test_library_20 tiny profile; throughput runs export `ATLAS_THROUGHPUT_BENCH_SEED` and write `slurm_readiness.json`. Production profiles remain unseeded by default; real Slurm performance still needs cluster `sacct` validation.
Next: Add a cached-prep-required/fail-fast micro primitive before claiming sub-5-minute end-to-end behavior, then run smoke/medium on real Slurm and compare against `bench2_fast_cpu_scorch_20260506`.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Throughput benchmark Slurm launch readiness | Owner: Codex
Change: Added cached-prep-required micro behavior with bench2 prep-cache staging, tightened throughput-only seed gating, added submit-level concurrent CPU validation, and expanded Slurm/readiness accounting artifacts.
Why: The goal shifted from rigorous cluster performance claims to getting a safer production-style Slurm launch plan ready on a non-Slurm machine by EoD.
Impact: `atlas throughput bench --profile micro` now fails fast if cached HOLO receptor prep is unavailable unless `--allow-fresh-prep` is used; throughput launches export `ATLAS_THROUGHPUT_BENCH=1` before Vina seeds are consumed, while normal Slurm submits do not forward seed env. `atlas slurm submit` rejects planned concurrent CPU budgets over 32.
Next: On the Slurm machine, rerun the medium dry-run preview, launch with explicit `--main-args`, monitor with `atlas slurm progress`, let the finalizer complete, then verify `sacct_available=true`, throughput integrity, and artifact sizing.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Slurm submit core-cap correction | Owner: Codex
Change: Removed production `atlas slurm submit` CPU cap enforcement and corrected docs that implied a hard 32-core Slurm-run limit.
Why: The 32-core limit is a local testing constraint for this agent/session, not a production Slurm policy; real cluster runs should use the allocation and queue policy selected by the user.
Impact: Slurm dry-run/submit now accepts larger `--cpus-per-task` and array concurrency. Throughput artifacts still report planned concurrent CPU budget for visibility, and local validation remains intentionally capped by the operator.
Next: Use capped local micro/smoke checks here, then move to the Slurm machine for real queue/controller/accounting validation.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: External tool installer coverage | Owner: Codex
Change: Added AmberTools and BANANA installer scripts under `tools/installers/`, wired them into `install_atlas_publication_stack.sh`, moved PyMOL into `environment.core.yml`, and aligned optional installer defaults with the shared Atlas tools root.
Why: AmberTools MMGBSA utilities and BANANA inference were runtime dependencies without reproducible installer-bundle entries, which made fresh one-command setup incomplete for those open-source workflows.
Impact: Users can now install/register AmberTools and BANANA through the existing publication-stack installer, with external artifacts landing under `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools` by default. Existing proprietary/BYOL Phenix usage remains optional and externally configured.
Next: Run the new installer flags on a clean machine or disposable prefix to validate dependency solving and BANANA import compatibility.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: ML PU, Temporal, Source-Transfer, and Decoy Hardening | Owner: Codex
Change: Added Elkan-Noto PU correction with strata-specific c estimates, SAR selection diagnostics, conservative availability-year enrichment, and automatic decoy-bias auditing for decoy benchmark panels.
Why: Publication ML claims needed explicit separation of measured inactives from unlabeled rows, diagnostic checks for SCAR/SAR violations, real decoy-bias outputs, and temporal metadata that does not pretend coarse SPD release year is row-level assay timing.
Impact: `--pu-mode elkan_noto` now writes `pu_elkan_noto_manifest.json`, `pu_elkan_noto_c_by_stratum.csv`, and `pu_sar_selection_diagnostics.json`; `run_decoy_benchmark` writes `decoy_bias_audit/`; source benchmark model-ready tables preserve provenance/year fields. Elkan-Noto is sensitivity-grade when SAR diagnostics warn.
Next: For full SPD, run Atlas scoring on the 95 selected relaxed PDBs, keep SPD exposure as a within-source exposure benchmark, and use true row-level ChEMBL/BindingDB/PubChem years for temporal claims rather than SPD release year.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: One-Command Source/PU ML Suite | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis.cli.run_ml_source_pu_suite` plus PU modes for stratified bagging, propensity-weighted PU, and PULSNAR-style positive clustering.
Why: Source-specific training, hard source-transfer stress tests, matched transfer, and PU sensitivity models were possible but scattered across individual commands.
Impact: `python -m analysis.cli.run_ml_source_pu_suite --out-dir data/<run_id>/ml_source_pu_suite` now runs separate curated/ToxCast/SPD experts, curated->ToxCast hard transfer, matched transfer, and PU sensitivity modes. The suite writes `ml_source_pu_suite_manifest.json` with metrics and claim-readiness pointers.
Next: On full SPD/FDA runs, preserve unknown rows and source/year metadata so stratified/propensity/PULSNAR PU modes have enough unlabeled coverage and real temporal/source-transfer audits.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: ML Score Source Defaults | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated full-library ML defaults to avoid using `atlas_score`, `consensus_score`, and partial SCORCH fields together as the primary feature set. Binding/PU defaults now use `consensus_banana_binding`; mechanism defaults use `mechanism_consensus_banana_graph`; SPD exposure nonleaky removes `atlas_score` and keeps consensus/BANANA/metadata features where present.
Why: Pilot ablations showed `atlas_score` and `consensus_score` are mostly redundant, SCORCH fields are conditional on the top rescored tranche, and `consensus_score + BANANA` is the cleanest full-table default while SCORCH remains a subset-only ablation.
Impact: Existing feature sets remain available for backwards-compatible ablations, but one-command training now reports the cleaner full-library defaults. SCORCH-containing models require explicit partial-rescoring allowance or filtered SCORCH-row datasets.
Next: After the full SPD run, rerun `data/pilotstudy/score_source_ablation_panel` logic on the expanded model-ready table and add MM/GBSA as a standalone ablation when available.
Date: 2026-05-15 | Task: Mechanism Default Revert | Owner: Codex
Change: Reverted the default mechanism expert in `run_ml_training_pass` back to `banana_atlas_binding`; retained `mechanism_consensus_banana_graph` only as an explicit optional/source-separated ablation.
Why: The score-source cleanup was intended for full-library binding/exposure defaults. Making graph evidence the default mechanism model risks label-source circularity and was broader than requested.
Impact: One-command training again uses the previous mechanism feature family by default. Graph-heavy mechanism scores remain available for evidence ranking and sensitivity analyses, not as the primary mechanism ML default.
Next: Add source-held graph ablations before making any supervised mechanism-graph performance claim.
Date: 2026-05-16 | Task: One-Command Clustergrammer Asset Install | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `tools/installers/install_clustergrammer_assets.sh`, wired the publication installer default/`--core`/`--public-smoke` paths to install Clustergrammer browser assets, moved Python `clustergrammer` and `nodejs` into `environment.core.yml`, and documented the asset installer. Installed the current local asset bundle under `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools/report_assets/clustergrammer` with a repo-local `report_assets` pointer.
Why: The public report path can request inline Clustergrammer assets, so fresh installs without the browser bundle or Python package could fail or downgrade unexpectedly during report generation.
Impact: `bash tools/installers/install_atlas_publication_stack.sh` now provisions the Python package, npm-backed browser bundle, pointer file, and public smoke path in one command. Host-specific `report_assets` pointers are ignored by git.
Next: Keep restricted/BYOL tools optional, but treat the publication installer as the fresh-clone full-health path for public report generation.
Date: 2026-05-16 | Task: Bench2 3-node parallelization canary | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated small distributed run auto-selection so `pdb_count <= 6` with `task_count >= pdb_count` chooses `distributed_combo_hybrid`, and added a small-list per-protein fallback that round-robins PDBs across tasks before using hash sharding. Added focused tests for the 3-task/3-PDB mode decision and empty-task avoidance.
Why: A 3-node full-node `-bench2 -fast` canary selected `distributed_per_protein` for 3 PDBs on 3 tasks, and hash sharding left one task/node permanently idle while the other two nodes performed docking.
Impact: The patched 3x48 canary assigned chunks evenly across tasks (`50/51/51`) and reached near-full utilization across all nodes during the peak docking window, but startup/prep/tail phases still limit the full-run average and the 15-minute allocation did not complete the full benchmark.
Next: Validate completion with cached prep or a longer production allocation only after using non-live tests for scheduling changes; keep future Slurm canaries to one 3-node 15-minute window unless explicitly approved.
Date: 2026-05-16 | Task: Whole-node bench2 canary submit flag | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `atlas slurm submit --bench2-canary`, `tools/slurm/run_bench2_canary.sh`, and `tools/slurm/submit_bench2_canary.sh`. The preset now uses three exclusive whole-node array tasks for 15 minutes, leaves `--cpus-per-task` as `auto`, and exports `ATLAS_USE_NODE_CPUS=1` so Atlas prefers `SLURM_CPUS_ON_NODE` over a smaller per-task value when the scheduler grants a larger node.
Why: A fixed `--cpus-per-task=48` can underutilize larger nodes on clusters with mixed 48-core and 110+ core hardware.
Impact: Dry-run previews show `SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK=auto`, `ATLAS_USE_NODE_CPUS=1`, `--exclusive`, `--time=00:15:00`, and the dedicated canary worker. Focused tests cover canary defaults, explicit overrides, and whole-node CPU detection.
Next: Before a full 90-PDB production run, launch one `--bench2-canary` job on the intended partition/account and inspect `[distributed.cpu.node]` plus monitor logs to confirm each task sees the full node CPU count and reaches a high steady/peak utilization window.
Date: 2026-05-20 | Task: Relocated adaptive chunk scheduling | Owner: Codex
Change: Extended relocated mode to export manifests/logs/data under `--all-dirs`, routed distributed planner/finalizer/report/integrity roots through relocated output roots, made the idev canary wrapper honor separate `ATLAS_ALL_DIRS`, forced dry canaries through DUD-only mode, and added adaptive low-minimum chunking with complexity-weighted ligand distribution plus auto hedging for high-worker/low-chunk runs.
Why: The prior SPR run produced relocated outputs without a consistently discoverable manifest/finalizer path, dry canaries could accidentally include `fda_library`, and static chunk sizing left too few claimable chunks for whole-node Slurm allocations once each Vina job intentionally used one CPU.
Impact: Scratch relocated runs should keep run manifests, logs, data, docked, post-docked, and analysis outputs under the scratch output bundle, while 3-node full-node benchmark/canary runs generate finer chunks and complexity-balanced queues to reduce late idle tails.
Next: Re-run a small relocated idev dry canary after the DUD-only wrapper fix, then spend one 3-node 30-minute bench2 fast allocation only if the canary shows steady high docking utilization and strict finalization finds the relocated manifest.

Date: 2026-05-25 | Task: descriptor-estimated-pk-exposure | Owner: Codex
Change: Added RDKit physchem descriptor enrichment for ML tables and a descriptor-estimated SPD exposure model that predicts free_cmax_um at drug level from descriptors, predicts AC50 at pair level from Atlas/chemistry/target features, then combines predicted AC50 and predicted free Cmax for exposure relevance.
Why: Directly using observed free_cmax_um to classify SPD exposure relevance is shortcut learning because the label is defined from AC50/free_cmax. Descriptor-derived estimated exposure is less memorizing and can be used before clinical PK is known.
Impact: Metadata refresh now fills rdkit_mol_wt, rdkit_mol_logp, rdkit_tpsa, rdkit_hbd, rdkit_hba, rdkit_rotatable_bonds, rdkit_formal_charge, rdkit_aromatic_rings, rdkit_fraction_csp3, and rdkit_qed where SMILES can be resolved. run_ml_training_pass runs exposure_estimated_pk by default for the exposure expert.
Next: On full SPD, compare descriptor-estimated PK, observed-PK post-filter, and no-PK exposure models under drug/scaffold/target/chemical-cluster holdouts with bootstrap confidence intervals.

Date: 2026-05-25 | Task: Scorch Runtime State Fix + Bench2 Rescore Diagnostics | Owner: Agent
Change: Fixed Scorch runtime-state propagation after the rescoring split by having the scoring bridge read mutable runtime values from the runtime-state module at call time; restored rescoring_scorch compatibility exports; added manifest-backed Scorch failure summaries to atlas status and post-run checks; reran postSimplify Bench2 Scorch rescoring for BNJS/BOJG/BNNQ Holo pH combos.
Why: The split left bridge-level SCORCH_SCRIPT/SCORCH_ROOT copies stale after preflight, causing Scorch subprocess command construction to resolve the script as None and leaving no post-docked Scorch/reranked CSVs for DUD-eval.
Impact: Scorch command construction now uses current preflight state; atlas status surfaces postprocessing return codes and affected combos; postSimplify post_docked outputs now include consensus_reranked_scorch.csv for all four Bench2 combos and regenerated DUD-eval reports POST_DOCKED_SCORCH rows as ok.
Next: Monitor future Bench2 runs for nonzero scorch_failures in atlas status and use atlas debug <RUN_ID> --deep for the manifest-backed failure context when they occur.

Why: Full SPD docking output can now be converted into binding, exposure, tissue, and mechanism expert tables without ad hoc rewiring.

Date: 2026-06-28 | Task: Live ML tool env installs | Owner: Codex
Change: Live-installed the MLOps, molecular, RL, and generative optional ML tool tiers under `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools` and hardened `tools/installers/install_mlops_tools.sh` to route pip, conda/micromamba, XDG, Matplotlib, and installer-local home caches into the tools tree while recording freeze/spec/source-state files.

Date: 2026-06-30 | Task: Live molecular/RL/generative ML tool installs | Owner: Codex
Change: Verified live molecular, RL, and generative optional ML tool environments under `/stor/work/VDS_Beckham/atlas/tools`; installed REINVENT4 and MOSES editably into the generative env; added installer support for editable REINVENT4/MOSES installs, CPU PyTorch wheel-index configuration, and default `numpy<2 pandas<2` compatibility pins.

REINVENT metadata declares newer NumPy/pandas ranges, so publication runs should keep the recorded freeze/spec files with outputs.

Date: 2026-06-30 | Task: Imported-run ML readiness loop | Owner: Codex

Wired new `atlas ml prepare-run|ready|splits|leaderboard|external-eval` wrappers plus new train/HPO split/sample/MLflow flags.
