Date: 2026-07-01 | Task: ML external-eval preprocessing artifacts | Owner: Codex
Change: Added model preprocessing artifacts with raw feature lists, fitted design columns, training medians, missing-indicator policy, and a saved training design matrix; external eval now blocks missing raw features/preprocessing mismatches and writes explicit calibration, conformal, and applicability-domain status outputs.
Why: External evaluation could silently drop unavailable raw features and zero-fill fitted model columns, making external metrics look valid when preprocessing could not be safely replayed.
Impact: New training outputs include `model_preprocessing.json` and `training_design_matrix.csv`; external eval requires these artifacts for safe replay and records blocker/skipped/ok statuses in `external_eval_manifest.json`, `external_calibration_summary.json`, `conformal_summary.json`, and `model_applicability_domain_summary.csv`.
Next: Re-run focused ML contracts after any concurrent edits land, then use the canonical `atlas dev verify --full --fix --smoke` gate when repo-wide worktree debt allows.

Date: 2026-07-01 | Task: Throughput/ML integration bench2 verification | Owner: Codex
Change: Fast-forwarded local `main` to upstream throughput/resume/SCORCH commit `2b77455c8`, reapplied the ML workflow changes, verified merged CLI wiring, and ran `bench2_upstream_throughput_20260701_a_sim` through the supported Slurm simulation path after real `sbatch` was unavailable. Finalized the completed run through `atlas slurm finalize`; DUD eval, report generation, master export, and strict throughput integrity completed.
Why: User requested merging the latest protein-prep-to-rescoring throughput changes with the local ML-side additions and validating bench2 accuracy after the merge. Real Slurm submission could not launch on this shell because `sbatch` was missing, so the local Slurm simulator was used for execution evidence.
Impact: Candidate run completed with 152/152 chunks, 0 failed chunks, 14/14 SCORCH shards, 493 rescored shard rows, and throughput integrity `expected=4920 post=4920 integrity_pass=true`. Direct DUD post-SCORCH metrics did not show a material accuracy regression versus `bench2_fast_cpu_scorch_20260506`; one ROC delta was -0.008 while all PR AUC deltas improved. Wall-time acceptance failed because local simulation was not like-for-like with the faster baseline. The original simulator wrapper stayed alive after finalization due no-op tail worker drain, so treat simulator wall time as non-production evidence.
Next: Run the same `atlas slurm submit --run-id <RUN_ID> --array 0-31%4 --cpus-per-task 8 --main-args "-bench2 --run-id {run_id}"` path on a real Slurm login node with `sbatch` available, then rerun `atlas analysis throughput acceptance`, `atlas analysis dud-eval`, and `tools/benchmark_bench2_compare.py compare` against `bench2_fast_cpu_scorch_20260506`.

Date: 2026-07-02 | Task: ML expert feature architecture cleanup | Owner: Codex
Change: Updated Atlas ML expert feature sets so clean binding uses raw consensus/BANANA-normalized/structure/class/RDKit inputs, frozen binding priors are separate baseline outputs, direct SPD exposure uses the frozen binding prior plus non-PK features, tissue scoring separates expression inputs from safety evidence, and clean mechanism excludes graph/pathway/target-safety evidence. Estimated-PK exposure is now explicit opt-in in `run_ml_training_pass`; evidence/sensitivity models require `--allow-label-definition-features`.
Why: Circularity audit found aggregate binding priors inside the clean binding expert and Open Targets/site safety fields inside clean mechanism inputs.
Impact: Existing feature-set names remain available, but their default contents are cleaner. Runs that intentionally train evidence-augmented mechanism models must pass `--allow-label-definition-features`; estimated-PK exposure no longer runs unless requested.
Next: Rerun `atlas ml train` or `python -m analysis.cli.run_ml_training_pass` on the refreshed SPD run and compare clean vs sensitivity outputs.

Date: 2026-07-03 | Task: SPD90 one-command ML training enrichment | Owner: Codex
Change: Added automatic SPD run-master label enrichment in analysis/ml/spd_label_enrichment.py and wired it into build_spd_four_expert_tables/run_ml_training_pass with optional override paths for SPD panel, PDB-target map, FDA ligand map, and target metadata. Fixed SPD missing-threshold handling so missing AC50/margin stays unknown and censored SPD exposure rows are not converted to positives from placeholder margins.
Why: Raw Atlas run masters contain PDB IDs and ligand_base values but not SPD-compatible drug/target labels or AC50/free-Cmax labels. Future ML training should work from one command instead of requiring manual pre-joins.
Impact: run_ml_training_pass can now prepare SPD binding/exposure expert tables directly from a finalized run master when local SPD/FDA mapping files are present. DUD/decoy rows remain benchmark-only/unknown, not production negatives.
Next: Join independent tissue/mechanism labels for full four-expert training on SPD90; current SPD90 supervised full pass trained binding and exposure only.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: SPD90 mechanism/tissue auto-context projection | Owner: Codex
Change: Added automatic mechanism-label projection and tissue-expression context scoring to SPD expert table preparation. `run_ml_training_pass` now supports optional `--mechanism-label-table` and `--target-expression-table`, but defaults auto-discover local mechanism labels and expression tables. Mechanism labels are projected by stable ligand/target keys, preserving source provenance and leaving non-overlapping rows unknown. Tissue scoring emits explicit no-expression-source manifests when HPA/GTEx/Bgee/OpenTargets expression tables are not locally staged.
Why: SPD90 raw run masters had docking/SPD assay labels but did not contain the pilot/global mechanism labels or tissue expression fields, so mechanism/tissue experts were skipped despite source artifacts existing elsewhere.
Impact: One-command SPD90 preparation now creates model-ready mechanism rows when overlapping mechanism labels exist. Current SPD90 projection produced 441 mechanism model-ready rows; tissue remains untrained until real expression/source labels are staged.
Next: Stage local HPA/GTEx/Bgee/OpenTargets expression tables under data/external/* or pass `--target-expression-table`, then rerun `python -m analysis.cli.run_ml_training_pass --run-id <RUN_ID> --out-dir <OUT_DIR>` for four-expert training.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: HPA/GTEx/Bgee/OpenTargets tissue expression staging | Owner: Codex
Change: Added reusable tissue-expression source staging via analysis.external.hpa_gtex and analysis.cli.stage_tissue_expression_sources. Downloaded/staged HPA consensus RNA, HPA-GTEx RNA, HPA proteinatlas metadata, Bgee human expression calls, and Open Targets baseline-expression parquet shards under data/external/hpa_gtex/. Rebuilt SPD90 context tables with data/external/hpa_gtex/tissue_expression.tsv.
Why: SPD90 training previously had tissue-scoring architecture but no local expression source table, so all tissue/site scores were missing and the tissue expert skipped.
Impact: Deterministic tissue context is now populated for SPD90 rows and can feed mechanism/tissue-context analyses without treating missing expression as negative. Supervised tissue training still requires an independent tissue_site_label; expression-derived scores are not labels.
Next: Define independent ADR-site/tissue labels or panel-specific tissue validation labels if a supervised tissue expert is needed; keep expression features as deterministic context until then.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: ADR-site evidence layer and mechanism PU/source-held recovery | Owner: Codex
Change: Added ADR-site mapping, target-ADR term projection into tissue scoring, ADR-specific site_relevance_score_by_adr, mechanism PU table generation, source-held PU evaluation, and top-K mechanism recovery reporting. Wired optional --run-mechanism-pu-evaluation into run_ml_training_pass and added standalone analysis.cli.run_mechanism_pu_evaluation. Optimized PU stratum keys and split hashing for large nullable ML tables.
Why: Mechanism/ADR evaluation needed reusable evidence-layer site context, separated positive/reliable-negative/unknown PU states, source-held validation, and decision-focused top-K recovery without changing the current expert model labels/features.
Impact: SPD90 rebuilt context table has target-ADR context on 251,882 rows and adr_site_group on 194,830 rows. Mechanism PU collapse produces 124 strict positives, 317 reliable negatives, and 287,598 excluded/conflicting rows; unknowns remain separate when present. Source-held PU is diagnostic/sensitivity-grade and still limited by source/prevalence shift.
Next: Use these outputs for evidence reporting; do not claim supervised tissue ML until independent tissue_site_label exists. Improve mechanism label scale and true target-ADR controls before publication-grade supervised mechanism claims.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: Mechanism PU cleanup and F1 metrics | Owner: Codex
Change: Fixed mechanism PU collapse so unlabeled nullable labels remain unknown instead of becoming excluded/conflicting; added F1@0.5 threshold metrics to shared classifier metrics; added all_rows vs labelable_only top-K mechanism recovery scopes with known_f1_at_k.
Why: SPD90 mechanism PU table was incorrectly reporting hundreds of thousands of excluded/conflicting rows because nullable pandas masks treated NA labels as masked rows. F1 was requested for thresholded classifier interpretation, while top-K recovery needed to distinguish unknown operational candidates from known-label recovery.
Impact: Mechanism PU outputs now expose large unknown/background pools for fold-local PU and report F1 metrics in model outputs. Existing labels are unchanged; only collapse/evaluation behavior changed. Top-K CSVs gain ranking_scope and known_f1_at_k columns.
Next: Use cleaned PU outputs for mechanism sensitivity evaluation, keep AUPRC/enrichment/top-K as primary ranking evidence, and improve strict target-ADR controls before making supervised mechanism claims.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: ADR panel audit, model-family comparison, and optional sparse-graph model section | Owner: Codex
Change: Added ADR/site panel readiness auditing with site-group vs keyword-only evidence counts; added mechanism PU panel filtering and multi-model comparison through run_mechanism_pu_evaluation --panel/--models; added SVM and KNN to the shared classifier factory with safe optional sample-weight fitting; added optional PyKEEN/PyG/DGL/hypergraph/KAN backend audit and model-section documentation.
Why: Mechanism ML should focus on panels with enough site-specific labels and compare model families under identical splits instead of reporting only the default logistic model. Sparse ADR mechanism data may also benefit from KGE, hypergraph, or KAN-style experimental models, but those need explicit optional backends and leakage audits.
Impact: New outputs include mechanism_adr_panel_readiness.csv, mechanism_adr_panel_source_balance.csv, mechanism_model_family_comparison.csv, panel-filtered top-K recovery files, and graph_model_backends.csv. Optional graph/KGE/KAN backends are documented but not installed or used by default.
Next: Treat cardiac/QT as the cleanest current high-confidence mechanism panel; review keyword-only GI/CNS/liver/endocrine assignments before using them for claims; install graph/KGE dependencies only when ready to build relation-aware edge/hyperedge split manifests.


Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: Graph/KGE backend install and mechanism edge manifests | Owner: Codex
Change: Installed CPU graph/KGE/KAN candidate backends in docking-env (torch 2.2.1+cpu, PyKEEN, PyG, DGL, pykan), upgraded graph backend audit to import-check packages, added mechanism graph split manifest generation with reified KGE triples, hyperedges, split manifests, and negative-edge sampling policy files, wired optional --write-graph-splits into mechanism PU evaluation, and added tools/installers/install_graph_ml_backends.sh for reproducible backend setup.
Why: KGE, hypergraph, and KAN mechanism models need runnable optional backends plus explicit edge-split and negative sampling rules before training can be scientifically defensible.
Impact: PyKEEN, PyG, DGL, PyG/DGL hypergraph candidates, and KAN now audit as available. Source-balanced mechanism labels produced 812 graph hyperedges, 196 positives, 616 explicit negatives, 2436 reified triples, and random/drug/target/source holdout edge manifests. Cardiac/QT-specific graph output remains empty unless the input table carries ADR/site panel fields; the graph code supports the panel, but source rows must include the needed context.
Next: Build cardiac/QT mechanism PU tables with drug-target-ADR/site fields, then train PyKEEN/PyG/DGL models only against the emitted edge manifests and keep corruption negatives benchmark/sensitivity-only unless generated fold-locally.


Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: Expanded tabular ML model families and cardiac/QT comparison | Owner: Codex
Change: Added elastic_net_logistic, explainable_boosting_machine/ebm, catboost, and lightgbm/shallow_lightgbm model types to the shared classifier factory and Optuna sweep choices. Installed InterpretML core/full package path, CatBoost, and verified LightGBM in docking-env while restoring numpy==1.26.4. Added tools/installers/install_tabular_ml_backends.sh for reproducible setup and ran cardiac/QT model-family comparisons under source-held and drug-holdout splits.
Why: Mechanism/ADR panels need model-family comparison beyond logistic/RF/XGBoost, especially for sparse labels where additive boosting or shallow tree boosting may be more appropriate.
Impact: All requested tabular model families train through existing leakage-checked preprocessing. Cardiac/QT drug-holdout comparison trained 9 model families on 260 train / 60 test labelable rows; AUROC was ~0.947 for most models, with SVM slightly highest at 0.948 and elastic-net lower at 0.915. The clean feature set in this table currently resolves to only site_relevance_score and protein_class, so model-family differences are feature-limited and should not be overinterpreted. Source-held transfer remains weak/imbalanced because the trainable heldout source uses only 3 positives in training and 109 positives in test.
Next: Project full nonleaky binding/exposure/RDKit/target-family features into the cardiac/QT panel table, then rerun all-model comparison with locked drug/target/source split manifests before choosing a claim-grade mechanism model.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: ML run ledger and flat mechanism baseline | Owner: Codex
Change: Added automatic model_run_record.json/csv outputs for train_ml_model, standalone write_model_run_ledger and config-driven run_model_update CLIs, optional sigmoid/isotonic probability-calibration recording, and a flat_mechanism_baseline feature set using raw structural, chemistry, target, and tissue inputs. Source-held mechanism evaluation now requires configurable minimum train positives/negatives as well as held-out positives/negatives.
Why: Future model updates need one comparable ledger row per run, and the modular expert architecture needs a flat direct mechanism baseline control. The prior source-held run trained on only three positives because the old gate checked only for two classes.
Impact: Every new model training run writes run/date/git/dataset/label/features/split/PU/model/hyperparameters/calibration/rows/positives/AUROC/AUPRC/top-K/Brier metadata. Source-held runs with too little held-in signal are skipped instead of reported as model-selection evidence.
Next: Project the full nonleaky binding/exposure/RDKit/target-family features into cardiac/QT panel tables before interpreting model-family differences as architecture effects.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: Strict ML feature-set comparison guard | Owner: Codex
Change: Added strict_feature_set support to train_ml_model plus --strict-feature-set in train_ml_model/run_ml_training_pass and default-strict behavior in run_model_update. Dataset manifests now record requested_features, missing_features, and feature_coverage_fraction.
Why: The cardiac/QT flat-vs-modular check silently used a partial feature subset because train_ml_model dropped unavailable columns. That made the comparison useful only as a wiring check, not a real model comparison.
Impact: Model-family and modular-vs-flat comparisons can now fail fast when the expected full feature set is absent. Exploratory partial-feature runs remain possible only when explicitly allowed.
Next: Build a full cardiac/QT model-ready table with RDKit, target_family, ligand_chemotype, structure_quality, BANANA, and staged out-of-fold expert outputs before interpreting flat-vs-modular performance.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: BANANA feature population for ML tables | Owner: Codex
Change: Added local feature backfill for ML tables, direct `analysis.cli.build_full_feature_table`, optimized BANANA pocket receptor search, wired `run_ml_training_pass --banana-scoring auto --banana-score-scope labelable`, and generated SPD90 cardiac/QT BANANA labelable scores.
Why: Strict flat/modular mechanism comparisons require BANANA, RDKit, chemotype, structure-quality, target-family, tissue, and mechanism fields in one unified table instead of silently training on partial features.
Impact: Training can now auto-score BANANA-dependent labelable rows and join score artifacts reproducibly. Full FDA-matrix BANANA scoring is intentionally explicit because CPU inference over ~267k ready rows projects to many hours. Missing BANANA remains unknown.
Next: For claim-grade comparisons, run the one-command training pass on the final SPD90/SPD-expanded table with strict feature sets and decide whether full-matrix BANANA scoring should be scheduled on GPU/HPC.

Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: ML training CPU threading flag | Owner: Codex
Change: Added --model-n-jobs to run_ml_training_pass and train_ml_model, and threaded it through supported sklearn/XGBoost/LightGBM/CatBoost/EBM model constructors without disabling nested model selection.
Why: Full SPD90 ML passes with BANANA, audits, ablations, and model-family sweeps need a controlled way to use more CPU while preserving reproducible one-command training.
Impact: Existing commands keep previous defaults unless --model-n-jobs is supplied. Threaded models can use more CPU; SVM and some fallback gradient boosting paths remain effectively serial.
Next: For claim-grade SPD90 runs, use --banana-score-scope labelable and --model-n-jobs 8-16, then compare wall time and metrics before using --banana-score-scope all.


Date: 2026-07-05 | Task: Cached ML model-family sweep | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `python -m analysis.cli.run_ml_model_family_sweep` to train binding/exposure/mechanism expert tables across model families and aggregate each `model_run_record.json` into `model_family_sweep_summary.csv` plus JSONL records.
Why: SPD90 BANANA scoring is expensive, so model-family comparisons should reuse cached `*.with_banana.csv` tables while preserving per-model ledgers with config, metrics, split, calibration, and provenance fields.
Impact: Future agents can run one sweep command over cached expert tables instead of manually launching repeated `train_ml_model` calls. SVM/other slow models are isolated per run, failures are recorded, and successful models remain usable.
Next: Use the sweep summary for model selection only under matching label, feature-set, and split settings; rerun with stronger OOD split manifests before making claim-grade model-family comparisons.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: Cardiac/QT mechanism panel quarantine | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis.ml.mechanism_panel_training` and `python -m analysis.cli.build_mechanism_panel_training_table` to build true ADR-panel-specific mechanism tables with dedicated labels such as `cardiac_qt_mechanism_label`. Added the `cardiac_qt_mechanism_clean` feature set, which preserves source/ADR/evidence columns in the table but excludes target family, protein class, site relevance, graph evidence, target-safety evidence, and source/ADR fields from clean training.
Why: The global mechanism model showed near-perfect drug-holdout metrics caused by target/source confounding. Cardiac/QT needs panel-specific labels, explicit quarantined features, source/target-held evaluation, and top-K recovery.
Impact: Cardiac/QT panel outputs under `data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/ml_training_pass_all_models_20260705/cardiac_qt_panel/` are now suitable for exploratory clean-feature evaluation. Target/source-held metrics remain caveated due sparse held-out positives.
Next: Add more cardiac/QT positives and measured negatives, then rerun with target/source/target-family heldout plus model-family comparison before making publication-grade claims.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: Phase 1 Atlas ML scope and label-balance audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added reusable label-balance audit modules (`analysis.ml.label_balance_audit`, `python -m analysis.cli.audit_label_balance`) and generated Phase 1 SPD90 outputs under `data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/ml_training_pass_phase1_v1_20260706/`. Trained focused target-holdout model-family comparisons for clean binding, direct SPD exposure, and flat mechanism diagnostic; evaluated frozen binding prior score columns separately and summarized tissue scorer coverage.
Why: The v1 publication path should prioritize binding/activity and direct SPD exposure as core ML claims, keep tissue as deterministic context, and treat mechanism as diagnostic/evidence-layer until labels are less source/family-confounded.
Impact: Future agents can run `python -m analysis.cli.audit_label_balance --dataset <csv> --label <label> --out-dir <dir>` after any label/table change to quickly identify all-positive/all-negative groups, fold-count failures, source prevalence shifts, and acquisition targets. Phase 1 metrics are exploratory target-holdout results, not final claim-grade split sweeps.
Next: Rerun Phase 1 under scaffold/chemical-cluster/source/target-family splits after final SPD-expanded data settle; keep cardiac/QT mechanism as top-K/evidence-layer until targeted cardiac safety rows improve family/source balance.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: External ML source registration | Owner: Codex
Change: Registered ChEMBL, BindingDB, ToxCast/Tox21, PubChem BioAssay, IUPHAR/BPS GtoPdb, and NCATS Inxight in the external-source catalog/spec/registry; added `--iuphar-gtopdb` and `--tox21` four-state inputs plus `--ncats-inxight-pk` PK staging.
Why: New assay and PK/safety sources need to enter the existing source-aware four-state/PK architecture without pooling labels or converting missing evidence into negatives.
Impact: Future ML staging can preserve source provenance for source-holdout/leakage audits; GtoPdb is positive-biased and Inxight is context-only, so neither source creates absence-based drug-target negatives.
Next: Stage local filtered exports, rebuild `source_label_spec`, rebuild four-state evidence with source holdouts, refresh feature metadata, then run the ML audit suite before interpreting metrics.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: Binding BANANA-vs-Atlas ablation comparison | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis.cli.run_binding_ablation_comparison` and binding ablation feature sets for BANANA-only, consensus-only, consensus/context without BANANA, RDKit/target metadata only, consensus+BANANA scores, and full clean binding. Wrote metrics-only outputs under `data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/ml_training_pass_phase1_v1_20260706/binding_clean_ablation_metrics_only/`.
Why: Needed a clean same-split comparison to determine whether Atlas binding models outperform BANANA due to real complementary signal or target/chemistry context shortcuts.
Impact: New CLI supports fast `--metrics-only` comparisons and full artifact mode. BigBind/BANANA training-overlap exclusion still requires staging BigBindV1 `activities_*`/`structures_*` split files locally.
Next: Add a BigBind split-membership audit once BigBindV1 files are staged; treat tissue scoring as deterministic evidence until independent `tissue_site_label` data exists.
Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: Target/chemotype shortcut-bias audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis.ml.target_chemotype_bias` and CLI `python -m analysis.cli.audit_target_chemotype_bias`; ran logistic and random-forest shortcut audits on the SPD90 BANANA-filled binding table under `data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/ml_training_pass_phase1_v1_20260706/target_chemotype_bias_audit*/`.
Why: Binding ablations showed metadata-only feature sets were strong enough that target/scaffold/chemotype shortcut bias needed a repeatable audit before interpreting model gains over BANANA.
Impact: The audit reports group prevalence, high-risk target/chemotype/scaffold groups, and shortcut-only model metrics. Current SPD90 labels show shortcut risk: 847 group flags, GPCR contributes ~70% of positives, and RF `target_family;protein_class;scaffold_key` reaches PR-AUC ~0.261 under target holdout.
Next: Treat target/chemotype/scaffold features as context-sensitive; require target-family/chemical-cluster holdouts and metadata-only baselines for claim-grade binding/exposure metrics. Stage BigBind `activities_train/val/test.csv` files before claiming BANANA training-overlap exclusion.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: Full-SPD additive external-source ingest | Owner: Codex
Change: Ran an additive full-SPD external-source ingest/rebuild under `data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/external_source_additive_20260706/` using RDK drug IDs and UniProt target IDs; staged ChEMBL, BindingDB, ToxCast/invitrodb, and IUPHAR/GtoPdb outputs and rebuilt four-state evidence plus QC reports.
Why: The pilot-source counts were not comparable to the 90-protein SPD set; the user requested an additive rebuild against the full SPD protein target set without replacing existing datasets.
Impact: Joined additive four-state labels cover 14,910 drug-target pairs across 83/90 targets with 1,723 positives and 13,187 negatives. PubChem BioAssay remains blocked because PUG-REST active/inactive CID responses hung before a complete additive TSV could be written.
Next: Resume PubChem with an assay-level queue/cache that paginates or skips huge CID result sets, then rerun the additive four-state rebuild and leakage/QC audits before training on the expanded labels.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: PubChem BioAssay additive retry | Owner: Codex
Change: Retried PubChem BioAssay PUG-REST ingestion for the full 90-protein SPD target set with bounded active/inactive CID requests, 25 AIDs per target, and checkpointed audits under `data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/external_source_additive_20260706/sources/pubchem_bioassay/`.
Why: The prior PubChem attempt hung on active/inactive CID responses before a complete additive TSV could be written.
Impact: The retry completed 4,500/4,500 CID requests with zero timeouts across 90/90 targets but found 0 exact CID overlaps with the SPD FDA/RDK drug set, so PubChem added 0 rows and 0 drug-target pairs; the existing four-state table was unchanged.
Next: If PubChem is still required, add an InChIKey/salt-normalized compound-resolution mode with assay-size guards before rebuilding labels; otherwise treat PubChem as attempted and non-contributory for this exact-CID additive pass.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: ML feature retention, chemotype, BigBind overlap, tissue labels | Owner: Codex
Change: Added retained-context-not-trained provenance to ML manifests/cards, `--keep-all-columns` for ML dataset builds, no-target-class binding ablations, RDKit structural chemotype backfill, BigBind overlap audit CLI, and independent tissue-site label projection.
Why: Risky context/evidence columns such as target_family, protein_class, graph evidence, and site scores must remain auditable in model-ready data while being excluded from clean training feature sets. Chemotype was previously all generic; BigBind/BANANA overlap needed split-membership auditing; tissue labels needed independent ADR/clinical provenance instead of expression-derived circular labels.
Impact: Training feature sets now control exclusion without deleting data columns. Refreshed SPD90 binding table has structural chemotypes and provenance columns. BigBind archive is downloaded and partially extracted with activity split files available; fast raw-SMILES overlap audit is written under the SPD90 phase1 run. Tissue label projection currently adds independent positives but no negatives, so use for evidence/PU-positive context, not balanced supervised tissue claims.
Next: Use refreshed metadata tables for subsequent training; run full canonical BigBind overlap in batch if exact InChIKey-level ligand overlap is required; add independent tissue negative/control sources before claiming supervised tissue classification.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: Phase 1 SPD90 ML comparison and additive four-state audit | Owner: Codex
Change: Refreshed the SPD90 binding/exposure comparison outputs, vectorized ML feature metadata target-family/source-lineage refresh for large additive tables, and changed the model-family exposure default to `spd_exposure_relevant`.
Why: The 408k-row additive four-state table needed scalable metadata refresh and the exposure sweep default was using an obsolete label column name.
Impact: Large additive tables now refresh without Python row-loop bottlenecks; source lineage now prioritizes evidence source columns before objective fields. The additive table is usable as binding/activity sensitivity data, not ADR-mechanism truth.
Next: Keep Phase 1 publication claims on clean SPD binding/exposure; only use additive four-state rows for sensitivity/PU background unless mechanism-specific labels are staged.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: SPD90 merged external four-state table and Phase 1 retraining | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis.ml.spd_external_four_state_merge` and `analysis.cli.build_spd_external_four_state_table`; built `spd90_model_ready_plus_external_four_state.csv` with preserved SPD labels, prefixed external four-state evidence labels, combined activity conflict rules, and tissue-site evidence projection.
Why: The previous external four-state table was additive evidence but not a model-ready replacement for the SPD90 feature table; it used different drug/target namespaces and did not carry SPD labels/features.
Impact: Phase 1 binding/exposure comparisons can now use one merged table. External four-state labels are sensitivity-grade; tissue labels remain positive-only evidence, not supervised tissue ML. Target-family/source imbalance remains the main generalization caveat.
Next: Add independent tissue/site negatives or controls before supervised tissue training; improve external four-state source/family balance before making strong target-family generalization claims.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: ML PU/tissue evaluation and source-family balancing defaults | Owner: Codex
Change: Quarantined high-memorization context columns from default clean ML feature sets while preserving explicit *_with_context aliases; added capped source/family group reweighting flags to train_ml_model, run_ml_training_pass, run_ml_model_family_sweep, and run_ml_source_pu_suite; switched one-command exposure default to spd_exposure_relevant. Focused smoke output: data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/ml_training_pass_phase1_v1_20260706/quarantined_reweight_smoke/binding_logistic_target_holdout.
Why: Current source/family imbalance and tissue positive-unlabeled labels need safer defaults. Clean models should not accidentally train on target_family, protein_class, or structure_quality; source/family balancing should be reproducible without creating ad hoc sampled tables.
Impact: Default clean binding/exposure/mechanism feature-set names now exclude structure_quality, protein_class, and target_family. Existing high-context behavior is still available through *_with_context feature sets for ablation/sensitivity. Group reweighting writes group_reweighting_manifest.json and group_reweighting_weights.csv when requested. Tissue labels with positives plus unknowns remain PU/ranking evidence, not ordinary supervised AUROC/AUPRC claims.
Next: Add a first-class tissue PU recovery command/report that scores held-out positives against unlabeled background and reports top-K recovery/enrichment, then rerun Phase 1 model-family comparisons with and without --group-reweight-cols source_family target_family.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: Tissue PU recovery and source-aware ML controls | Owner: Codex
Change: Added dedicated tissue PU recovery reporting, integrated tissue as a model-family sweep expert, wired tissue PU into the one-command ML training pass, and added source/family reweighting to source calibration.
Why: Tissue labels are positive/unlabeled, so ordinary AUROC/AUPRC would imply unknowns are confirmed negatives; source/family imbalance also needs explicit weighting and calibration controls.
Impact: Tissue comparisons now report top-K positive recovery, enrichment over background, held-out-positive recovery, and case-study CSVs; source calibrators can emit reweighting manifests. Tissue outputs remain PU/evidence-ranking claims unless independent tissue negatives are added.
Next: Run full model-family sweeps with `--experts binding exposure tissue mechanism` after any data refresh, and inspect `tissue_pu_topk_recovery.csv` plus source/group balance before interpreting tissue claims.

Date: 2026-07-06 | Task: Phase 1 ML sweep and BANANA cache policy | Owner: Codex
Change: Made `run_ml_training_pass --banana-scoring auto` cache-aware for existing BANANA-derived feature columns and added `--mechanism-label` to `run_ml_model_family_sweep` so merged four-state tables can be swept without throwaway CSVs. Ran calibrated SPD90 Phase 1 training and a core model-family sweep under drug-holdout with source/family reweighting.
Why: BANANA scores should not be recomputed on every training pass when stable cached columns already exist; mechanism comparisons need to point at `external_four_state_ml_label` in merged SPD90 tables.
Impact: Default training avoids excessive BANANA runtime unless `--banana-scoring always` is requested. Core sweep outputs are under `data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/ml_phase1_core_model_sweep_20260706_cached/`; optional all-model sweep was stopped after SVM proved too slow, leaving partial outputs in `ml_phase1_model_family_sweep_20260706_cached/`.
Next: Keep SVM/KNN/EBM/CatBoost optional, not default; use binding/exposure as Phase 1 primary claims, tissue as PU/evidence ranking, and mechanism as diagnostic until stronger independent controls are added.

Date: 2026-07-07 | Task: Phase 1 evaluation-ready ML validation package | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis.cli.run_phase1_evaluation_ready` to produce Phase 1 `evaluation_ready_*` artifacts for clean SPD binding and direct SPD exposure claims. The wrapper now runs binding ablations, binding/exposure model-family validation, calibration/reliability outputs, held-out split summaries, label-status summaries, and a top-level `evaluation_ready_metric_coverage.csv`. It caps subprocess thread environment variables to avoid oversubscribed grouped-CV runs.
Why: The Phase 1 publication path should lead with measured SPD binding/activity recovery and SPD exposure-relevance prioritization, while keeping tissue as evidence/PU context and mechanism ML as diagnostic. The user requested explicit validation-ready files and confirmation that PR-AUC, Precision@K, enrichment@K, calibration, Brier, ablations, drug/chemical/target holdouts, family diagnostic, and external-validation status are present.
Impact: Outputs are under `data/spd90_fda_fda_dud_spr10_40h_patchdist_20260525_161503/evaluation_ready_phase1_20260707/`. Binding and exposure held-out split summaries, calibration artifacts, split manifests, and metric coverage are present. Repeated grouped-CV confidence intervals remain marked missing because RF-including and logistic-only grouped-CV attempts were too slow for the interactive pass; use a scheduled/offline run for full grouped CV. External validation remains deferred to the source-transfer suite because ChEMBL/BindingDB/ToxCast labels are separate objectives, not direct SPD exposure validation.
Next: Schedule `run_grouped_cv_stability` as an offline job with explicit CPU caps, then run `python -m analysis.cli.run_ml_source_pu_suite --out-dir <run_dir>/ml_source_pu_suite` for hard source-transfer stress testing before any external-generalization claim.

Date: 2026-07-07 | Task: AtlasSPD_phase1 evaluation freeze and model-family expansion | Owner: Codex
Change: Added CatBoost and EBM to the Phase 1 evaluation-ready default model panel, added skip-existing reuse for model-family sweeps, added an explicit SPD exposure binding-prior-only baseline, and froze the current Phase 1 model-ready dataset under data/AtlasSPD_phase1/.
Why: Phase 1 comparisons need a stable dataset name, a direct exposure baseline showing how far binding prior alone gets, and model-family coverage beyond logistic/RF/LightGBM.
Impact: Future Phase 1 runs can reuse existing model records while adding missing model families. AtlasSPD_phase1/model_ready/AtlasSPD_phase1_model_ready.csv is the stable model-ready table; top-level evaluation summaries are copied under AtlasSPD_phase1/evaluation_ready/.
Next: Treat target-family holdout as diagnostic because family balance remains coarse. Run repeated grouped CV offline once the Phase 1 table is stable enough for final validation.

Date: 2026-07-07 | Task: Read-only Dataset EDA Tooling | Owner: Codex
Change: Added `analysis.ml.dataset_eda`, CLI `analysis.cli.run_dataset_eda`, and `atlas analysis dataset-eda` wrapper for read-only tabular dataset exploration. The command writes column summaries, optional ydata-profiling reports, Phi_K/phik and dython association matrices, scikit-learn mutual information rankings when a label is provided, and NetworkX/PyVis association-network artifacts under a separate output directory. Updated the tabular ML installer to include the optional EDA stack.
Why: Atlas SPD phase-1/model-ready tables need quick first-pass inspection without editing source datasets or launching model-training workflows.
Impact: Existing datasets are only read; outputs default to `outputs/data/dataset_eda/`. Missing optional packages are recorded in `dataset_eda_manifest.json` unless `--fail-on-missing` is used.
Next: Run `bash tools/installers/install_tabular_ml_backends.sh` if the optional EDA packages are not installed, then use `atlas analysis dataset-eda --dataset-name AtlasSPD_phase1 --list-matches` to select the exact table before generating reports.

Date: 2026-07-07 | Task: Phase 1 family-generalization diagnostics | Owner: Codex
Change: Added stratified target holdout by target family to grouped CV, exposed it through analysis.cli.run_grouped_cv_stability, added sgd_logistic as a fast repeated-fold diagnostic model, and generated AtlasSPD_phase1 family-generalization outputs under data/AtlasSPD_phase1/evaluation_ready/family_generalization/.
Why: The previous target_family_holdout held out only GPCR under the deterministic split, making it a harsh single-family stress test rather than a complete family-generalization panel.
Impact: Phase 1 now has leave-one-family-out metrics for each feasible target family and a stratified target-holdout-by-family diagnostic. Protease binding remains underpowered and is skipped because it has only one positive. Heavy models should remain in the standard model-family panel; family diagnostics default to fast linear checks unless run offline.
Next: Treat family-level binding generalization as weak except transporter/GPCR AUROC signal; use stratified target holdout plus target/chemical holdouts as main Phase 1 checks, and add balanced family labels before making strong target-family claims.

Date: 2026-07-13 | Task: Atlasv0.0.07 ML add-on run safety | Owner: Codex
Change: Added the canonical `atlas ml score-addons` wrapper, exact pair/library-manifest aliases, `--compare-run`, a minimum reference exhaustiveness of 2, a 32-worker hard cap, active-run refusal, and an exclusive cross-process add-on lock. Documented that ML add-ons must not use raw `main.py`, fast mode, DUD-only mode, or `--dud-library`.
Why: The historical SPD positive-add-on run was launched with `-fast -dude`, which selected the intended per-PDB libraries through a benchmark-only mechanism but generated low-exhaustiveness raw poses and risked concurrent-run overload.
Impact: The historical merged clean scores remain usable because their primary score was replaced by a common-reference pass using the SPD90 stage-1 context at exhaustiveness 2; tiny-library percentiles and fast-run SCORCH values remain quarantined. Future canonical add-on scoring fails before launch when any Atlas run manifest is active or another add-on scorer holds the lock.
Next: Resolve or finalize `postFDAfixSPDrun` before any new add-on scoring, then use `atlas ml score-addons --run-id <ID> --pairs <CSV> --compare-run <FULL_RUN>`; rerun SCORCH only from publication-grade poses if it is needed as a model feature.

Date: 2026-07-13 | Task: Atlasv0.0.07 stale-aware run activity guard | Owner: Codex
Change: Hardened the add-on launch guard to use exact run-id process markers, manifest-owned Slurm IDs, relocated manifest roots, a 72-hour fail-closed activity window, and an exclusive cross-process lock; old status-only manifests are no longer treated as live forever.
Why: Atlas contains many completed or abandoned manifests whose root lifecycle remained running, while the shared Slurm helper could otherwise inherit an unrelated caller job ID and create false active-run evidence.
Impact: New add-on scoring is blocked by current process, scheduler, or recent manifest evidence and by concurrent add-on lock ownership. The guard currently identifies postFDAfixSPDrun and postFDAfixSPDrun_prepcheck_20260713 as active records and launches no docking while they remain unresolved.
Next: Finalize or explicitly resolve those two manifests, then use atlas ml score-addons with the exact pair manifest and completed SPD90 comparison run; do not use raw main.py, fast, or DUD-only flags.

The frozen Phase 1 source also contains 34 binding labels that disagree with both committed and canonical relation-aware binding logic, confirming artifact/code drift, plus 40 exposure labels that agree with committed legacy behavior but differ from canonical interval semantics. No production table or model was regenerated, no receptor mapping changed, and existing drug-target model-ready deduplication remains in place.
Next: Review whether the 46 selected `>10` boundary cases should adopt the canonical negative interpretation. Only after that decision should production derivation integration and an Atlas patch-version entry proceed; handle the intended drug-PDB model unit and 9I52/9LLG deduplication in a separate reviewed prompt.

Date: 2026-07-26 | Task: Atlasv0.0.17 exact-decimal SPD threshold hardening | Owner: Codex
Change: Replaced binary-float threshold adjudication with source-preserving Decimal bounds and threshold-scaled free-Cmax comparisons, routed the materialized-panel compatibility fallback through the canonical helper when interval provenance is available, advanced only the parser implementation to `spd_activity_interval_v3`, and added exact decimal, unit-conversion, production-status, and full-data regression gates.

Impact: All decimal boundary fixtures pass. Reconciliation against immutable Phase B outputs preserves all 121,097 raw and 95,512 selected-pair labels and statuses with zero Prompt 2C changes, while retaining exactly 77 raw and 46 selected legacy unknown-to-negative corrections. Binding/Exposure policy versions, receptor mappings, eligibility, row units, selection, weak-margin behavior, frozen tables, and models remain unchanged.
